33: /// Number of times the linear part of the partial rounds is skipped in the
34: /// Poseidon cpu implemetation (0 is the default implementation without skips at
35: /// all).
36: pub(crate) const NB_SKIPS_CPU: usize = 2;
37: 
38: /// Off-circuit Poseidon state.
39: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
40: pub struct PoseidonState<F: PoseidonField> {
41:     pre_computed: PreComputedRoundCPU<F>,
42:     register: [F; WIDTH],
43:     queue: Vec<F>,
44:     squeeze_position: usize,
45:     input_len: Option<usize>,
46: }
47: 
48: // Applies the MDS matrix to a state and adds the round constants. All arguments
49: // have length `WIDTH`. To save the addition cost, the implementation is done by
50: // mutating the `constants` slice, and eventually copying it into `state`.
51: fn linear_layer<F: PoseidonField>(state: &mut [F], constants: &mut [F]) {
52:     #[allow(clippy::needless_range_loop)]
53:     for i in 0..WIDTH {
54:         for j in 0..WIDTH {
55:             constants[i] += F::MDS[i][j] * state[j];
56:         }
57:     }
58:     state.copy_from_slice(constants);
59: }
60: 
61: /// A cpu version of the full round of Poseidon's permutation. Operates by
62: /// mutating the `state` argument (length `WIDTH`).
63: pub(crate) fn full_round_cpu<F: PoseidonField>(round_index: usize, state: &mut [F]) {
64:     state.iter_mut().for_each(|x| *x = x.square().square() * *x);
65:     let mut new_state = if round_index == NB_FULL_ROUNDS + NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS - 1 {
66:         [F::ZERO; WIDTH]
67:     } else {
68:         F::ROUND_CONSTANTS[round_index + 1]
69:     };
70:     linear_layer(state, &mut new_state);
71: }
72: 
73: // A cpu version of Poseidon with `1 + NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT` partial rounds.
74: fn partial_round_cpu<F: PoseidonField>(
75:     pre_computed: &PreComputedRoundCPU<F>,
76:     round_batch_index: usize,
77:     state: &mut [F], // Length `WIDTH`.
78: ) {
79:     pre_computed
80:         .partial_round_id
81:         .eval::<NB_SKIPS_CPU>(&pre_computed.round_constants[round_batch_index], state);
82: }
83: 
84: /// A cpu version of Poseidon with `1 + NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT` partial rounds. Also
85: /// returns the values of the last column of the skipped rows
86: /// (`NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT` elements) as needed to fill the circuit's rows.
87: pub(crate) fn partial_round_cpu_for_circuits<F: PoseidonField>(
88:     pre_computed: &PreComputedRoundCircuit<F>,
89:     round_batch_index: usize,
90:     state: &mut [F], // Length `WIDTH`.
91: ) -> [F; NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT] {
92:     pre_computed
93:         .partial_round_id
94:         .eval::<NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT>(&pre_computed.round_constants[round_batch_index], state)
95: }
96: 
97: // Alternative partial round version, without any skips.
98: fn partial_round_cpu_raw<F: PoseidonField>(round: usize, state: &mut [F]) {
99:     state[WIDTH - 1] *= state[WIDTH - 1].square().square();
100:     let mut new_state = F::ROUND_CONSTANTS[round + 1];
101:     linear_layer(state, &mut new_state)
102: }
103: 
104: /// A cpu version of the full Poseidon's permutation with partial-round skips.
105: pub fn permutation_cpu<F: PoseidonField>(pre_computed: &PreComputedRoundCPU<F>, state: &mut [F]) {
106:     let nb_skips = pre_computed.partial_round_id.nb_skips;
107:     let nb_main_partial_rounds = NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS / (1 + nb_skips);
108:     let remainder_partial_rounds = NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS % (1 + nb_skips);
109: 
110:     for (x, k0) in state.iter_mut().zip(F::ROUND_CONSTANTS[0]) {
111:         *x += k0;
112:     }
113:     (0..NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2).for_each(|round_index| full_round_cpu(round_index, state));
114:     (0..nb_main_partial_rounds)
115:         .for_each(|round_batch_index| partial_round_cpu(pre_computed, round_batch_index, state));
116:     (NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2 + NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS - remainder_partial_rounds..)
117:         .take(remainder_partial_rounds)
118:         .for_each(|round_index| partial_round_cpu_raw(round_index, state));
119:     (NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2 + NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS..)
120:         .take(NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2)
121:         .for_each(|round_index| {
122:             full_round_cpu(round_index, state);
123:         })
124: }
125: 
126: // A cpu implementation of the sponge operations, building on the Poseidon's
127: // permutation.
128: impl<F: PoseidonField> SpongeCPU<F, F> for PoseidonChip<F> {
129:     type StateCPU = PoseidonState<F>;
130: 
131:     fn init(input_len: Option<usize>) -> Self::StateCPU {
132:         let mut register = [F::ZERO; WIDTH];
133:         register[RATE] = F::from_u128(input_len.map(|l| l as u128).unwrap_or(1 << 64));
134:         let pre_computed = PreComputedRoundCPU::init();
135:         PoseidonState {
136:             pre_computed,
137:             register,
138:             queue: Vec::new(),
139:             squeeze_position: 0,
140:             input_len,
141:         }
142:     }
143: 
144:     fn absorb(state: &mut Self::StateCPU, inputs: &[F]) {
145:         state.queue.extend(inputs);
146:         state.squeeze_position = 0;
147:     }
148: 
149:     fn squeeze(state: &mut Self::StateCPU) -> F {
150:         if state.squeeze_position > 0 {
151:             // If `input_len` was specified, we only allow 1 squeeze.
152:             if state.input_len.is_some() {
153:                 panic!("Attempting to squeeze multiple times a fixed-size Poseidon sponge (CPU).")
154:             };
155:             debug_assert!(state.queue.is_empty());
156:             let output = state.register[state.squeeze_position % RATE];
157:             state.squeeze_position = (state.squeeze_position + 1) % RATE;
158:             return output;
159:         }
160: 
161:         match state.input_len {
162:             None => {
163:                 let padding = F::from(state.queue.len() as u64);
164:                 state.queue.push(padding);
165:             }
166:             Some(len) => {
167:                 if state.queue.len() != len {
168:                     panic!("Inconsistent lengths in fixed-size Poseidon sponge (CPU). Expected: {}, found: {}.", len, state.queue.len())
169:                 };
170:             }
171:         }
172: 
173:         for chunk in state.queue.chunks(RATE) {
174:             for (entry, value) in state.register.iter_mut().zip(chunk.iter()) {
175:                 *entry += value;
176:             }
177:             permutation_cpu(&state.pre_computed, &mut state.register);
178:         }
179: 
180:         state.queue = Vec::new();
181:         state.squeeze_position = 1 % RATE;
182:         state.register[0]
183:     }
184: }
185: 
186: impl<F: PoseidonField> TranscriptHash for PoseidonState<F> {
187:     type Input = Vec<F>;
188:     type Output = F;
189: 
190:     fn init() -> Self {
191:         PoseidonChip::init(None)
192:     }
193: 
194:     fn absorb(&mut self, input: &Self::Input) {
195:         PoseidonChip::absorb(self, input)
196:     }
197: 
198:     fn squeeze(&mut self) -> Self::Output {
199:         PoseidonChip::squeeze(self)
200:     }
201: }

309:     use rand::SeedableRng;
310:     use rand_chacha::ChaCha12Rng;

312:     use super::*;
313:     use crate::hash::poseidon::permutation_cpu;
314: 
315:     // A version of Poseidon's permutation, without round skips. Has been tested
316:     // against the previous version of Poseidon (replaced since Merge request #521).
317:     fn permutation_cpu_raw<F: PoseidonField>(state: &mut [F]) {
318:         for (x, k0) in state.iter_mut().zip(F::ROUND_CONSTANTS[0]) {
319:             *x += k0;
320:         }
321:         for round_index in 0..NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2 {
322:             full_round_cpu(round_index, state);
323:         }
324:         for round_index in (NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2..).take(NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS) {
325:             partial_round_cpu_raw(round_index, state);
326:         }
327:         for round_index in (NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2 + NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS..).take(NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2) {
328:             full_round_cpu(round_index, state);
329:         }
330:     }
331:     // Tests the performances of the cpu version of Poseidon. In debug mode, also
332:     // tests the consistency between the version with and without round skips.
333:     fn consistency_cpu<F: PoseidonField + ff::FromUniformBytes<64>>(nb_samples: usize) {
334:         println!(
335:             ">> Testing the consistency between the two cpu implementations of the permutation ({NB_SKIPS_CPU} round skips VS no round skips)."
336:         );
337: 
338:         let pre_computed = PreComputedRoundCPU::init();
339:         let mut rng = ChaCha12Rng::seed_from_u64(0xf007ba11);
340:         (0..nb_samples)
341:             .for_each(|_| {
342:                 let input: [F; WIDTH] =
343:                     core::array::from_fn(|_| F::random(&mut rng));
344:                 let mut res1 = input;
345:                 let mut res2 = input;
346:                 permutation_cpu_raw(&mut res1);
347:                 permutation_cpu(&pre_computed, &mut res2);
348:                 if res1 != res2 {
349:                     panic!("=> Inconsistencies between the cpu implementations of the permutations.\n\nOn input x = {:?},\n\npermutation_cpu_no_skip(x) = {:?}\n\npermutation_cpu_with_skips(x) = {:?}\n", input, res1, res2)
350:                 }
351:             });
352:         println!("=> No internal inconsistency found.")
353:     }
354: 
355:     #[test]
356:     fn cpu_test() {
357:         // Testing cpu performances. In debug mode, also tests the consistency between
358:         // the optimised and non-optimised cpu implementations of the permutation.
359:         consistency_cpu::<midnight_curves::Fq>(1);
360:     }

=== PRIMARY deliverables/sp04-native-finalizer-source-2026-09-10/point-origin-source-01/sources/midnight-circuits-7.2.4/src/verifier/accumulator.rs FULL FILE SHA25668d3f2af25e496b379a7ffd4359ab030f8f1d947fe6b4b0e67b0540b3cde41d6; FULL ===

14: //! Module for in-circuit accumulators (and their off-circuit counterpart).
15: //!
16: //! An accumulator is a pair of points (lhs, rhs), represented with
17: //! respective MSMs that is supposed to satisfy:
18: //!
19: //!   e(lhs, \[τ\]₂) = e(rhs, \[1\]₂)
20: //!
21: //! where τ is the corresponding SRS toxic waste.
22: //!
23: //! This property is preserved by the `accumulate` function, which combines two
24: //! accumulators into one; the resulting accumulator satisfies the property iff
25: //! both inputs do. We thus call this property the accumulator "invariant".
26: //!
27: //! Note that implication <= holds unconditionally, whereas implication => holds
28: //! "computationally".
29: 
30: use std::collections::BTreeMap;
31: 
32: use ff::Field;
33: use group::Group;
34: use midnight_proofs::{
35:     circuit::{Layouter, Value},
36:     plonk::Error,
37:     poly::{
38:         kzg::{
39:             msm::{DualMSM, MSMKZG},
40:             params::ParamsVerifierKZG,
41:         },
42:         CommitmentLabel,
43:     },
44: };
45: use num_bigint::BigUint;
46: use num_traits::One;
47: 
48: #[cfg(not(feature = "truncated-challenges"))]
49: use crate::verifier::utils::powers;
50: #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")]
51: use crate::verifier::utils::{truncate_off_circuit, truncated_powers};
52: use crate::{
53:     instructions::{hash::HashCPU, HashInstructions, PublicInputInstructions},
54:     types::{AssignedBit, InnerValue, Instantiable},
55:     verifier::{
56:         fixed_commitment_name,
57:         msm::{AssignedMsm, Msm},
58:         perm_commitment_name,
59:         utils::AssignedBoundedScalar,
60:         SelfEmulation,
61:     },
62: };
63: 
64: /// Type for off-circuit accumulators.
65: ///
66: /// Note that the points are represented with MSMs which may have
67: /// a fixed-base scalars part. In order to evaluate the accumulator, one may
68: /// thus need to provide the corresponding fixed bases.
69: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
70: pub struct Accumulator<S: SelfEmulation> {
71:     lhs: Msm<S>,
72:     rhs: Msm<S>,
73: }
74: 
75: /// Type for in-circuit accumulators (in-circuit analog of `Accumulator`).
76: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
77: pub struct AssignedAccumulator<C: SelfEmulation> {
78:     pub(crate) lhs: AssignedMsm<C>,
79:     pub(crate) rhs: AssignedMsm<C>,
80: }
81: 
82: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Accumulator<S> {
83:     /// Converts the off-circuit dual MSM into an `Accumulator<S>` by separating
84:     /// the fixed-base scalars aside in a BTreeMap indexed by the base name with
85:     /// a custom prefix.
86:     ///
87:     /// This function also takes a map of fixed bases indexed by their name,
88:     /// which is used to perform a sanity check on the fixed-base scalars of the
89:     /// dual MSM.
90:     pub fn from_dual_msm(
91:         dual_msm: DualMSM<S::Engine>,
92:         prefix: &str,
93:         fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<String, S::C>,
94:     ) -> Self {
95:         let (lhs, rhs) = dual_msm.split();
96: 
97:         let process_msm = |msm: Vec<(&CommitmentLabel, &S::F, &S::C)>| {
98:             let mut bases = Vec::with_capacity(msm.len());
99:             let mut scalars = Vec::with_capacity(msm.len());
100:             let mut fixed_base_scalars = BTreeMap::new();
101:             for (label, scalar, base) in msm {
102:                 match label {
103:                     CommitmentLabel::Fixed(i) => {
104:                         let name = fixed_commitment_name(prefix, *i);
105:                         assert_eq!(fixed_bases.get(&name), Some(base));
106:                         fixed_base_scalars.insert(name, *scalar);
107:                     }
108:                     CommitmentLabel::Permutation(i) => {
109:                         let name = perm_commitment_name(prefix, *i);
110:                         assert_eq!(fixed_bases.get(&name), Some(base));
111:                         fixed_base_scalars.insert(name, *scalar);
112:                     }
113:                     CommitmentLabel::Custom(s) if s == "-G" => {
114:                         assert_eq!(fixed_bases.get(s), Some(base));
115:                         fixed_base_scalars.insert("-G".into(), *scalar);
116:                     }
117:                     _ => {
118:                         bases.push(*base);
119:                         scalars.push(*scalar);
120:                     }
121:                 }
122:             }
123:             (bases, scalars, fixed_base_scalars)
124:         };
125: 
126:         let (lhs_bases, lhs_scalars, lhs_fixed_base_scalars) = process_msm(lhs);
127:         let (rhs_bases, rhs_scalars, rhs_fixed_base_scalars) = process_msm(rhs);
128: 
129:         Accumulator {
130:             lhs: Msm::new(&lhs_bases, &lhs_scalars, &lhs_fixed_base_scalars),
131:             rhs: Msm::new(&rhs_bases, &rhs_scalars, &rhs_fixed_base_scalars),
132:         }
133:     }
134: 
135:     /// Checks whether the accumulator, when evaluated with the provided
136:     /// fixed-bases, satisfies the pairing invariant w.r.t. the SRS verifier
137:     /// parameters.
138:     pub fn check(
139:         &self,
140:         params: &ParamsVerifierKZG<S::Engine>,
141:         fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<String, S::C>,
142:     ) -> bool {
143:         let lhs = MSMKZG::<S::Engine>::from_base(&self.lhs.eval(fixed_bases));
144:         let rhs = MSMKZG::<S::Engine>::from_base(&self.rhs.eval(fixed_bases));
145:         DualMSM::new(lhs, rhs).check(params)
146:     }
147: 
148:     /// Returns a trivial accumulator that satisfies the pairing invariant.
149:     ///
150:     /// The variable-base scalar is 1 (matching the invariant of collapsed
151:     /// accumulators, where the variable part has been collapsed to a single
152:     /// base with scalar 1). The base is the identity point and all
153:     /// fixed-base scalars are zero, so both sides evaluate to the identity
154:     /// regardless.
155:     pub fn trivial(fixed_base_names: &[String]) -> Self {
156:         let zero_fixed = fixed_base_names.iter().map(|n| (n.clone(), S::F::ZERO)).collect();
157:         Accumulator {
158:             lhs: Msm::new(&[S::C::identity()], &[S::F::ONE], &BTreeMap::new()),
159:             rhs: Msm::new(&[S::C::identity()], &[S::F::ONE], &zero_fixed),
160:         }
161:     }
162: 
163:     /// An accumulator a given lhs and rhs terms respectively.
164:     pub fn new(lhs: Msm<S>, rhs: Msm<S>) -> Self {
165:         Accumulator { lhs, rhs }
166:     }
167: 
168:     /// The left-hand side of this accumulator.
169:     pub fn lhs(&self) -> Msm<S> {
170:         self.lhs.clone()
171:     }
172: 
173:     /// The right-hand side of this accumulator.
174:     pub fn rhs(&self) -> Msm<S> {
175:         self.rhs.clone()
176:     }
177: 
178:     /// Given the actual fixed bases, resolves the fixed-base part of the
179:     /// internal MSMs by pairing each named scalar with its base and moving
180:     /// them to regular variable-base entries.
181:     ///
182:     /// After this call, `fixed_base_scalars` of each internal MSM becomes
183:     /// empty.
184:     ///
185:     /// # Panics
186:     ///
187:     /// If some of the keys in `fixed_base_scalars` from the internal MSMs do
188:     /// not appear in the provided `fixed_bases` map.
189:     pub fn resolve_fixed_bases(&mut self, fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<String, S::C>) {
190:         self.lhs.resolve_fixed_bases(fixed_bases);
191:         self.rhs.resolve_fixed_bases(fixed_bases);
192:     }
193: 
194:     /// Evaluates the variable part of the Accumulator collapsing each
195:     /// side to a single point (and a scalar of 1), leaving the fixed-base part
196:     /// of both sides intact.
197:     ///
198:     /// This function mutates self.
199:     pub fn collapse(&mut self) {
200:         self.lhs.collapse();
201:         self.rhs.collapse();
202:     }
203: 
204:     /// Accumulates several accumulators together. The resulting acc will
205:     /// satisfy the invariant iff all the accumulators individually do.
206:     pub fn accumulate(accs: &[Self]) -> Self {
207:         let hash_input =
208:             accs.iter().flat_map(AssignedAccumulator::as_public_input).collect::<Vec<_>>();
209: 
210:         let r = <S::SpongeChip as HashCPU<S::F, S::F>>::hash(&hash_input);
211:         let rs = (0..accs.len()).map(|i| r.pow([i as u64]));
212:         #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")]
213:         let rs = rs.map(truncate_off_circuit).collect::<Vec<_>>();
214: 
215:         let mut acc = accs[0].clone();
216:         for (other, ri) in accs.iter().zip(rs).skip(1) {
217:             acc.lhs = acc.lhs.accumulate_with_r(&other.lhs, ri);
218:             acc.rhs = acc.rhs.accumulate_with_r(&other.rhs, ri);
219:         }
220: 
221:         acc
222:     }
223: }
224: 
225: impl<S: SelfEmulation> InnerValue for AssignedAccumulator<S> {
226:     type Element = Accumulator<S>;
227: 
228:     fn value(&self) -> Value<Accumulator<S>> {
229:         (self.lhs.value())
230:             .zip(self.rhs.value())
231:             .map(|(lhs, rhs)| Accumulator { lhs, rhs })
232:     }
233: }
234: 
235: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Instantiable<S::F> for AssignedAccumulator<S> {
236:     fn as_public_input(acc: &Accumulator<S>) -> Vec<S::F> {
237:         [
238:             AssignedMsm::as_public_input(&acc.lhs),
239:             AssignedMsm::as_public_input(&acc.rhs),
240:         ]
241:         .into_iter()
242:         .flatten()
243:         .collect()
244:     }
245: 
246:     /// Always returns `None`: the encoding is not invertible, since the size of
247:     /// the inner MSMs cannot be known from the public input format.
248:     fn from_public_input(_fields: &[S::F]) -> Option<Accumulator<S>> {
249:         None
250:     }
251: }
252: 
253: impl<S: SelfEmulation> AssignedAccumulator<S> {
254:     /// Converts the off-circuit accumulator into two vectors of scalars. The
255:     /// first will be used as a normal instance, whereas the second will be
256:     /// plugged-in in as a committed instance.
257:     ///
258:     /// The committed instance part corresponds to the MSM (fixed and non-fixed)
259:     /// scalars of the accumulator RHS.
260:     pub fn as_public_input_with_committed_scalars(acc: &Accumulator<S>) -> (Vec<S::F>, Vec<S::F>) {
261:         let (rhs_scalars, rhs_committed_scalars) =
262:             AssignedMsm::as_public_input_with_committed_scalars(&acc.rhs);
263: 
264:         let normal_instance = [AssignedMsm::as_public_input(&acc.lhs), rhs_scalars]
265:             .into_iter()
266:             .flatten()
267:             .collect();
268: 
269:         (normal_instance, rhs_committed_scalars)
270:     }
271: }
272: 
273: impl<S: SelfEmulation> AssignedAccumulator<S> {
274:     /// Witnesses an accumulator of `lhs_len` bases/scalars and a `BTreeMap` of
275:     /// fixed_base_scalars indexed by the given `lhs_fixed_base_names`.
276:     ///
277:     /// Similar arguments determine the size and shape of the accumulator
278:     /// right-hand side.
279:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
280:     pub fn assign(
281:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
282:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
283:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
284:         lhs_len: usize,
285:         rhs_len: usize,
286:         lhs_fixed_base_names: &[String],
287:         rhs_fixed_base_names: &[String],
288:         acc_val: Value<Accumulator<S>>,
289:     ) -> Result<Self, Error> {
290:         let (acc_lhs_val, acc_rhs_val) = acc_val.map(|acc| (acc.lhs, acc.rhs)).unzip();
291:         Ok(AssignedAccumulator::new(
292:             AssignedMsm::<S>::assign(
293:                 layouter,
294:                 curve_chip,
295:                 scalar_chip,
296:                 lhs_len,
297:                 lhs_fixed_base_names,
298:                 acc_lhs_val,
299:             )?,
300:             AssignedMsm::<S>::assign(
301:                 layouter,
302:                 curve_chip,
303:                 scalar_chip,
304:                 rhs_len,
305:                 rhs_fixed_base_names,
306:                 acc_rhs_val,
307:             )?,
308:         ))
309:     }
310: 
311:     /// An `AssignedAccumulator` a given lhs and rhs terms respectively.
312:     pub fn new(lhs: AssignedMsm<S>, rhs: AssignedMsm<S>) -> Self {
313:         Self { lhs, rhs }
314:     }
315: 
316:     /// Scales the given acc by the given assigned bit.
317:     ///
318:     /// This function mutates self.
319:     pub fn scale_by_bit(
320:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
321:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
322:         cond: &AssignedBit<S::F>,
323:         acc: &mut Self,
324:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
325:         let cond_as_bounded = AssignedBoundedScalar {
326:             scalar: cond.clone().into(),
327:             bound: BigUint::one(),
328:         };
329:         acc.lhs.scale(layouter, scalar_chip, &cond_as_bounded)?;
330:         acc.rhs.scale(layouter, scalar_chip, &cond_as_bounded)
331:     }
332: 
333:     /// Evaluates the variable part of the AssignedAccumulator collapsing each
334:     /// side to a single point (and a scalar of 1), leaving the fixed-base part
335:     /// of both sides intact.
336:     ///
337:     /// Calls to this function will probably be the bottleneck of any recursive
338:     /// circuit, but it allows one to condense a carrying computation into a
339:     /// single point, enabling powerful predicates such as
340:     /// incrementally-verifiable computation (IVC).
341:     ///
342:     /// Alternatively, one may choose not to collapse an accumulator, fully
343:     /// restrict it with public inputs and evaluate it off-circuit.
344:     ///
345:     /// This function mutates self.
346:     pub fn collapse(
347:         &mut self,
348:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
349:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
350:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
351:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
352:         self.lhs.collapse(layouter, curve_chip, scalar_chip)?;
353:         self.rhs.collapse(layouter, curve_chip, scalar_chip)
354:     }
355: 
356:     /// Given the actual fixed bases, resolves the fixed-base part of the
357:     /// internal MSMs by pairing each named scalar with its base and moving
358:     /// them to regular variable-base entries.
359:     ///
360:     /// After this call, `fixed_base_scalars` of each internal MSM becomes
361:     /// empty.
362:     ///
363:     /// # Panics
364:     ///
365:     /// If some of the keys in `fixed_base_scalars` from the internal MSMs do
366:     /// not appear in the provided `fixed_bases` map.
367:     pub fn resolve_fixed_bases(&mut self, fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<String, S::AssignedPoint>) {
368:         self.lhs.resolve_fixed_bases(fixed_bases);
369:         self.rhs.resolve_fixed_bases(fixed_bases);
370:     }
371: 
372:     /// Accumulates several accumulators together. The resulting acc will
373:     /// satisfy the invariant iff all the accumulators individually do.
374:     pub fn accumulate(
375:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
376:         acc_pi_chip: &impl PublicInputInstructions<S::F, AssignedAccumulator<S>>,
377:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
378:         sponge_chip: &S::SpongeChip,
379:         accs: &[Self],
380:     ) -> Result<Self, Error> {
381:         let hash_input = accs
382:             .iter()
383:             .map(|acc| acc_pi_chip.as_public_input(layouter, acc))
384:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?
385:             .into_iter()
386:             .flatten()
387:             .collect::<Vec<_>>();
388: 
389:         let r = sponge_chip.hash(layouter, &hash_input)?;
390:         #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")]
391:         let rs = truncated_powers::<S::F>(layouter, scalar_chip, &r, accs.len())?;
392:         #[cfg(not(feature = "truncated-challenges"))]
393:         let rs = powers::<S::F>(layouter, scalar_chip, &r, accs.len())?
394:             .iter()
395:             .map(|ri| AssignedBoundedScalar::new(ri, None))
396:             .collect::<Vec<_>>();
397: 
398:         let mut acc = accs[0].clone();
399:         for (other, ri) in accs.iter().zip(rs).skip(1) {
400:             acc.lhs = acc.lhs.accumulate_with_r(layouter, scalar_chip, &other.lhs, &ri)?;
401:             acc.rhs = acc.rhs.accumulate_with_r(layouter, scalar_chip, &other.rhs, &ri)?;
402:         }
403: 
404:         Ok(acc)
405:     }
406: }

53: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
54: pub(crate) struct CommitmentData<S: SelfEmulation> {
55:     commitment: AssignedMsm<S>,
56:     set_index: usize,
57:     point_indices: Vec<usize>,
58:     evals: Vec<AssignedNative<S::F>>,
59: }
60: 
61: impl<S: SelfEmulation> CommitmentData<S> {
62:     fn new(commitment: AssignedMsm<S>) -> Self {
63:         CommitmentData {
64:             commitment,
65:             set_index: 0,
66:             point_indices: vec![],
67:             evals: vec![],
68:         }
69:     }
70: }
71: 
72: type IntermediateSets<S> = (
73:     Vec<CommitmentData<S>>,
74:     Vec<Vec<AssignedNative<<S as SelfEmulation>::F>>>,
75: );
76: 
77: fn construct_intermediate_sets<S: SelfEmulation, I>(
78:     queries: I,
79:     default_eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,
80: ) -> Result<IntermediateSets<S>, Error>
81: where
82:     I: IntoIterator<Item = VerifierQuery<S>> + Clone,
83: {
84:     // Construct sets of unique commitments and corresponding information about
85:     // their queries.
86:     let mut commitment_map: Vec<CommitmentData<S>> = vec![];
87: 
88:     // Also construct mapping from a unique point to a point_index. This defines
89:     // an ordering on the points.
90:     // Note that we use a HashMap, whereas halo2 uses a BTreeMap. This is because
91:     // `AssignedScalar` does not implement `Ord`, but implements `Hash`.
92:     // This difference is not a problem, since the order of keys does not matter
93:     // for this algorithm.
94:     let mut point_index_map = HashMap::new();
95: 
96:     // Iterate over all of the queries, computing the ordering of the points
97:     // while also creating new commitment data.
98:     for query in queries.clone() {
99:         let num_points = point_index_map.len();
100:         let point_idx = point_index_map.entry(query.get_point()).or_insert(num_points);
101: 
102:         if let Some(pos) =
103:             commitment_map.iter().position(|comm| comm.commitment == query.get_commitment())
104:         {
105:             if commitment_map[pos].point_indices.contains(point_idx) {
106:                 return Err(Error::Synthesis("repeated query".into()));
107:             }
108:             commitment_map[pos].point_indices.push(*point_idx);
109:         } else {
110:             let mut tmp = CommitmentData::new(query.get_commitment());
111:             tmp.point_indices.push(*point_idx);
112:             commitment_map.push(tmp);
113:         }
114:     }
115: 
116:     // Also construct inverse mapping from point_index to the point
117:     let mut inverse_point_index_map = HashMap::new();
118:     for (point, &point_index) in point_index_map.iter() {
119:         inverse_point_index_map.insert(point_index, point.clone());
120:     }
121: 
122:     // Construct map of unique ordered point_idx_sets to their set_idx.
123:     let mut point_idx_sets = HashMap::new();
124:     // Also construct mapping from commitment to point_idx_set
125:     let mut commitment_set_map = Vec::new();
126: 
127:     for commitment_data in commitment_map.iter() {
128:         let mut point_index_set = BTreeSet::new();
129:         // Note that point_index_set is ordered, unlike point_indices
130:         for &point_index in commitment_data.point_indices.iter() {
131:             point_index_set.insert(point_index);
132:         }
133: 
134:         // Push point_index_set to CommitmentData for the relevant commitment
135:         commitment_set_map.push((commitment_data.commitment.clone(), point_index_set.clone()));
136: 
137:         let num_sets = point_idx_sets.len();
138:         point_idx_sets.entry(point_index_set).or_insert(num_sets);
139:     }
140: 
141:     // Initialise empty evals vec for each unique commitment
142:     for commitment_data in commitment_map.iter_mut() {
143:         let len = commitment_data.point_indices.len();
144:         commitment_data.evals = vec![default_eval.clone(); len];
145:     }
146: 
147:     // Populate set_index, evals and points for each commitment using point_idx_sets
148:     for query in queries {
149:         // The index of the point at which the commitment is queried
150:         let point_index = point_index_map.get(&query.get_point()).unwrap();
151: 
152:         // The point_index_set at which the commitment was queried
153:         let mut point_index_set = BTreeSet::new();
154:         for (commitment, point_idx_set) in commitment_set_map.iter() {
155:             if query.get_commitment() == *commitment {
156:                 point_index_set.clone_from(point_idx_set);
157:             }
158:         }
159:         assert!(!point_index_set.is_empty());
160: 
161:         // The set_index of the point_index_set
162:         let set_index = point_idx_sets.get(&point_index_set).unwrap();
163:         for commitment_data in commitment_map.iter_mut() {
164:             if query.get_commitment() == commitment_data.commitment {
165:                 commitment_data.set_index = *set_index;
166:             }
167:         }
168:         let point_index_set: Vec<usize> = point_index_set.iter().cloned().collect();
169: 
170:         // The offset of the point_index in the point_index_set
171:         let point_index_in_set = point_index_set.iter().position(|i| i == point_index).unwrap();
172: 
173:         for commitment_data in commitment_map.iter_mut() {
174:             if query.get_commitment() == commitment_data.commitment {
175:                 // Insert the eval using the ordering of the point_index_set
176:                 commitment_data.evals[point_index_in_set] = query.get_eval();
177:             }
178:         }
179:     }
180: 
181:     // Get actual points in each point set
182:     let mut point_sets: Vec<Vec<AssignedNative<S::F>>> = vec![Vec::new(); point_idx_sets.len()];
183:     for (point_idx_set, &set_idx) in point_idx_sets.iter() {
184:         for &point_idx in point_idx_set.iter() {
185:             let point = inverse_point_index_map.get(&point_idx).unwrap();
186:             point_sets[set_idx].push((*point).clone());
187:         }
188:     }
189: 
190:     Ok((commitment_map, point_sets))
191: }
192: 
193: // ---------------------------
194: // See halo2 src/poly/query.rs
195: // ---------------------------

197: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
198: /// Structure to store a VerifierQuery.
199: pub(crate) struct VerifierQuery<S: SelfEmulation> {
200:     /// Point at which polynomial is queried.
201:     point: AssignedNative<S::F>,
202:     /// Optional label identifying the commitment in this query.
203:     label: CommitmentLabel,
204:     /// Commitment to the polynomial.
205:     commitment: AssignedMsm<S>,
206:     /// Evaluation of polynomial at query point.
207:     eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,
208: }
209: 
210: impl<S: SelfEmulation> VerifierQuery<S> {
211:     /// Create a verifier query on a commitment.
212:     /// This function requires an assigned bounded scalar of one as input.
213:     pub(crate) fn new(
214:         one: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>,
215:         point: &AssignedNative<S::F>,
216:         label: CommitmentLabel,
217:         commitment: &S::AssignedPoint,
218:         eval: &AssignedNative<S::F>,
219:     ) -> Self {
220:         Self {
221:             point: point.clone(),
222:             label,
223:             commitment: AssignedMsm::from_term(one, commitment),
224:             eval: eval.clone(),
225:         }
226:     }
227: 
228:     /// Create a verifier query on a commitment (represented as an MSM).
229:     pub(crate) fn new_from_msm(
230:         point: &AssignedNative<S::F>,
231:         label: CommitmentLabel,
232:         commitment: &AssignedMsm<S>,
233:         eval: &AssignedNative<S::F>,
234:     ) -> Self {
235:         Self {
236:             point: point.clone(),
237:             label,
238:             commitment: commitment.clone(),
239:             eval: eval.clone(),
240:         }
241:     }
242: 
243:     /// Create a verifier query on a fixed commitment (given its name).
244:     /// This function requires an assigned bounded scalar of one as input.
245:     pub(crate) fn new_fixed(
246:         one: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>,
247:         point: &AssignedNative<S::F>,
248:         label: CommitmentLabel,
249:         commitment_name: &str,
250:         eval: &AssignedNative<S::F>,
251:     ) -> Self {
252:         Self {
253:             point: point.clone(),
254:             label,
255:             commitment: AssignedMsm::from_fixed_term(one, commitment_name),
256:             eval: eval.clone(),
257:         }
258:     }
259: 
260:     fn get_point(&self) -> AssignedNative<S::F> {
261:         self.point.clone()
262:     }
263: 
264:     fn get_eval(&self) -> AssignedNative<S::F> {
265:         self.eval.clone()
266:     }
267: 
268:     fn get_commitment(&self) -> AssignedMsm<S> {
269:         self.commitment.clone()
270:     }
271: 
272:     #[allow(dead_code)]
273:     // Neither this function nor the commitment labels on verifier
274:     // queries are currently needed, but we keep them for consistency
275:     // with the `proofs/` implementation and for potential future use.
276:     fn get_label(&self) -> &CommitmentLabel {
277:         &self.label
278:     }
279: }

281: // ---------------------------------
282: // See halo2 src/utils/arithmetic.rs
283: // ---------------------------------
284: 
285: fn msm_inner_product<S: SelfEmulation>(
286:     layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
287:     scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
288:     msms: &[AssignedMsm<S>],
289:     scalars: &[AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>],
290: ) -> Result<AssignedMsm<S>, Error> {
291:     let mut res = AssignedMsm::empty();
292:     let mut msms = msms.to_vec();
293:     for (msm, s) in msms.iter_mut().zip(scalars) {
294:         msm.scale(layouter, scalar_chip, s)?;
295:         res.add_msm(layouter, scalar_chip, msm)?;
296:     }
297:     Ok(res)
298: }
299: 
300: /// Computes the inner product of a set of polynomial evaluations and a set of
301: /// scalar values. This function computes the weighted sum of polynomial
302: /// evaluations. Each vector in `evals_set` is multiplied element-wise by a
303: /// corresponding scalar from `scalars`, and the results are accumulated
304: /// into a single vector.
305: fn evals_inner_product<F: CircuitField>(
306:     layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
307:     scalar_chip: &impl ArithInstructions<F, AssignedNative<F>>,
308:     evals_set: &[Vec<AssignedNative<F>>],
309:     scalars: &[AssignedBoundedScalar<F>],
310: ) -> Result<Vec<AssignedNative<F>>, Error> {
311:     let zero = scalar_chip.assign_fixed(layouter, F::ZERO)?;
312:     let mut res = vec![zero.clone(); evals_set[0].len()];
313:     for (poly_evals, s) in evals_set.iter().zip(scalars) {
314:         for i in 0..res.len() {
315:             // res[i] := s.scalar * poly_evals[i] + res[i]
316:             res[i] = mul_add(layouter, scalar_chip, &s.scalar, &poly_evals[i], &res[i])?;
317:         }
318:     }
319:     Ok(res)
320: }
321: 
322: // -----------------------------
323: // See halo2 src/poly/kzg/mod.rs
324: // -----------------------------
325: 
326: /// Verifies a bunch of KZG queries in a multi-open argument.
327: /// The resulting accumulator satisfies the invariant iff all queries are valid.
328: pub(crate) fn multi_prepare<I, S: SelfEmulation>(
329:     layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
330:     #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")] curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
331:     scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
332:     transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
333:     queries: I,
334: ) -> Result<AssignedAccumulator<S>, Error>
335: where
336:     I: IntoIterator<Item = VerifierQuery<S>> + Clone,
337: {
338:     let x1 = transcript_gadget.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
339:     let x2 = transcript_gadget.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
340: 
341:     let default_eval = scalar_chip.assign_fixed(layouter, S::F::default())?;
342:     let (commitment_map, point_sets) = construct_intermediate_sets(queries, default_eval)?;
343: 
344:     let mut q_coms: Vec<_> = vec![vec![]; point_sets.len()];
345:     let mut q_eval_sets = vec![vec![]; point_sets.len()];
346: 
347:     for com_data in commitment_map.into_iter() {
348:         q_coms[com_data.set_index].push(com_data.commitment);
349:         q_eval_sets[com_data.set_index].push(com_data.evals);
350:     }
351: 
352:     let truncated_x1_powers = {
353:         let nb_x1_powers = q_coms.iter().map(|v| v.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
354:         assert!(nb_x1_powers >= q_eval_sets.iter().map(|v| v.len()).max().unwrap_or(0));
355:         truncated_powers(layouter, scalar_chip, &x1, nb_x1_powers)?
356:     };
357: 
358:     let q_coms = q_coms
359:         .iter()
360:         .map(|msms| msm_inner_product(layouter, scalar_chip, msms, &truncated_x1_powers))
361:         .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
362: 
363:     let q_eval_sets = q_eval_sets
364:         .iter()
365:         .map(|evals| evals_inner_product(layouter, scalar_chip, evals, &truncated_x1_powers))
366:         .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
367: 
368:     // Sort point sets by ascending cardinality to ensure the first set is the one
369:     // that contains fixed commitments (which are evaluated at x only). This
370:     // property is not necessary for the actual proving system, but it is important
371:     // for in-circuit verification of proofs. (It enables an optimization based on
372:     // an internal collapse.)
373:     //
374:     // The (len, i) key provides a deterministic total order even when two sets
375:     // share the same cardinality.
376:     let (q_coms, q_eval_sets, point_sets) = {
377:         let mut order: Vec<usize> = (0..point_sets.len()).collect();
378:         order.sort_by_key(|&i| (point_sets[i].len(), i));
379:         let q_coms: Vec<_> = order.iter().map(|&i| q_coms[i].clone()).collect();
380:         let q_eval_sets: Vec<_> = order.iter().map(|&i| q_eval_sets[i].clone()).collect();
381:         let point_sets: Vec<_> = order.iter().map(|&i| point_sets[i].clone()).collect();
382:         (q_coms, q_eval_sets, point_sets)
383:     };
384: 
385:     let f_com = transcript_gadget.read_point(layouter)?;
386: 
387:     let x3 = transcript_gadget.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
388:     #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")]
389:     let x3 = truncate::<S::F>(layouter, scalar_chip, &x3)?;
390:     #[cfg(not(feature = "truncated-challenges"))]
391:     let x3 = AssignedBoundedScalar::new(&x3, None);
392: 
393:     let mut q_evals_on_x3 = Vec::with_capacity(q_eval_sets.len());
394:     for _ in 0..q_eval_sets.len() {
395:         q_evals_on_x3.push(transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?);
396:     }
397: 
398:     let zero = scalar_chip.assign_fixed(layouter, S::F::ZERO)?;
399:     let f_eval = point_sets
400:         .iter()
401:         .zip(q_eval_sets.iter())
402:         .zip(q_evals_on_x3.iter())
403:         .rev()
404:         .try_fold(zero, |acc_eval, ((points, evals), proof_eval)| {
405:             let r_eval =
406:                 evaluate_interpolated_polynomial(layouter, scalar_chip, points, evals, &x3.scalar)?;
407: 
408:             // eval = (proof_eval - r_eval) / prod_i (x3 - point_i)
409:             let mut den = scalar_chip.sub(layouter, &x3.scalar, &points[0])?;
410:             for point in points.iter().skip(1) {
411:                 // TODO: This can be optimized with add_and_double_mul
412:                 let x3_minus_point = scalar_chip.sub(layouter, &x3.scalar, point)?;
413:                 den = scalar_chip.mul(layouter, &den, &x3_minus_point, None)?;
414:             }
415:             let mut eval = scalar_chip.sub(layouter, proof_eval, &r_eval)?;
416:             eval = scalar_chip.div(layouter, &eval, &den)?;
417: 
418:             // acc_eval * x2 + eval
419:             mul_add(layouter, scalar_chip, &acc_eval, &x2, &eval)
420:         })?;
421: 
422:     let x4 = transcript_gadget.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
423:     let truncated_x4_powers =
424:         truncated_powers::<S::F>(layouter, scalar_chip, &x4, q_coms.len() + 1)?;
425: 
426:     let one = AssignedBoundedScalar::one(layouter, scalar_chip)?;
427: 
428:     let final_com = {
429:         let mut coms = q_coms;
430:         let f_com_as_msm = AssignedMsm::from_term(&one, &f_com);
431: 
432:         // We collapse all AssignedMsm at this point to later leverage the fact that x4
433:         // powers are truncated. Exceptionally, the first one is not collapsed,
434:         // as the first x4 power is 1.
435:         #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")]
436:         coms.iter_mut()
437:             .skip(1)
438:             .try_for_each(|com| com.collapse(layouter, curve_chip, scalar_chip))?;
439:         coms.push(f_com_as_msm);
440: 
441:         msm_inner_product(layouter, scalar_chip, &coms, &truncated_x4_powers)?
442:     };
443: 
444:     let v = {
445:         let mut evals = q_evals_on_x3;
446:         evals.push(f_eval);
447: 
448:         let scalar_x4_powers: Vec<_> =
449:             truncated_x4_powers.iter().map(|s| s.scalar.clone()).collect();
450: 
451:         AssignedBoundedScalar::new(
452:             &inner_product(layouter, scalar_chip, &evals, &scalar_x4_powers)?,
453:             None,
454:         )
455:     };
456: 
457:     let pi = transcript_gadget.read_point(layouter)?;
458:     let pi_msm = AssignedMsm::from_term(&one, &pi);
459: 
460:     // Scale zπ
461:     let mut scaled_pi = pi_msm.clone();
462:     scaled_pi.scale(layouter, scalar_chip, &x3)?;
463: 
464:     // (π, C − vG + zπ)
465:     let left = pi_msm; // π
466: 
467:     let right = {
468:         let mut right = final_com; // C
469:         let minus_v_gen = AssignedMsm::from_fixed_term(&v, "-G");
470:         right.add_msm(layouter, scalar_chip, &minus_v_gen)?; // -vG
471:         right.add_msm(layouter, scalar_chip, &scaled_pi)?; // zπ
472:         right
473:     };
474: 
475:     Ok(AssignedAccumulator::new(left, right))
476: }

14: //! A module for in-circuit lookup arguments. It is the in-circuit analog
15: //! of file proofs/src/plonk/lookup/verifier.rs.
16: //!
17: //! The "expressions" part is dealt with in our `expressions/` directory.

32:     pub(crate) product_next_eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,
33:     pub(crate) permuted_input_eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,
34:     pub(crate) permuted_input_inv_eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,
35:     pub(crate) permuted_table_eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,

41:     permuted_table_commitment: S::AssignedPoint,
42: }

46:     permuted: PermutationCommitments<S>,
47:     product_commitment: S::AssignedPoint,
48: }
49: 
50: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
51: pub(crate) struct Evaluated<S: SelfEmulation> {
52:     committed: Committed<S>,
53:     pub(crate) evaluated: LookupEvaluated<S>,
54: }
55: 
56: pub(crate) fn read_permuted_commitments<S: SelfEmulation>(
57:     layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
58:     transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
59: ) -> Result<PermutationCommitments<S>, Error> {
60:     let permuted_input_commitment = transcript_gadget.read_point(layouter)?;
61:     let permuted_table_commitment = transcript_gadget.read_point(layouter)?;
62: 
63:     Ok(PermutationCommitments {
64:         permuted_input_commitment,
65:         permuted_table_commitment,
66:     })
67: }
68: 
69: impl<S: SelfEmulation> PermutationCommitments<S> {
70:     pub(crate) fn read_product_commitment(
71:         self,
72:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
73:         transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
74:     ) -> Result<Committed<S>, Error> {
75:         let product_commitment = transcript_gadget.read_point(layouter)?;
76: 
77:         Ok(Committed {
78:             permuted: self,
79:             product_commitment,
80:         })
81:     }
82: }
83: 
84: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Committed<S> {
85:     pub(crate) fn evaluate(
86:         self,
87:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
88:         transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
89:     ) -> Result<Evaluated<S>, Error> {
90:         let product_eval = transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?;
91:         let product_next_eval = transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?;
92:         let permuted_input_eval = transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?;
93:         let permuted_input_inv_eval = transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?;
94:         let permuted_table_eval = transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?;
95: 
96:         Ok(Evaluated {
97:             committed: self,
98:             evaluated: LookupEvaluated {
99:                 product_eval,
100:                 product_next_eval,
101:                 permuted_input_eval,
102:                 permuted_input_inv_eval,
103:                 permuted_table_eval,
104:             },
105:         })
106:     }
107: }
108: 
109: // "expressions" is implemented in `expressions/lookup.rs`
110: 
111: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Evaluated<S> {
112:     pub(crate) fn queries(
113:         &self,
114:         one: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>, // 1
115:         x: &AssignedNative<S::F>,          // evaluation point x
116:         x_next: &AssignedNative<S::F>,     // x * \omega
117:         x_prev: &AssignedNative<S::F>,     // x * \omega^(-1)
118:     ) -> Vec<VerifierQuery<S>> {
119:         vec![
120:             // Open lookup product commitment at x
121:             VerifierQuery::new(
122:                 one,
123:                 x,
124:                 CommitmentLabel::NoLabel,
125:                 &self.committed.product_commitment,
126:                 &self.evaluated.product_eval,
127:             ),
128:             // Open lookup input commitments at x
129:             VerifierQuery::new(
130:                 one,
131:                 x,
132:                 CommitmentLabel::NoLabel,
133:                 &self.committed.permuted.permuted_input_commitment,
134:                 &self.evaluated.permuted_input_eval,
135:             ),
136:             // Open lookup table commitments at x
137:             VerifierQuery::new(
138:                 one,
139:                 x,
140:                 CommitmentLabel::NoLabel,
141:                 &self.committed.permuted.permuted_table_commitment,
142:                 &self.evaluated.permuted_table_eval,
143:             ),
144:             // Open lookup input commitments at \omega^{-1} x
145:             VerifierQuery::new(
146:                 one,
147:                 x_prev,
148:                 CommitmentLabel::NoLabel,
149:                 &self.committed.permuted.permuted_input_commitment,
150:                 &self.evaluated.permuted_input_inv_eval,
151:             ),
152:             // Open lookup product commitment at \omega x
153:             VerifierQuery::new(
154:                 one,
155:                 x_next,
156:                 CommitmentLabel::NoLabel,
157:                 &self.committed.product_commitment,
158:                 &self.evaluated.product_next_eval,
159:             ),
160:         ]
161:     }
162: }

52: type VerifyingKey<S> =
53:     plonk::VerifyingKey<<S as SelfEmulation>::F, KZGCommitmentScheme<<S as SelfEmulation>::Engine>>;
54: 
55: /// Type for in-circuit verifying keys.
56: ///
57: /// This type carries off-circuit a lot of the information about the vk.
58: /// The only in-circuit field is the `transcript_repr`.
59: ///
60: /// The only entry-point for this function is intended to be
61: /// [VerifierGadget::assign_vk_as_public_input]. This is possible because fixed
62: /// commitments are dealt with off-circuit, i.e., the resulting accumulator of
63: /// [VerifierGadget::prepare] contains the scalars of the
64: /// fixed-commitments, in the `fixed_base_scalars` field (of its RHS).
65: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
66: pub struct AssignedVk<S: SelfEmulation> {
67:     vk_name: String,
68:     domain: EvaluationDomain<S::F>,
69:     cs: ConstraintSystem<S::F>,
70:     transcript_repr: AssignedNative<S::F>,
71: }
72: 
73: impl<S: SelfEmulation> InnerValue for AssignedVk<S> {
74:     type Element = VerifyingKey<S>;
75: 
76:     fn value(&self) -> Value<VerifyingKey<S>> {
77:         unimplemented!(
78:             "It is not possible to get a full verifying key out of an
79:              AssignedVk, as the latter does not include fixed commitments."
80:         )
81:     }
82: }
83: 
84: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Instantiable<S::F> for AssignedVk<S> {
85:     fn as_public_input(vk: &VerifyingKey<S>) -> Vec<S::F> {
86:         AssignedNative::<S::F>::as_public_input(&vk.transcript_repr())
87:     }
88: 
89:     /// Always returns `None`: [Self::as_public_input] encodes the verifying key
90:     /// as its `transcript_repr()`, which is not invertible.
91:     fn from_public_input(_fields: &[S::F]) -> Option<VerifyingKey<S>> {
92:         None
93:     }
94: }
95: 
96: /// Canonical name for the i-th verifying-key fixed commitment.
97: pub fn fixed_commitment_name(prefix: &str, i: usize) -> String {
98:     format!("{prefix}_fixed_com_{i}")
99: }
100: 
101: /// Canonical name for the i-th verifying-key permutation commitment.
102: pub fn perm_commitment_name(prefix: &str, i: usize) -> String {
103:     format!("{prefix}_perm_com_{i}")
104: }
105: 
106: impl<S: SelfEmulation> AssignedVk<S> {
107:     /// Canonical name for the i-th fixed commitment of this AssignedVk.
108:     fn fixed_commitment_name(&self, i: usize) -> String {
109:         fixed_commitment_name(&self.vk_name, i)
110:     }
111: 
112:     /// Canonical name for the i-th perm commitment of this AssignedVk.
113:     fn perm_commitment_name(&self, i: usize) -> String {
114:         perm_commitment_name(&self.vk_name, i)
115:     }
116: }
117: 
118: /// Extracts the fixed bases from the verifying key, indexed by their
119: /// canonical name.
120: pub fn fixed_bases<S: SelfEmulation>(
121:     vk_name: &str,
122:     vk: &VerifyingKey<S>,
123: ) -> BTreeMap<String, S::C> {
124:     let mut fixed_bases = BTreeMap::new();
125: 
126:     fixed_bases.insert(String::from("-G"), -S::C::generator());
127: 
128:     let fixed_commitments = vk.fixed_commitments();
129:     let perm_commitments = vk.permutation().commitments();
130: 
131:     for (i, com) in fixed_commitments.iter().enumerate() {
132:         fixed_bases.insert(fixed_commitment_name(vk_name, i), *com);
133:     }
134: 
135:     for (i, com) in perm_commitments.iter().enumerate() {
136:         fixed_bases.insert(perm_commitment_name(vk_name, i), *com);
137:     }
138: 
139:     fixed_bases
140: }
141: 
142: /// The names of the fixed bases of a verifying key. This function is designed
143: /// to be called before having an actual verifying key. Only the number of fixed
144: /// and permutation commitments is necessary, not their actual values.
145: pub fn fixed_base_names<S: SelfEmulation>(
146:     vk_name: &str,
147:     nb_fixed_commitments: usize,
148:     nb_perm_commitments: usize,
149: ) -> Vec<String> {
150:     let mut names = Vec::with_capacity(nb_fixed_commitments + nb_perm_commitments + 1);
151: 
152:     // This term will be introduced by the KZG multiopen argument as a fixed base.
153:     // It corresponds to the negated designated generator. It is not proper of the
154:     // verifying key, but there is no harm in having it here (it needs to be
155:     // introduced at some point anyway and this is a good place).
156:     names.push("-G".into());
157: 
158:     for i in 0..nb_fixed_commitments {
159:         names.push(fixed_commitment_name(vk_name, i));
160:     }
161: 
162:     for i in 0..nb_perm_commitments {
163:         names.push(perm_commitment_name(vk_name, i));
164:     }
165: 
166:     names
167: }

14: //! A module for in-circuit partial MSMs and its off-circuit analog.
15: //! These MSM have a fixed-base part which is represented by the corresponding
16: //! scalars only.
17: //! (The bases are assumed to be fixed and globally known.)
18: 
19: use std::collections::{btree_map::Entry, BTreeMap};
20: 
21: use ff::Field;
22: use midnight_curves::msm::msm_best;
23: use midnight_proofs::{
24:     circuit::{Layouter, Value},
25:     plonk::Error,
26: };
27: 
28: use crate::{
29:     field::AssignedNative,
30:     instructions::PublicInputInstructions,
31:     types::{InnerValue, Instantiable},
32:     verifier::{
33:         types::SelfEmulation,
34:         utils::{
35:             add_bounded_scalars, assign_bounded_scalars, mul_bounded_scalars, AssignedBoundedScalar,
36:         },
37:     },
38: };
39: 
40: /// Type for off-circuit multi-scalar multiplications.
41: ///
42: /// This structure represents the following computation:
43: /// `<scalars, bases> + <fixed_bases, fixed_base_scalars>`
44: ///
45: /// Note that the `fixed_bases` are not part of this structure, they are
46: /// supposed to be globally known and will be provided when evaluating the MSM.
47: ///
48: /// (`scalars` and `bases` are guaranteed to have the same length.)
49: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
50: pub struct Msm<S: SelfEmulation> {
51:     bases: Vec<S::C>,
52:     scalars: Vec<S::F>,
53:     fixed_base_scalars: BTreeMap<String, S::F>,
54: }
55: 
56: /// Type for in-circuit multi-scalar multiplications.
57: ///
58: /// This is the in-circuit analog of `Msm<C>`.
59: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
60: pub struct AssignedMsm<S: SelfEmulation> {
61:     bases: Vec<S::AssignedPoint>,
62:     pub(crate) scalars: Vec<AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>>,
63:     fixed_base_scalars: BTreeMap<String, AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>>,
64: }
65: 
66: impl<S: SelfEmulation> PartialEq for AssignedMsm<S> {
67:     fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
68:         self.bases == other.bases
69:             && self.scalars == other.scalars
70:             && self.fixed_base_scalars == other.fixed_base_scalars
71:     }
72: }
73: 
74: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Eq for AssignedMsm<S> {}
75: 
76: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Msm<S> {
77:     /// Creates a new MSM from the given slice of bases, scalars and a BTreeMap
78:     /// of fixed_base_scalars.
79:     ///
80:     /// # Panics
81:     ///
82:     /// If `|bases| != |scalars|`.
83:     pub fn new(
84:         bases: &[S::C],
85:         scalars: &[S::F],
86:         fixed_base_scalars: &BTreeMap<String, S::F>,
87:     ) -> Self {
88:         assert_eq!(bases.len(), scalars.len());
89:         Msm {
90:             bases: bases.to_vec(),
91:             scalars: scalars.to_vec(),
92:             fixed_base_scalars: fixed_base_scalars.clone(),
93:         }
94:     }
95: 
96:     /// The bases of this MSM.
97:     pub fn bases(&self) -> Vec<S::C> {
98:         self.bases.clone()
99:     }
100: 
101:     /// The (non-fixed-base) scalars of this MSM.
102:     pub fn scalars(&self) -> Vec<S::F> {
103:         self.scalars.clone()
104:     }
105: 
106:     /// The fixed-base scalars of this MSM.
107:     pub fn fixed_base_scalars(&self) -> BTreeMap<String, S::F> {
108:         self.fixed_base_scalars.clone()
109:     }
110: 
111:     /// Creates a new MSM from the given base-scalar pairs, with an empty tree
112:     /// of fixed_base_scalars.
113:     ///
114:     /// # Panics
115:     ///
116:     /// If `|bases| != |scalars|`.
117:     pub fn from_terms(bases: &[S::C], scalars: &[S::F]) -> Self {
118:         assert_eq!(bases.len(), scalars.len());
119:         Msm {
120:             bases: bases.to_vec(),
121:             scalars: scalars.to_vec(),
122:             fixed_base_scalars: BTreeMap::new(),
123:         }
124:     }
125: 
126:     /// Evaluates the variable part of the AssignedMsm (the scalar-base pairs)
127:     /// collapsing it to a single point (and a scalar of 1), leaving the
128:     /// fixed-base part intact.
129:     ///
130:     /// This function mutates self.
131:     pub fn collapse(&mut self) {
132:         let affine_bases: Vec<S::G1Affine> = self.bases.iter().map(|&b| b.into()).collect();
133:         let collapsed_base = msm_best(&self.scalars, &affine_bases);
134: 
135:         self.bases = vec![collapsed_base];
136:         self.scalars = vec![S::F::ONE];
137:     }
138: 
139:     /// Given the actual fixed bases, resolves the fixed-base part of the MSM
140:     /// by pairing each named scalar with its base and moving them to regular
141:     /// variable-base entries.
142:     ///
143:     /// After this call, `fixed_base_scalars` becomes empty.
144:     ///
145:     /// # Panics
146:     ///
147:     /// If some of the keys in `fixed_base_scalars` do not appear in the
148:     /// provided `fixed_bases` map.
149:     pub fn resolve_fixed_bases(&mut self, fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<String, S::C>) {
150:         for (name, scalar) in &self.fixed_base_scalars {
151:             let base = fixed_bases.get(name).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("Base not provided: {name}"));
152:             self.bases.push(*base);
153:             self.scalars.push(*scalar);
154:         }
155:         self.fixed_base_scalars.clear();
156:     }
157: 
158:     /// Evaluates the MSM with the provided fixed_bases.
159:     /// I.e. it computes `<scalars, bases> + <fixed_bases, fixed_base_scalars>`.
160:     ///
161:     /// # Panics
162:     ///
163:     /// If some of the keys in the `fixed_base_scalars` of the MSM do not appear
164:     /// in the tree of `fixed_bases`.
165:     ///
166:     /// Note that the converse is not a problem, i.e., the keys of `fixed_bases`
167:     /// can be a superset of the keys of `fixed_base_scalars`.
168:     pub fn eval(&self, fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<String, S::C>) -> S::C {
169:         let mut bases = self.bases.clone();
170:         let mut scalars = self.scalars.clone();
171: 
172:         for (key, scalar) in self.fixed_base_scalars.iter() {
173:             let base = fixed_bases.get(key).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("Base not provided: {key}"));
174:             bases.push(*base);
175:             scalars.push(*scalar);
176:         }
177: 
178:         let affine_bases: Vec<S::G1Affine> = bases.iter().map(|&b| b.into()).collect();
179:         msm_best(&scalars, &affine_bases)
180:     }
181: 
182:     /// Accumulates two MSMs with the given scalar r.
183:     /// The resulting MSM evaluates (on any `fixed_bases`) to
184:     /// `self.eval(fixed_bases) + r * other.eval(fixed_bases)`.
185:     pub fn accumulate_with_r(&self, other: &Self, r: S::F) -> Self {
186:         let mut acc = self.clone();
187: 
188:         acc.bases.extend(other.bases.clone());
189:         acc.scalars.extend(other.scalars.iter().map(|s| *s * r));
190: 
191:         for (key, value) in other.fixed_base_scalars.clone() {
192:             let r_times_value = r * value;
193:             acc.fixed_base_scalars
194:                 .entry(key)
195:                 .and_modify(|e| *e += r_times_value)
196:                 .or_insert(r_times_value);
197:         }
198: 
199:         acc
200:     }
201: }
202: 
203: impl<S: SelfEmulation> InnerValue for AssignedMsm<S> {
204:     type Element = Msm<S>;
205: 
206:     fn value(&self) -> Value<Self::Element> {
207:         let bases: Value<Vec<S::C>> = Value::from_iter(self.bases.iter().map(|base| base.value()));
208: 
209:         let scalars: Value<Vec<S::F>> =
210:             Value::from_iter(self.scalars.iter().map(|s| s.scalar.value().copied()));
211: 
212:         let fixed_based_scalars: Value<BTreeMap<String, S::F>> = Value::from_iter(
213:             self.fixed_base_scalars
214:                 .iter()
215:                 .map(|(name, s)| s.scalar.value().map(|s| (name.clone(), *s))),
216:         );
217: 
218:         scalars
219:             .zip(bases)
220:             .zip(fixed_based_scalars)
221:             .map(|((scalars, bases), fixed_base_scalars)| Msm {
222:                 bases,
223:                 scalars,
224:                 fixed_base_scalars,
225:             })
226:     }
227: }
228: 
229: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Instantiable<S::F> for AssignedMsm<S> {
230:     fn as_public_input(msm: &Msm<S>) -> Vec<S::F> {
231:         [
232:             msm.bases.iter().flat_map(S::AssignedPoint::as_public_input).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
233:             msm.scalars.clone(),
234:             msm.fixed_base_scalars.values().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
235:         ]
236:         .into_iter()
237:         .flatten()
238:         .collect::<Vec<_>>()
239:     }
240: 
241:     /// Always returns `None`: the flat encoding loses the structural metadata
242:     /// needed to rebuild the MSM, so it is not invertible.
243:     fn from_public_input(_fields: &[S::F]) -> Option<Msm<S>> {
244:         None
245:     }
246: }
247: 
248: impl<S: SelfEmulation> AssignedMsm<S> {
249:     /// Converts the off-circuit MSM into two vectors of scalars. The first
250:     /// will be used as a normal instance, whereas the second will be plugged-in
251:     /// in as a committed instance.
252:     ///
253:     /// The committed instance part corresponds to the (fixed and non-fixed)
254:     /// scalars of the MSM.
255:     pub fn as_public_input_with_committed_scalars(msm: &Msm<S>) -> (Vec<S::F>, Vec<S::F>) {
256:         let normal_instance =
257:             msm.bases.iter().flat_map(S::AssignedPoint::as_public_input).collect();
258: 
259:         let committed_instance = [
260:             msm.scalars.clone(),
261:             msm.fixed_base_scalars.values().copied().collect(),
262:         ]
263:         .concat();
264: 
265:         (normal_instance, committed_instance)
266:     }
267: }
268: 
269: impl<S: SelfEmulation> AssignedMsm<S> {
270:     pub(crate) fn in_circuit_as_public_input(
271:         &self,
272:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
273:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
274:     ) -> Result<Vec<AssignedNative<S::F>>, Error> {
275:         Ok([
276:             self.bases
277:                 .iter()
278:                 .map(|base| curve_chip.as_public_input(layouter, base))
279:                 .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?
280:                 .into_iter()
281:                 .flatten()
282:                 .collect::<Vec<_>>(),
283:             self.scalars.iter().map(|s| s.clone().scalar).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
284:             self.fixed_base_scalars.values().map(|s| s.clone().scalar).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
285:         ]
286:         .into_iter()
287:         .flatten()
288:         .collect())
289:     }
290: 
291:     pub(crate) fn constrain_as_public_input(
292:         &self,
293:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
294:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
295:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
296:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
297:         self.bases
298:             .iter()
299:             .try_for_each(|base| curve_chip.constrain_as_public_input(layouter, base))?;
300: 
301:         self.scalars
302:             .iter()
303:             .try_for_each(|s| scalar_chip.constrain_as_public_input(layouter, &s.clone().scalar))?;
304: 
305:         self.fixed_base_scalars
306:             .values()
307:             .try_for_each(|s| scalar_chip.constrain_as_public_input(layouter, &s.clone().scalar))
308:     }
309: 
310:     pub(crate) fn constrain_as_public_input_with_committed_scalars(
311:         &self,
312:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
313:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
314:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
315:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
316:         self.bases
317:             .iter()
318:             .try_for_each(|base| curve_chip.constrain_as_public_input(layouter, base))?;
319: 
320:         self.scalars.iter().try_for_each(|s| {
321:             let mut a = S::F::ZERO;
322:             s.scalar.clone().value().map(|v| a = *v);
323:             S::constrain_scalar_as_committed_public_input(layouter, scalar_chip, &s.scalar)
324:         })?;
325: 
326:         self.fixed_base_scalars.values().try_for_each(|s| {
327:             S::constrain_scalar_as_committed_public_input(layouter, scalar_chip, &s.scalar)
328:         })
329:     }
330: }
331: 
332: impl<S: SelfEmulation> AssignedMsm<S> {
333:     /// Witnesses an MSM computation of `len` bases/scalars and a `BTreeMap` of
334:     /// fixed_base_scalars indexed by the given `fixed_base_names`.
335:     ///
336:     /// # Warning
337:     ///
338:     /// The points of the MSM are not enforced to be part of the relevant prime
339:     /// order subgroup.
340:     ///
341:     /// # Panics
342:     ///
343:     /// If `msm_value` is known and its number of variable bases differs from
344:     /// `len`, or its number of fixed-base scalars differs from
345:     /// `fixed_base_names.len()`.
346:     pub fn assign(
347:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
348:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
349:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
350:         len: usize,
351:         fixed_base_names: &[String],
352:         msm_value: Value<Msm<S>>,
353:     ) -> Result<Self, Error> {
354:         let bases_val = msm_value.as_ref().map(|msm| msm.bases.clone()).transpose_vec(len);
355: 
356:         let scalars_val = msm_value.as_ref().map(|msm| msm.scalars.clone()).transpose_vec(len);
357: 
358:         let fixed_base_scalars_val = msm_value
359:             .as_ref()
360:             .map(|msm| {
361:                 // We only use the keys inside the Value to iterate over it in the right order,
362:                 // these are then discarded.
363:                 msm.fixed_base_scalars.iter().map(|s| *s.1).collect::<Vec<_>>()
364:             })
365:             .transpose_vec(fixed_base_names.len());
366: 
367:         // Sort the fixed_base_names to ensure consistency with the BTreeMap.
368:         let mut fixed_base_names = fixed_base_names.to_vec();
369:         fixed_base_names.sort();
370: 
371:         let bases = bases_val
372:             .iter()
373:             .map(|p| S::assign_without_subgroup_check(layouter, curve_chip, *p))
374:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
375:         let scalars = assign_bounded_scalars(layouter, scalar_chip, &scalars_val)?;
376:         let fixed_base_scalars: BTreeMap<String, AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>> = {
377:             let scalars = assign_bounded_scalars(layouter, scalar_chip, &fixed_base_scalars_val)?;
378:             fixed_base_names.iter().cloned().zip(scalars).collect()
379:         };
380: 
381:         Ok(AssignedMsm {
382:             scalars,
383:             bases,
384:             fixed_base_scalars,
385:         })
386:     }
387: 
388:     /// An empty AssignedMsm with no fixed base scalars, that evaluates to the
389:     /// identity point.
390:     pub fn empty() -> Self {
391:         Self {
392:             scalars: vec![],
393:             bases: vec![],
394:             fixed_base_scalars: BTreeMap::new(),
395:         }
396:     }
397: 
398:     /// Creates a new MSM from the given base (with a scalar of 1).
399:     pub fn from_term(scalar: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>, base: &S::AssignedPoint) -> Self {
400:         Self {
401:             scalars: vec![scalar.clone()],
402:             bases: vec![base.clone()],
403:             fixed_base_scalars: BTreeMap::new(),
404:         }
405:     }
406: 
407:     /// Creates a new MSM from the given fixed base name (with a scalar of 1).
408:     pub fn from_fixed_term(scalar: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>, base_name: &str) -> Self {
409:         Self {
410:             scalars: vec![],
411:             bases: vec![],
412:             fixed_base_scalars: [(base_name.to_string(), scalar.clone())].into_iter().collect(),
413:         }
414:     }
415: 
416:     /// Adds a `(scalar, base)` term to the AssignedMsm.
417:     pub fn add_term(&mut self, scalar: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>, base: &S::AssignedPoint) {
418:         self.scalars.push(scalar.clone());
419:         self.bases.push(base.clone());
420:     }
421: 
422:     /// Adds two AssignedMsm.
423:     pub fn add_msm(
424:         &mut self,
425:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
426:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
427:         other: &Self,
428:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
429:         self.scalars.extend(other.scalars.clone());
430:         self.bases.extend(other.bases.clone());
431: 
432:         for (key, value) in other.fixed_base_scalars.clone() {
433:             match self.fixed_base_scalars.entry(key) {
434:                 Entry::Occupied(mut occ) => {
435:                     *occ.get_mut() = add_bounded_scalars(layouter, scalar_chip, occ.get(), &value)?;
436:                 }
437:                 Entry::Vacant(vac) => {
438:                     vac.insert(value);
439:                 }
440:             }
441:         }
442: 
443:         Ok(())
444:     }
445: 
446:     /// Evaluates the variable part of the AssignedMsm (the scalar-base pairs)
447:     /// collapsing it to a single point (and a scalar of 1), leaving the
448:     /// fixed-base part intact.
449:     ///
450:     /// This function mutates self.
451:     pub fn collapse(
452:         &mut self,
453:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
454:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
455:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
456:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
457:         let scalars = self
458:             .scalars
459:             .iter()
460:             .map(|s| (s.scalar.clone(), s.bound.bits() as usize))
461:             .collect::<Vec<_>>();
462: 
463:         let collapsed_base = S::msm(layouter, curve_chip, &scalars, &self.bases)?;
464: 
465:         self.bases = vec![collapsed_base];
466:         self.scalars = vec![AssignedBoundedScalar::one(layouter, scalar_chip)?];
467: 
468:         Ok(())
469:     }
470: 
471:     /// Given the actual fixed bases, resolves the fixed-base part of the MSM
472:     /// by pairing each named scalar with its base and moving them to regular
473:     /// variable-base entries.
474:     ///
475:     /// After this call, `fixed_base_scalars` becomes empty.
476:     ///
477:     /// # Panics
478:     ///
479:     /// If some of the keys in `fixed_base_scalars` do not appear in the
480:     /// provided `fixed_bases` map.
481:     pub fn resolve_fixed_bases(&mut self, fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<String, S::AssignedPoint>) {
482:         for (name, scalar) in &self.fixed_base_scalars {
483:             let base = fixed_bases
484:                 .get(name)
485:                 .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("Fixed base not provided: {name}"));
486:             self.bases.push(base.clone());
487:             self.scalars.push(scalar.clone());
488:         }
489:         self.fixed_base_scalars.clear();
490:     }
491: 
492:     /// Scales all the scalars of the AssignedMsm by the given factor r.
493:     ///
494:     /// This function mutates self.
495:     pub fn scale(
496:         &mut self,
497:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
498:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
499:         r: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>,
500:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
501:         self.scalars = (self.scalars.iter())
502:             .map(|s| mul_bounded_scalars(layouter, scalar_chip, s, r))
503:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
504: 
505:         for s in self.fixed_base_scalars.values_mut() {
506:             *s = mul_bounded_scalars(layouter, scalar_chip, s, r)?;
507:         }
508: 
509:         Ok(())
510:     }
511: 
512:     /// Accumulates two AssignedMSMs with the a given scalar r.
513:     /// The resulting AssignedMSMs evaluates (on `fixed_bases`) to
514:     /// `self.eval(fixed_bases) + r * other.eval(fixed_bases)`.
515:     pub fn accumulate_with_r(
516:         &self,
517:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
518:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
519:         other: &Self,
520:         r: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>,
521:     ) -> Result<Self, Error> {
522:         let mut other = other.clone();
523:         other.scale(layouter, scalar_chip, r)?;
524: 
525:         let mut acc = self.clone();
526:         acc.add_msm(layouter, scalar_chip, &other)?;
527: 
528:         Ok(acc)
529:     }
530: }

33: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
34: pub(crate) struct Committed<S: SelfEmulation> {
35:     permutation_product_commitments: Vec<S::AssignedPoint>,
36: }

38: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
39: pub(crate) struct EvaluatedSet<S: SelfEmulation> {
40:     pub(crate) permutation_product_eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,
41:     pub(crate) permutation_product_next_eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,
42:     pub(crate) permutation_product_last_eval: Option<AssignedNative<S::F>>,
43: }
44:

47:     pub(crate) permutation_evals: Vec<AssignedNative<S::F>>,
48: }
49: 
50: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]

52:     coms: Committed<S>,
53:     pub(crate) sets: Vec<EvaluatedSet<S>>,

55: 
56: pub(crate) fn read_product_commitments<S: SelfEmulation>(
57:     layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
58:     transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
59:     cs: &ConstraintSystem<S::F>,
60: ) -> Result<Committed<S>, Error> {
61:     let chunk_len = cs.degree() - 2;
62: 
63:     let permutation_product_commitments = cs
64:         .permutation()
65:         .get_columns()
66:         .chunks(chunk_len)
67:         .map(|_| transcript_gadget.read_point(layouter))
68:         .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
69: 
70:     Ok(Committed {
71:         permutation_product_commitments,
72:     })
73: }
74: 
75: /// This is the in-circuit analog of `evaluate` implemented for `VerifyingKey`
76: /// in halo2 src/plonk/permutation/verifier.rs.
77: ///
78: /// Instead of evaluating it for the `VerifyingKey`, we directly require the
79: /// `nb_perm_commitments` as an argument.
80: pub(crate) fn evaluate_permutation_common<S: SelfEmulation>(
81:     layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
82:     transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
83:     nb_perm_commitments: usize,
84: ) -> Result<CommonEvaluated<S>, Error> {
85:     let permutation_evals = (0..nb_perm_commitments)
86:         .map(|_| transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter))
87:         .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
88: 
89:     Ok(CommonEvaluated { permutation_evals })
90: }
91: 
92: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Committed<S> {
93:     pub(crate) fn evaluate(
94:         self,
95:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
96:         transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
97:     ) -> Result<Evaluated<S>, Error> {
98:         let mut sets = vec![];
99: 
100:         let mut iter = self.permutation_product_commitments.iter();
101: 
102:         while iter.next().is_some() {
103:             let permutation_product_eval = transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?;
104:             let permutation_product_next_eval = transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?;
105:             let permutation_product_last_eval = if iter.len() > 0 {
106:                 Some(transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?)
107:             } else {
108:                 None
109:             };
110: 
111:             sets.push(EvaluatedSet {
112:                 permutation_product_eval,
113:                 permutation_product_next_eval,
114:                 permutation_product_last_eval,
115:             });
116:         }
117: 
118:         Ok(Evaluated { coms: self, sets })
119:     }
120: }
121: 
122: // "expressions" is implemented in `expressions/permutation.rs`
123: 
124: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Evaluated<S> {
125:     pub(crate) fn queries(
126:         &self,
127:         one: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>, // 1
128:         x: &AssignedNative<S::F>,          // evaluation point x
129:         x_next: &AssignedNative<S::F>,     // x * \omega
130:         x_last: &AssignedNative<S::F>,     // x * \omega^(-blinding_factors + 1)
131:     ) -> Vec<VerifierQuery<S>> {
132:         let mut queries = vec![];
133:         for (i, set) in self.sets.iter().enumerate() {
134:             // Open permutation product commitments at x and \omega x
135:             queries.push(VerifierQuery::new(
136:                 one,
137:                 x,
138:                 CommitmentLabel::NoLabel,
139:                 &self.coms.permutation_product_commitments[i],
140:                 &set.permutation_product_eval,
141:             ));
142:             queries.push(VerifierQuery::new(
143:                 one,
144:                 x_next,
145:                 CommitmentLabel::NoLabel,
146:                 &self.coms.permutation_product_commitments[i],
147:                 &set.permutation_product_next_eval,
148:             ));
149:         }
150: 
151:         // Open it at \omega^{last} x for all but the last set
152:         for (i, set) in self.sets.iter().enumerate().rev().skip(1) {
153:             queries.push(VerifierQuery::new(
154:                 one,
155:                 x_last,
156:                 CommitmentLabel::NoLabel,
157:                 &self.coms.permutation_product_commitments[i],
158:                 set.permutation_product_last_eval.as_ref().unwrap(),
159:             ));
160:         }
161: 
162:         queries
163:     }
164: }
165: 
166: impl<S: SelfEmulation> CommonEvaluated<S> {
167:     /// This function differs from the halo2 one because we deal with fixed
168:     /// commitments off-circuit. Thus, we do not require the actual permutation
169:     /// common commitments, but their names.
170:     pub(crate) fn queries(
171:         &self,
172:         commitment_names: &[String],
173:         one: &AssignedBoundedScalar<S::F>, // 1
174:         x: &AssignedNative<S::F>,          // evaluation point x
175:     ) -> Vec<VerifierQuery<S>> {
176:         assert_eq!(commitment_names.len(), self.permutation_evals.len());
177: 
178:         commitment_names
179:             .iter()
180:             .enumerate()
181:             .zip(self.permutation_evals.iter())
182:             .map(|((i, com_name), eval)| {
183:                 VerifierQuery::new_fixed(one, x, CommitmentLabel::Permutation(i), com_name, eval)
184:             })
185:             .collect()
186:     }
187: }
