114:     fn read(buffer: &mut impl Read) -> io::Result<Self> {
115:         let mut bytes = <Self as PrimeField>::Repr::default();
116: 
117:         buffer.read_exact(bytes.as_mut())?;
118: 
119:         Option::from(Self::from_repr(bytes))
120:             .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("Invalid BN scalar encoding in proof"))
121:     }
122: }
123: 
124: #[cfg(feature = "dev-curves")]
125: impl Sampleable<Blake2bState> for Fr {
126:     fn sample(hash_output: Vec<u8>) -> Self {
127:         assert!(hash_output.len() <= 64);
128:         let mut bytes = [0u8; 64];
129:         bytes[..hash_output.len()].copy_from_slice(&hash_output);
130:         Fr::from_uniform_bytes(&bytes)
131:     }
132: }
133: 
134: // //////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
135: // /// Implementation of Hashable for BLS12-381 with Blake //
136: // //////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
137: 
138: impl<H: TranscriptHash<Input = Vec<u8>>> Hashable<H> for midnight_curves::G1Projective {
139:     /// Converts it to compressed form in bytes
140:     fn to_input(&self) -> Vec<u8> {
141:         Hashable::<H>::to_bytes(self)
142:     }
143: 
144:     fn to_bytes(&self) -> Vec<u8> {
145:         <Self as GroupEncoding>::to_bytes(self).as_ref().to_vec()
146:     }
147: 
148:     fn read(buffer: &mut impl Read) -> io::Result<Self> {
149:         let mut bytes = <Self as GroupEncoding>::Repr::default();
150: 
151:         buffer.read_exact(bytes.as_mut())?;
152: 
153:         Option::from(Self::from_bytes(&bytes))
154:             .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("Invalid BLS12-381 point encoding in proof"))
155:     }
156: }

158: impl<H: TranscriptHash<Input = Vec<u8>>> Hashable<H> for midnight_curves::Fq {
159:     fn to_input(&self) -> Vec<u8> {
160:         self.to_repr().to_vec()
161:     }
162: 
163:     fn to_bytes(&self) -> Vec<u8> {
164:         self.to_repr().to_vec()
165:     }
166:

168:         let mut bytes = <Self as PrimeField>::Repr::default();
169:

171: 
172:         Option::from(Self::from_repr(bytes))
173:             .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("Invalid BLS12-381 scalar encoding in proof"))
174:     }
175: }
176: 
177: impl Sampleable<Blake2bState> for midnight_curves::Fq {
178:     fn sample(hash_output: Vec<u8>) -> Self {
179:         assert!(hash_output.len() <= 64);
180:         assert!(hash_output.len() >= (midnight_curves::Fq::NUM_BITS as usize / 8) + 12);
181:         let mut bytes = [0u8; 64];
182:         bytes[..hash_output.len()].copy_from_slice(&hash_output);
183:         midnight_curves::Fq::from_uniform_bytes(&bytes)
184:     }
185: }
186: 
187: /// WARNING: With a 32-byte hash output and a 255-bit scalar field, the
188: /// statistical distance to uniform is not negligible, some field elements are
189: /// about twice as likely as others. This is acceptable for Fiat-Shamir but
190: /// would not be for applications requiring near-uniform sampling.
191: impl Sampleable<Blake2b256> for midnight_curves::Fq {
192:     fn sample(hash_output: Vec<u8>) -> Self {
193:         assert_eq!(hash_output.len(), 32);
194:         let mut bytes = [0u8; 64];
195:         bytes[..32].copy_from_slice(&hash_output);
196:         midnight_curves::Fq::from_uniform_bytes(&bytes)
197:     }
198: }

=== PRIMARY deliverables/sp04-native-finalizer-source-2026-09-10/point-origin-source-01/sources/midnight-proofs-0.8.2/src/transcript/mod.rs FULL FILE SHA25667df214e7d8695faf1239c7addd6deff2f02ba566c43554a2f2d8f8cf2d37183; FULL ===
1: //! This module contains utilities and traits for dealing with Fiat-Shamir
2: //! transcripts.
3: mod implementors;
4: use std::io::{self, Cursor, Read, Write};
5: 
6: pub use implementors::Blake2b256;
7: 
8: /// Prefix to a prover's message soliciting a challenge
9: const BLAKE2B_PREFIX_CHALLENGE: u8 = 0;
10: 
11: /// Prefix to a prover's message
12: const BLAKE2B_PREFIX_COMMON: u8 = 1;
13: 
14: /// Hash function that can be used for transcript
15: pub trait TranscriptHash: Clone {
16:     /// Input type of the hash function
17:     type Input;
18:     /// Output type of the hash function
19:     type Output;
20: 
21:     /// Initialise the hasher
22:     fn init() -> Self;
23:     /// Absorb an element
24:     fn absorb(&mut self, input: &Self::Input);
25:     /// Squeeze an output
26:     fn squeeze(&mut self) -> Self::Output;
27: }
28: 
29: /// Traits to represent values that can be hashed with a `TranscriptHash`
30: pub trait Hashable<H: TranscriptHash>: Sized {
31:     /// Converts the hashable type to a format that can be hashed with `H`
32:     fn to_input(&self) -> H::Input;
33: 
34:     /// Converts the hashable type to bytes to be added to the transcript.
35:     fn to_bytes(&self) -> Vec<u8>;
36: 
37:     /// Reads bytes from a buffer and returns `Self`.
38:     fn read(buffer: &mut impl Read) -> io::Result<Self>;
39: }
40: 
41: /// Trait to represent values that can be sampled from a `TranscriptHash`
42: pub trait Sampleable<H: TranscriptHash> {
43:     /// Converts `H`'s output to Self
44:     fn sample(hash_output: H::Output) -> Self;
45: }
46: 
47: /// Generic transcript view
48: pub trait Transcript: Clone {
49:     /// Hash function
50:     type Hash: TranscriptHash;
51: 
52:     /// Initialises the transcript
53:     fn init() -> Self;
54: 
55:     /// Parses an existing transcript
56:     fn init_from_bytes(bytes: &[u8]) -> Self;
57: 
58:     /// Squeeze a challenge of type `T`, which only needs to be `Sampleable`
59:     /// with the corresponding hash function.
60:     fn squeeze_challenge<T: Sampleable<Self::Hash>>(&mut self) -> T;
61: 
62:     /// Writing a hashable element `T` to the transcript without writing it to
63:     /// the proof, treating it as a common commitment.
64:     fn common<T: Hashable<Self::Hash>>(&mut self, input: &T) -> io::Result<()>;
65: 
66:     /// Read a hashable element `T` from the prover.
67:     fn read<T: Hashable<Self::Hash>>(&mut self) -> io::Result<T>;
68: 
69:     /// Write a hashable element `T` to the proof and the transcript.
70:     fn write<T: Hashable<Self::Hash>>(&mut self, input: &T) -> io::Result<()>;
71: 
72:     /// Returns the buffer with the proof.
73:     fn finalize(self) -> Vec<u8>;
74: 
75:     /// Checks that the transcript is empty.
76:     /// This is used to make sure a transcript does not contain trailing bytes
77:     /// at the end of a proof verification.
78:     fn assert_empty(&mut self) -> io::Result<()>;
79: }
80: 
81: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
82: /// Transcript used in proofs, parametrised by its hash function.
83: pub struct CircuitTranscript<H: TranscriptHash> {
84:     state: H,
85:     buffer: Cursor<Vec<u8>>,
86: }
87: 
88: impl<H: TranscriptHash> CircuitTranscript<H> {
89:     /// Returns the buffer for non default reading of the buffer (such as for
90:     /// reading an empty proof)
91:     pub fn buffer(&mut self) -> &mut Cursor<Vec<u8>> {
92:         &mut self.buffer
93:     }
94: }
95: 
96: impl<H: TranscriptHash> Transcript for CircuitTranscript<H> {
97:     type Hash = H;
98: 
99:     fn init() -> Self {
100:         Self {
101:             state: H::init(),
102:             buffer: Cursor::new(vec![]),
103:         }
104:     }
105: 
106:     fn init_from_bytes(bytes: &[u8]) -> Self {
107:         Self {
108:             state: H::init(),
109:             buffer: Cursor::new(bytes.to_vec()),
110:         }
111:     }
112: 
113:     fn squeeze_challenge<T: Sampleable<H>>(&mut self) -> T {
114:         T::sample(self.state.squeeze())
115:     }
116: 
117:     fn common<T: Hashable<H>>(&mut self, input: &T) -> io::Result<()> {
118:         self.state.absorb(&input.to_input());
119: 
120:         Ok(())
121:     }
122: 
123:     fn read<T: Hashable<H>>(&mut self) -> io::Result<T> {
124:         let val = T::read(&mut self.buffer)?;
125:         self.common(&val)?;
126: 
127:         Ok(val)
128:     }
129: 
130:     fn write<T: Hashable<H>>(&mut self, input: &T) -> io::Result<()> {
131:         self.common(input)?;
132:         let bytes = input.to_bytes();
133:         self.buffer.write_all(&bytes)
134:     }
135: 
136:     fn finalize(self) -> Vec<u8> {
137:         self.buffer.into_inner()
138:     }
139: 
140:     fn assert_empty(&mut self) -> io::Result<()> {
141:         if self.buffer.get_ref().len() == self.buffer.position() as usize {
142:             return Ok(());
143:         }
144: 
145:         Err(io::Error::new(
146:             io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
147:             "Transcript has unexpected trailing bytes.",
148:         ))
149:     }
150: }
151: 
152: pub(crate) fn read_n<C, T>(transcript: &mut T, n: usize) -> io::Result<Vec<C>>
153: where
154:     T: Transcript,
155:     C: Hashable<T::Hash>,
156: {
157:     (0..n).map(|_| transcript.read()).collect()
158: }

=== PRIMARY deliverables/sp04-native-finalizer-source-2026-09-10/point-origin-source-01/sources/native-695351/aggregation/src/ivc/circuit.rs FULL FILE SHA2560d3231519552a051f04de9d810a1bda3d0b731323adb84f3aef9afa8ac4c3f3f; FULL ===
1: //! IVC circuit definition and [`Relation`] implementation.
2: //!
3: //! The circuit proves a single IVC step: given a previous state, a transition
4: //! witness and a proof that the previous state was itself valid, it verifies
5: //! the prior proof, applies the transition, and produces a new accumulator
6: //! that attests to the entire chain up to the new state.
7: //!
8: //! The genesis case is handled specially: when the previous state is genesis,
9: //! there is no meaningful prior proof to verify, so the circuit substitutes a
10: //! default accumulator that satisfies the verification invariant.
11: 
12: use midnight_circuits::{
13:     instructions::{BinaryInstructions, PublicInputInstructions},
14:     types::Instantiable,
15:     verifier::{
16:         Accumulator, AssignedAccumulator, AssignedKZGMultiCommitment, AssignedVk, InCircuitKZG,
17:     },
18: };
19: use midnight_proofs::{
20:     circuit::{Layouter, Value},
21:     plonk::ConstraintSystem,
22:     poly::{EvaluationDomain, PolynomialLabel},
23: };
24: use midnight_zk_stdlib::{Relation, ZkStdLib, ZkStdLibArch};
25: 
26: use super::{F, Ivc, IvcError, S};
27: 
28: /// The public instance (statement) of an IVC proof.
29: ///
30: /// Contains:
31: /// - a commitment to the verifying key (of the IVC circuit itself),
32: /// - the current state (after the latest transition),
33: /// - the accumulator (that summarises all prior steps).
34: ///
35: /// **Important:** the `vk_repr` field must **not** be trusted as-is. The
36: /// verifier must compare it against the canonical `vk_repr` obtained by
37: /// running [`setup`](super::setup()).
38: /// See [`IvcVerifier::verify`](super::IvcVerifier::verify) for details.
39: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
40: pub struct IvcInstance<T: Ivc> {
41:     pub(crate) vk_repr: F,
42:     pub(crate) state: T::State,
43:     pub(crate) acc: Accumulator<S>,
44: }
45: 
46: impl<T: Ivc> IvcInstance<T> {
47:     /// Returns the current state.
48:     pub fn state(&self) -> &T::State {
49:         &self.state
50:     }
51: }
52: 
53: /// The private witness for a single IVC step.
54: ///
55: /// Contains:
56: /// - a previous state (input to the transition),
57: /// - a previous accumulator (summarises all steps up to the previous state),
58: /// - a proof asserting the validity of the previous step (if not genesis),
59: /// - a transition witness (input that drives the state change).
60: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
61: pub struct IvcWitness<T: Ivc> {
62:     pub(crate) prev_state: T::State,
63:     pub(crate) prev_acc: Accumulator<S>,
64:     pub(crate) prev_proof: Vec<u8>,
65:     pub(crate) transition_witness: T::Witness,
66: }
67: 
68: /// The IVC circuit, parameterized by a transition function `T`.
69: ///
70: /// Implements the [`Relation`] of the IVC logic. Namely, that for a given
71: /// [`IvcInstance`] `(vk_repr, state, acc)` there exists an [`IvcWitness`]
72: /// `(prev_state, prev_acc, prev_proof, transition_witness)` such that:
73: ///
74: /// 1. `state` is the result of applying the transition function to `prev_state`
75: ///    with `transition_witness`,
76: /// 2. `prev_state` is genesis OR `prev_proof` is a valid proof (under
77: ///    `vk_repr`) for the instance `(vk_repr, prev_state, prev_acc)`, attesting
78: ///    that `prev_state` was itself reached legitimately,
79: /// 3. `acc` is the accumulation of `prev_acc` with the accumulator resulting
80: ///    from verifying `prev_proof`.
81: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
82: pub struct IvcCircuit<T: Ivc> {
83:     domain: EvaluationDomain<F>,
84:     cs: ConstraintSystem<F>,
85:     ctx: T::Context,
86: }
87: 
88: impl<T: Ivc> IvcCircuit<T> {
89:     /// Creates a new IVC circuit.
90:     ///
91:     /// The `ctx` contains metadata that parametrizes the IVC computation
92:     /// (transition function). See [`IvcContext`](super::IvcContext).
93:     pub fn new(domain: EvaluationDomain<F>, cs: ConstraintSystem<F>, ctx: T::Context) -> Self {
94:         IvcCircuit { domain, cs, ctx }
95:     }
96: 
97:     /// Returns a reference to the context.
98:     pub fn ctx(&self) -> &T::Context {
99:         &self.ctx
100:     }
101: 
102:     /// The [ZkStdLibArch] for the IVC circuit, combining the transition's
103:     /// requirements with the verifier's (bls12_381 and poseidon).
104:     pub fn arch() -> ZkStdLibArch {
105:         let mut arch = T::arch();
106:         arch.bls12_381 = true;
107:         arch.poseidon = true;
108:         arch
109:     }
110: 
111:     /// Returns the constraint-system degree of the IVC circuit.
112:     pub fn cs_degree() -> usize {
113:         midnight_zk_stdlib::cs_degree(Self::arch())
114:     }
115: }
116: 
117: impl<T: Ivc> Relation for IvcCircuit<T> {
118:     type Instance = IvcInstance<T>;
119: 
120:     type Witness = IvcWitness<T>;
121: 
122:     type Error = IvcError;
123: 
124:     fn used_chips(&self) -> ZkStdLibArch {
125:         Self::arch()
126:     }
127: 
128:     fn format_instance(instance: &Self::Instance) -> Result<Vec<F>, IvcError> {
129:         Ok([
130:             vec![instance.vk_repr],
131:             T::format_public_input(&instance.state),
132:             AssignedAccumulator::<S>::as_public_input(&instance.acc),
133:         ]
134:         .concat())
135:     }
136: 
137:     fn circuit(
138:         &self,
139:         std_lib: &ZkStdLib,
140:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
141:         instance: Value<Self::Instance>,
142:         witness: Value<Self::Witness>,
143:     ) -> Result<(), IvcError> {
144:         let verifier_gadget = std_lib.verifier();
145:         let ivc_gadget = T::new(std_lib.clone(), &self.ctx);
146: 
147:         let assigned_self_vk: AssignedVk<S, InCircuitKZG<S>> = verifier_gadget
148:             .assign_vk_as_public_input(
149:                 layouter,
150:                 &self.domain,
151:                 &self.cs,
152:                 instance.as_ref().map(|x| x.vk_repr),
153:             )?;
154: 
155:         let prev_state_val = witness.as_ref().map(|w| w.prev_state.clone());
156:         let prev_state = ivc_gadget.assign(layouter, prev_state_val)?;
157: 
158:         let next_state = ivc_gadget.circuit_transition(
159:             layouter,
160:             &prev_state,
161:             witness.as_ref().map(|w| w.transition_witness.clone()),
162:         )?;
163:         ivc_gadget.constrain_as_public_input(layouter, &next_state)?;
164: 
165:         let fixed_base_labels = midnight_circuits::verifier::fixed_base_labels::<S>(
166:             self.cs.num_fixed_columns() + self.cs.num_selectors(),
167:             self.cs.permutation().columns.len(),
168:         );
169: 
170:         let prev_acc_value = witness.as_ref().map(|w| w.prev_acc.clone());
171:         let prev_acc = verifier_gadget.assign_collapsed_accumulator(
172:             layouter,
173:             &fixed_base_labels,
174:             prev_acc_value,
175:         )?;
176: 
177:         let prev_proof_pi = [
178:             verifier_gadget.as_public_input(layouter, &assigned_self_vk)?,
179:             ivc_gadget.as_public_input(layouter, &prev_state)?,
180:             verifier_gadget.as_public_input(layouter, &prev_acc)?,
181:         ]
182:         .concat();
183: 
184:         let instance_com = AssignedKZGMultiCommitment::commitment_to_zero(
185:             layouter,
186:             std_lib.bls12_381(),
187:             PolynomialLabel::CommittedInstance(0),
188:         )?;
189: 
190:         // Verify a witnessed proof that ensures the validity of `prev_state`.
191:         // The proof is valid iff `prev_proof_acc` satisfies the invariant.
192:         let mut prev_proof_acc = verifier_gadget.prepare(
193:             layouter,
194:             &assigned_self_vk,
195:             &[instance_com],
196:             &[&prev_proof_pi],
197:             witness.map(|w| w.prev_proof),
198:         )?;
199: 
200:         // If `prev_state` is genesis, the provided accumulator is discarded/multiplied
201:         // by 0 so that it trivially satisfies the invariant.
202: 
203:         let is_not_genesis = {
204:             let b = ivc_gadget.circuit_is_genesis(std_lib, layouter, &self.ctx, &prev_state)?;
205:             std_lib.not(layouter, &b)?
206:         };
207:         AssignedAccumulator::scale_by_bit(
208:             layouter,
209:             std_lib.bls12_381().scalar_field_chip(),
210:             &is_not_genesis,
211:             &mut prev_proof_acc,
212:         )?;
213: 
214:         let mut next_acc = verifier_gadget.accumulate(layouter, &[prev_proof_acc, prev_acc])?;
215: 
216:         next_acc.collapse(
217:             layouter,
218:             std_lib.bls12_381(),
219:             std_lib.bls12_381().scalar_field_chip(),
220:         )?;
221: 
222:         verifier_gadget.constrain_as_public_input(layouter, &next_acc)?;
223:         Ok(())
224:     }
225: 
226:     fn write_relation<W: std::io::Write>(&self, writer: &mut W) -> std::io::Result<()> {
227:         writer.write_all(&self.domain.k().to_le_bytes())?;
228:         T::write_context(&self.ctx, writer)
229:     }
230: 
231:     fn read_relation<R: std::io::Read>(reader: &mut R) -> std::io::Result<Self> {
232:         let mut k_bytes = [0u8; 4];
233:         reader.read_exact(&mut k_bytes)?;
234:         let k = u32::from_le_bytes(k_bytes);
235: 
236:         let ctx = T::read_context(reader)?;
237: 
238:         let mut cs = ConstraintSystem::default();
239:         ZkStdLib::configure(&mut cs, (Self::arch(), (k - 1) as u8));
240:         let domain = EvaluationDomain::new(cs.degree() as u32, k);
241: 
242:         Ok(IvcCircuit { domain, cs, ctx })
243:     }
244: }

=== PRIMARY deliverables/sp04-native-finalizer-source-2026-09-10/point-origin-source-01/sources/native-695351/aggregation/src/ivc/verifier.rs FULL FILE SHA2568a8034299533228612cca9054427cc086d51deb7c2d6bdeb17748b1ec9284bd5; FULL ===
1: //! IVC verifier.
2: //!
3: //! Given an [`IvcInstance`] and the corresponding proof bytes, the
4: //! [`IvcVerifier`] checks that a valid chain of transitions from genesis
5: //! to the claimed state exists. Verification is constant-time regardless
6: //! of how many steps the prover has performed.
7: 
8: use midnight_circuits::{hash::poseidon::PoseidonState, verifier::Accumulator};
9: use midnight_proofs::{
10:     plonk::{self},
11:     poly::{
12:         PolynomialLabel,
13:         kzg::{KZGCommitmentScheme, commitment::KZGMultiCommitment, params::ParamsVerifierKZG},
14:     },
15:     transcript::{CircuitTranscript, Transcript},
16: };
17: use midnight_zk_stdlib::{MidnightVK, Relation};
18: 
19: use super::{E, F, Ivc, IvcCircuit, IvcError, IvcInstance, S};
20: 
21: /// Lightweight IVC verifier carrying:
22: /// - the application context (for the decider check),
23: /// - the self-verifying key,
24: /// - the SRS verifier parameters (for the pairing check).
25: ///
26: /// Returned by [`super::setup()`].
27: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
28: pub struct IvcVerifier<T: Ivc> {
29:     pub(crate) ctx: T::Context,
30:     pub(crate) vk: MidnightVK,
31:     pub(crate) params_verifier: ParamsVerifierKZG<E>,
32: }
33: 
34: impl<T: Ivc> IvcVerifier<T> {
35:     /// Verifies an IVC proof against the given instance.
36:     ///
37:     /// Checks that the proof is valid with respect to the given instance by:
38:     /// 1. Preparing the proof to obtain a proof accumulator.
39:     /// 2. Accumulating it with the instance's accumulator.
40:     /// 3. Checking the pairing invariant on the result.
41:     /// 4. Running the application-level [`decider`](super::IvcState::decider)
42:     ///    on the instance state.
43:     ///
44:     /// This method checks that `instance.vk_repr` matches the canonical
45:     /// verifying key held by this verifier (derived from
46:     /// [`setup`](super::setup())). Without this check, a proof generated
47:     /// under a different (potentially malicious) circuit could pass
48:     /// verification.
49:     pub fn verify(&self, instance: &IvcInstance<T>, proof: &[u8]) -> Result<(), IvcError> {
50:         // Reject proofs whose instance claims a different verifying key.
51:         if instance.vk_repr != self.vk.vk().transcript_repr() {
52:             return Err(IvcError::VkMismatch);
53:         }
54: 
55:         if !T::decider(&self.ctx, &instance.state) {
56:             return Err(IvcError::DeciderFailed);
57:         }
58: 
59:         let fixed_bases = midnight_circuits::verifier::fixed_bases::<S>(self.vk.vk());
60: 
61:         let pi =
62:             IvcCircuit::<T>::format_instance(instance).map_err(|_| IvcError::InvalidInstance)?;
63: 
64:         let mut transcript = CircuitTranscript::<PoseidonState<F>>::init_from_bytes(proof);
65:         let dual_msm =
66:             plonk::prepare::<F, KZGCommitmentScheme<E>, CircuitTranscript<PoseidonState<F>>>(
67:                 self.vk.vk(),
68:                 &[KZGMultiCommitment::commitment_to_zero(
69:                     PolynomialLabel::CommittedInstance(0),
70:                 )],
71:                 &[&pi],
72:                 &mut transcript,
73:             )
74:             .map_err(|_| IvcError::InvalidProof)?;
75: 
76:         transcript.assert_empty().map_err(|_| IvcError::TranscriptNotEmpty)?;
77: 
78:         let proof_acc = Accumulator::from_dual_msm(dual_msm, &fixed_bases);
79: 
80:         // Verify that both `proof_acc` and `instance.acc` satisfy the pairing
81:         // invariant, with a single pairing, by accumulating them first.
82:         let final_acc = Accumulator::<S>::accumulate(&[proof_acc, instance.acc.clone()]);
83:         if !final_acc.check(&self.params_verifier, &fixed_bases) {
84:             return Err(IvcError::InvalidProof);
85:         };
86: 
87:         Ok(())
88:     }
89: }

93: /// Number of columns required by the custom gates of this chip.
94: pub fn nb_foreign_ecc_chip_columns<F, C, B, S>() -> usize
95: where
96:     F: CircuitField,
97:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
98:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
99: {
100:     // The scalar field is treated as a gadget. Here we only account for the columns
101:     // that this chip requires for its own custom gates.
102:     // The 2 in `2 + |moduli|` corresponds to `u_col` + `cond_col`.
103:     // The outer `+ 1` corresponds to the advice column for the index of
104:     // `multi_select`.
105:     B::NB_LIMBS as usize + max(B::NB_LIMBS as usize, 2 + B::moduli().len()) + 1
106: }
107: 
108: /// Shared MSM randomness for the windowed double-and-add algorithm.
109: ///
110: /// In the windowed MSM, a random point `r` is used to randomize the
111: /// double-and-add accumulator, ensuring that intermediate values do not
112: /// collide with table entries (in particular, avoiding equal x-coordinates
113: /// or identity points). This allows the use of incomplete addition
114: /// throughout the loop, which is cheaper than complete addition.
115: ///
116: /// The value of `r` can be chosen by the prover (who will choose it
117: /// uniformly at random for statistical completeness) and this is not a
118: /// problem for soundness.
119: ///
120: /// By sharing `r` (and thus `α = (2^WS - 1) * r`) across all MSM calls on
121: /// the same chip, precomputed tables for the same base point can be reused
122: /// across MSM invocations. Sharing the randomness across all MSMs hinders
123: /// completeness, but only by a polynomial factor: we still get statistical
124: /// completeness (i.e. completeness with overwhelming probability over the
125: /// choice of `r`).
126: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
127: struct MsmRandomness<F, C, B>
128: where
129:     F: CircuitField,
130:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
131:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
132: {
133:     r: AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>,
134:     neg_alpha: AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>,
135: }
136: 
137: /// Map from window size to the corresponding [`MsmRandomness`], lazily
138: /// populated on first MSM call for each window size.
139: type MsmRandomnessMap<F, C, B> = HashMap<usize, MsmRandomness<F, C, B>>;
140: 
141: /// ['ECChip'] to perform foreign Weierstrass EC operations.
142: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
143: pub struct ForeignWeierstrassEccChip<F, C, B, S, N>
144: where
145:     F: CircuitField,
146:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
147:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
148:     S: ScalarFieldInstructions<F>,
149:     S::Scalar: InnerValue<Element = C::ScalarField>,
150:     N: NativeInstructions<F>,
151: {
152:     config: ForeignWeierstrassEccConfig<C>,
153:     native_gadget: N,
154:     base_field_chip: FieldChip<F, C::Base, B, N>,
155:     scalar_field_chip: S,
156:     // A table tag counter to make sure all dynamic lookup tables are independent.
157:     // This counter is always increased after loading a new table.
158:     // It will never overflow unless you include more than 2^64 tables, will you?
159:     // Even in that case, we would get a compile-time error.
160:     tag_cnt: Rc<RefCell<u64>>,
161:     // Shared random point `r` for the windowed MSM double-and-add algorithm.
162:     // The value of `r` can be chosen by the prover (who will choose it uniformly
163:     // at random for statistical completeness) and this is not a problem for
164:     // soundness.
165:     // Lazily initialized on first MSM call. By sharing `r` across MSM calls,
166:     // computations depending on it e.g. α can be cached and reused.
167:     // Importantly, such cache is keyed by window size.
168:     msm_randomness: Rc<RefCell<MsmRandomnessMap<F, C, B>>>,
169:     // A random point used in windowed_msm (to shift the initial accumulator) so that the
170:     // double-and-add loop can internally use incomplete addition. Sampled once at chip
171:     // construction time.
172:     random_point: C::CryptographicGroup,
173: }
174: 
175: /// Type for foreign EC points.
176: /// The identity is represented with field `is_id`, whose value is `1` iff the
177: /// point is the identity. If `is_id` is set, the values of `x` and `y` are
178: /// irrelevant and can be anything.
179: /// x2 is a ModInt encoding the square of x, which is computed once and stored.
180: /// This value is used by our custom gates, it allows us to implement all
181: /// custom gates without degree-3 terms, so we can reuse the same auxiliary
182: /// moduli as for ModArith.mul.
183: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
184: #[must_use]
185: pub struct AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>
186: where
187:     F: CircuitField,
188:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
189:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
190: {
191:     point: Value<C::CryptographicGroup>,
192:     is_id: AssignedBit<F>,
193:     x: AssignedField<F, C::Base, B>,
194:     y: AssignedField<F, C::Base, B>,
195: }
196: 
197: impl<F, C, B> PartialEq for AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>
198: where
199:     F: CircuitField,
200:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
201:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
202: {
203:     fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
204:         self.is_id == other.is_id && self.x == other.x && self.y == other.y
205:     }
206: }
207: 
208: impl<F, C, B> Eq for AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>
209: where
210:     F: CircuitField,
211:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
212:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
213: {
214: }
215: 
216: impl<F, C, B> Hash for AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>
217: where
218:     F: CircuitField,
219:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
220:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
221: {
222:     fn hash<H: Hasher>(&self, state: &mut H) {
223:         self.is_id.hash(state);
224:         self.x.hash(state);
225:         self.y.hash(state);
226:     }
227: }
228: 
229: impl<F, C, B> Instantiable<F> for AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>
230: where
231:     F: CircuitField,
232:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
233:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
234: {
235:     fn as_public_input(p: &C::CryptographicGroup) -> Vec<F> {
236:         let (x, y) = (*p).into().coordinates().unwrap_or((C::Base::ZERO, C::Base::ZERO));
237:         let mut pis = [
238:             AssignedField::<F, C::Base, B>::as_public_input(&x).as_slice(),
239:             AssignedField::<F, C::Base, B>::as_public_input(&y).as_slice(),
240:         ]
241:         .concat();
242: 
243:         let is_id: bool = p.is_identity().into();
244:         pis.push(F::from(is_id as u64));
245: 
246:         pis
247:     }
248: 
249:     #[cfg(any(test, feature = "testing"))]
250:     fn from_public_input(fields: &[F]) -> Option<C::CryptographicGroup> {
251:         if *fields.last()? == F::ONE {
252:             return Some(C::CryptographicGroup::identity());
253:         }
254:         let nb_limbs_per_batch = (F::CAPACITY / B::LOG2_BASE) as usize;
255:         let nb_pi_per_coord = B::NB_LIMBS.div_ceil(nb_limbs_per_batch as u32) as usize;
256:         if fields.len() != 2 * nb_pi_per_coord + 1 {
257:             return None;
258:         }
259:         let x = AssignedField::<F, C::Base, B>::from_public_input(&fields[..nb_pi_per_coord])?;
260:         let y = AssignedField::<F, C::Base, B>::from_public_input(
261:             &fields[nb_pi_per_coord..nb_pi_per_coord * 2],
262:         )?;
263:         C::from_xy(x, y).map(|p| p.into_subgroup())
264:     }
265: }
266: 
267: impl<F, C, B> InnerValue for AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>
268: where
269:     F: CircuitField,
270:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
271:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
272: {
273:     type Element = C::CryptographicGroup;
274: 
275:     fn value(&self) -> Value<Self::Element> {
276:         self.point
277:     }
278: }

280: impl<F, C, B> InnerConstants for AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>
281: where
282:     F: CircuitField,
283:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
284:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
285: {
286:     fn inner_zero() -> C::CryptographicGroup {
287:         C::CryptographicGroup::identity()
288:     }

291:         C::CryptographicGroup::generator()
292:     }

326: impl<F, C, B, S, N> AssignmentInstructions<F, AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>>
327:     for ForeignWeierstrassEccChip<F, C, B, S, N>
328: where
329:     F: CircuitField,
330:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
331:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
332:     S: ScalarFieldInstructions<F>,
333:     S::Scalar: InnerValue<Element = C::ScalarField>,
334:     N: NativeInstructions<F>,
335: {
336:     /// Assigns a private curve point and enforces in-circuit that it is on the
337:     /// curve and lies in the prime-order subgroup.
338:     ///
339:     /// If you deliberately need to skip the subgroup check, use
340:     /// [`EccInstructions::assign_without_subgroup_check`] instead.
341:     fn assign(
342:         &self,
343:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
344:         value: Value<C::CryptographicGroup>,
345:     ) -> Result<AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>, Error> {
346:         if C::COFACTOR > 1 {
347:             let cofactor = C::ScalarField::from_u128(C::COFACTOR);
348:             // Exhibit a cofactor-root Q and assert h * Q = p in-circuit.
349:             // This guarantees that p ∈ C::CryptographicGroup = h · E(Fp).
350:             let cofactor_root = self.assign_without_subgroup_check(
351:                 layouter,
352:                 value.map(|point| point * cofactor.invert().unwrap()),
353:             )?;
354:             self.mul_by_constant(layouter, cofactor, &cofactor_root)
355:         } else {
356:             self.assign_without_subgroup_check(layouter, value)
357:         }
358:     }
359: 
360:     fn assign_fixed(
361:         &self,
362:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
363:         constant: C::CryptographicGroup,
364:     ) -> Result<AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>, Error> {
365:         let (xv, yv, is_id_value) = if C::CryptographicGroup::is_identity(&constant).into() {
366:             (C::Base::ZERO, C::Base::ZERO, true)
367:         } else {
368:             let coordinates = constant
369:                 .into()
370:                 .coordinates()
371:                 .expect("assign_point_unchecked: invalid point given");
372:             (coordinates.0, coordinates.1, false)
373:         };
374:         let is_id = self.native_gadget.assign_fixed(layouter, is_id_value)?;
375:         let x = self.base_field_chip().assign_fixed(layouter, xv)?;
376:         let y = self.base_field_chip().assign_fixed(layouter, yv)?;
377:         let p = AssignedForeignPoint::<F, C, B> {
378:             point: Value::known(constant),
379:             is_id,
380:             x,
381:             y,
382:         };
383:         Ok(p)
384:     }
385: }

387: impl<F, C, B, S, N> PublicInputInstructions<F, AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>>
388:     for ForeignWeierstrassEccChip<F, C, B, S, N>
389: where
390:     F: CircuitField,
391:     C: WeierstrassCurve,
392:     B: FieldEmulationParams<F, C::Base>,
393:     S: ScalarFieldInstructions<F>,
394:     S::Scalar: InnerValue<Element = C::ScalarField>,
395:     N: NativeInstructions<F> + PublicInputInstructions<F, AssignedBit<F>>,
396: {
397:     fn as_public_input(
398:         &self,
399:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
400:         p: &AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>,
401:     ) -> Result<Vec<AssignedNative<F>>, Error> {
402:         let mut pis = [
403:             self.base_field_chip.as_public_input(layouter, &p.x)?.as_slice(),
404:             self.base_field_chip.as_public_input(layouter, &p.y)?.as_slice(),
405:         ]
406:         .concat();
407: 
408:         pis.push(p.is_id.clone().into());
409: 
410:         Ok(pis)
411:     }
412: 
413:     fn constrain_as_public_input(
414:         &self,
415:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
416:         assigned: &AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>,
417:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
418:         self.as_public_input(layouter, assigned)?
419:             .iter()
420:             .try_for_each(|c| self.native_gadget.constrain_as_public_input(layouter, c))
421:     }
422: 
423:     fn assign_as_public_input(
424:         &self,
425:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
426:         value: Value<C::CryptographicGroup>,
427:     ) -> Result<AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>, Error> {
428:         // Given our optimized way of constraining a point as public input, we
429:         // cannot optimize the direct assignment as PI. We just compose `assign`
430:         // with `constrain_as_public_input`.
431:         let point = self.assign_without_subgroup_check(layouter, value)?;
432:         self.constrain_as_public_input(layouter, &point)?;
433:         Ok(point)
434:     }
435: }

932:             .value()

934:             .map(|(x, y)| C::from_xy(x, y).unwrap_or(C::identity()).into_subgroup());

986:     /// does.

988:         config: &ForeignWeierstrassEccConfig<C>,
989:         native_gadget: &N,

998:             config: config.clone(),

1000:             base_field_chip,

1002:             tag_cnt: Rc::new(RefCell::new(1)),
1003:             msm_randomness: Rc::new(RefCell::new(HashMap::new())),
1004:             random_point,
1005:         }
1006:     }
1007:

1010:     fn completeness_error_if<V>(value: &Value<V>, f: impl FnOnce(&V) -> bool) -> Result<(), Error> {
1011:         value.error_if_known_and(f).map_err(|_| Error::CompletenessFailure)
1012:     }

1024:     /// Configures the foreign ECC chip
1025:     pub fn configure(
1026:         meta: &mut ConstraintSystem<F>,
1027:         base_field_config: &FieldChipConfig,
1028:         advice_columns: &[Column<Advice>],
1029:         nb_parallel_range_checks: usize,
1030:         max_bit_len: u32,
1031:     ) -> ForeignWeierstrassEccConfig<C> {
1032:         // Assert that there is room for the cond_col in the existing columns of the
1033:         // field_chip configurations.
1034:         let cond_col_idx = base_field_config.x_cols.len() + base_field_config.v_cols.len() + 1;
1035:         assert!(advice_columns.len() > cond_col_idx);
1036:         let cond_col = advice_columns[cond_col_idx];
1037:         meta.enable_equality(cond_col);
1038:

1040:             meta,
1041:             base_field_config,
1042:             &cond_col,
1043:             nb_parallel_range_checks,
1044:             max_bit_len,
1045:         );

1048:             meta,
1049:             base_field_config,
1050:             &cond_col,
1051:             nb_parallel_range_checks,
1052:             max_bit_len,
1053:         );
1054: 
1055:         let tangent_config = TangentConfig::<C>::configure::<F, B>(
1056:             meta,
1057:             base_field_config,
1058:             &cond_col,
1059:             nb_parallel_range_checks,
1060:             max_bit_len,
1061:         );
1062: 
1063:         let lambda_squared_config = LambdaSquaredConfig::<C>::configure::<F, B>(
1064:             meta,
1065:             base_field_config,
1066:             &cond_col,
1067:             nb_parallel_range_checks,
1068:             max_bit_len,
1069:         );
1070: 
1071:         let (q_multi_select, idx_col_multi_select, tag_col_multi_select) =
1072:             configure_multi_select_lookup(meta, advice_columns, base_field_config);
1073: 
1074:         ForeignWeierstrassEccConfig {
1075:             base_field_config: base_field_config.clone(),
1076:             on_curve_config,
1077:             slope_config,
1078:             tangent_config,
1079:             lambda_squared_config,
1080:             q_multi_select,
1081:             idx_col_multi_select,

1083:         }
1084:     }

1086:     /// Converts a curve point in C : WeierstrassCurve to AssignedForeignPoint.
1087:     /// Used for loading possibly secret points into the circuit.
1088:     /// The point is not asserted (with constraints) to be on the curve.
1089:     /// The point may be the identity.
1090:     fn assign_point_unchecked(
1091:         &self,
1092:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
1093:         p: Value<C::CryptographicGroup>,
1094:     ) -> Result<AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>, Error> {
1095:         let values = p.map(|p| {
1096:             if C::CryptographicGroup::is_identity(&p).into() {
1097:                 (C::Base::ZERO, C::Base::ZERO, true)
1098:             } else {
1099:                 let coordinates =
1100:                     p.into().coordinates().expect("assign_point_unchecked: invalid point given");
1101:                 (coordinates.0, coordinates.1, false)
1102:             }
1103:         });
1104:         let x = self.base_field_chip().assign(layouter, values.map(|v| v.0))?;
1105:         let y = self.base_field_chip().assign(layouter, values.map(|v| v.1))?;
1106:         let is_id = self.native_gadget.assign(layouter, values.map(|v| v.2))?;
1107:         let p = AssignedForeignPoint::<F, C, B> {
1108:             point: p,
1109:             is_id,
1110:             x,
1111:             y,
1112:         };
1113:         Ok(p)
1114:     }
1115: 
1116:     /// Given `p` and `q`, returns `p + q`.
1117:     ///
1118:     /// This function is incomplete because it is not designed to deal with
1119:     /// the cases where `p` or `q` are the identity nor cases where `p = ±q`
1120:     /// (i.e. `p.x = q.x`).
1121:     /// Consequently, this function will never return the identity point.
1122:     ///
1123:     /// # Preconditions
1124:     ///
1125:     /// - `p != id`
1126:     /// - `q != id`
1127:     /// - `p != q`
1128:     ///
1129:     /// It is the responsibility of the caller to guarantee that all
1130:     /// preconditions are met, this function *does not* necessarily become
1131:     /// unsatisfiable if they are violated.
1132:     ///
1133:     /// # Unsatisfiable Circuit
1134:     ///
1135:     /// If `p = -q`.
1136:     fn incomplete_add(
1137:         &self,
1138:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<F>,
1139:         p: &AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>,
1140:         q: &AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>,
1141:     ) -> Result<AssignedForeignPoint<F, C, B>, Error> {
1142:         let r_curve = p.value().zip(q.value()).map(|(p, q)| p + q);
1143:         let r = self.assign_point_unchecked(layouter, r_curve)?;
1144: 
1145:         // Assert that r is not the identity.
1146:         self.native_gadget.assert_equal(layouter, &p.is_id, &r.is_id)?;
1147: 
1148:         // The preconditions of [incomplete_add] (and the previous assertion
1149:         // that r != id) guarantee that the following call satisfies all the
1150:         // preconditions of [assert_add].

33: /// Number of times the linear part of the partial rounds is skipped in the
34: /// Poseidon cpu implemetation (0 is the default implementation without skips at
35: /// all).
36: pub(crate) const NB_SKIPS_CPU: usize = 2;
37: 
38: /// Off-circuit Poseidon state.
39: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
40: pub struct PoseidonState<F: PoseidonField> {
41:     pre_computed: PreComputedRoundCPU<F>,
42:     register: [F; WIDTH],
43:     queue: Vec<F>,
44:     squeeze_position: usize,
45:     input_len: Option<usize>,
46: }
47: 
48: // Applies the MDS matrix to a state and adds the round constants. All arguments
49: // have length `WIDTH`. To save the addition cost, the implementation is done by
50: // mutating the `constants` slice, and eventually copying it into `state`.
51: fn linear_layer<F: PoseidonField>(state: &mut [F], constants: &mut [F]) {
52:     #[allow(clippy::needless_range_loop)]
53:     for i in 0..WIDTH {
54:         for j in 0..WIDTH {
55:             constants[i] += F::MDS[i][j] * state[j];
56:         }
57:     }
58:     state.copy_from_slice(constants);
59: }
60: 
61: /// A cpu version of the full round of Poseidon's permutation. Operates by
62: /// mutating the `state` argument (length `WIDTH`).
63: pub(crate) fn full_round_cpu<F: PoseidonField>(round_index: usize, state: &mut [F]) {
64:     state.iter_mut().for_each(|x| *x = x.square().square() * *x);
65:     let mut new_state = if round_index == NB_FULL_ROUNDS + NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS - 1 {
66:         [F::ZERO; WIDTH]
67:     } else {
68:         F::ROUND_CONSTANTS[round_index + 1]
69:     };
70:     linear_layer(state, &mut new_state);
71: }
72: 
73: // A cpu version of Poseidon with `1 + NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT` partial rounds.
74: fn partial_round_cpu<F: PoseidonField>(
75:     pre_computed: &PreComputedRoundCPU<F>,
76:     round_batch_index: usize,
77:     state: &mut [F], // Length `WIDTH`.
78: ) {
79:     pre_computed
80:         .partial_round_id
81:         .eval::<NB_SKIPS_CPU>(&pre_computed.round_constants[round_batch_index], state);
82: }
83: 
84: /// A cpu version of Poseidon with `1 + NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT` partial rounds. Also
85: /// returns the values of the last column of the skipped rows
86: /// (`NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT` elements) as needed to fill the circuit's rows.
87: pub(crate) fn partial_round_cpu_for_circuits<F: PoseidonField>(
88:     pre_computed: &PreComputedRoundCircuit<F>,
89:     round_batch_index: usize,
90:     state: &mut [F], // Length `WIDTH`.
91: ) -> [F; NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT] {
92:     pre_computed
93:         .partial_round_id
94:         .eval::<NB_SKIPS_CIRCUIT>(&pre_computed.round_constants[round_batch_index], state)
95: }
96: 
97: // Alternative partial round version, without any skips.
98: fn partial_round_cpu_raw<F: PoseidonField>(round: usize, state: &mut [F]) {
99:     state[WIDTH - 1] *= state[WIDTH - 1].square().square();
100:     let mut new_state = F::ROUND_CONSTANTS[round + 1];
101:     linear_layer(state, &mut new_state)
102: }
103: 
104: /// A cpu version of the full Poseidon's permutation with partial-round skips.
105: pub fn permutation_cpu<F: PoseidonField>(pre_computed: &PreComputedRoundCPU<F>, state: &mut [F]) {
106:     let nb_skips = pre_computed.partial_round_id.nb_skips;
107:     let nb_main_partial_rounds = NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS / (1 + nb_skips);
108:     let remainder_partial_rounds = NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS % (1 + nb_skips);
109: 
110:     for (x, k0) in state.iter_mut().zip(F::ROUND_CONSTANTS[0]) {
111:         *x += k0;
112:     }
113:     (0..NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2).for_each(|round_index| full_round_cpu(round_index, state));
114:     (0..nb_main_partial_rounds)
115:         .for_each(|round_batch_index| partial_round_cpu(pre_computed, round_batch_index, state));
116:     (NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2 + NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS - remainder_partial_rounds..)
117:         .take(remainder_partial_rounds)
118:         .for_each(|round_index| partial_round_cpu_raw(round_index, state));
119:     (NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2 + NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS..)
120:         .take(NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2)
121:         .for_each(|round_index| {
122:             full_round_cpu(round_index, state);
123:         })
124: }
125: 
126: // A cpu implementation of the sponge operations, building on the Poseidon's
127: // permutation.
128: impl<F: PoseidonField> SpongeCPU<F, F> for PoseidonChip<F> {
129:     type StateCPU = PoseidonState<F>;
130: 
131:     fn init(input_len: Option<usize>) -> Self::StateCPU {
132:         let mut register = [F::ZERO; WIDTH];
133:         register[RATE] = F::from_u128(input_len.map(|l| l as u128).unwrap_or(1 << 64));
134:         let pre_computed = PreComputedRoundCPU::init();
135:         PoseidonState {
136:             pre_computed,
137:             register,
138:             queue: Vec::new(),
139:             squeeze_position: 0,
140:             input_len,
141:         }
142:     }
143: 
144:     fn absorb(state: &mut Self::StateCPU, inputs: &[F]) {
145:         state.queue.extend(inputs);
146:         state.squeeze_position = 0;
147:     }
148: 
149:     fn squeeze(state: &mut Self::StateCPU) -> F {
150:         if state.squeeze_position > 0 {
151:             // If `input_len` was specified, we only allow 1 squeeze.
152:             if state.input_len.is_some() {
153:                 panic!("Attempting to squeeze multiple times a fixed-size Poseidon sponge (CPU).")
154:             };
155:             debug_assert!(state.queue.is_empty());
156:             let output = state.register[state.squeeze_position % RATE];
157:             state.squeeze_position = (state.squeeze_position + 1) % RATE;
158:             return output;
159:         }
160: 
161:         match state.input_len {
162:             None => {
163:                 let padding = F::from(state.queue.len() as u64);
164:                 state.queue.push(padding);
165:             }
166:             Some(len) => {
167:                 if state.queue.len() != len {
168:                     panic!(
169:                         "Inconsistent lengths in fixed-size Poseidon sponge (CPU). Expected: {}, found: {}.",
170:                         len,
171:                         state.queue.len()
172:                     )
173:                 };
174:             }
175:         }
176: 
177:         for chunk in state.queue.chunks(RATE) {
178:             for (entry, value) in state.register.iter_mut().zip(chunk.iter()) {
179:                 *entry += value;
180:             }
181:             permutation_cpu(&state.pre_computed, &mut state.register);
182:         }
183: 
184:         state.queue = Vec::new();
185:         state.squeeze_position = 1 % RATE;
186:         state.register[0]
187:     }
188: }
189: 
190: impl<F: PoseidonField> TranscriptHash for PoseidonState<F> {
191:     type Input = Vec<F>;
192:     type Output = F;
193: 
194:     fn init() -> Self {
195:         PoseidonChip::init(None)
196:     }
197: 
198:     fn absorb(&mut self, input: &Self::Input) {
199:         PoseidonChip::absorb(self, input)
200:     }
201: 
202:     fn squeeze(&mut self) -> Self::Output {
203:         PoseidonChip::squeeze(self)
204:     }
205: }
206:

208: // /// Implementation of Hashable for BLS12-381 with Poseidon //

211: impl Hashable<PoseidonState<midnight_curves::Fq>> for midnight_curves::G1Projective {
212:     fn to_input(&self) -> Vec<midnight_curves::Fq> {
213:         AssignedForeignPoint::<midnight_curves::Fq, midnight_curves::G1Projective, MEP>::as_public_input(self)
214:     }
215: 
216:     fn to_bytes(&self) -> Vec<u8> {
217:         <midnight_curves::G1Affine as GroupEncoding>::to_bytes(&self.into())
218:             .as_ref()
219:             .to_vec()
220:     }
221: 
222:     fn read(buffer: &mut impl Read) -> io::Result<Self> {
223:         let mut bytes = <midnight_curves::G1Affine as GroupEncoding>::Repr::default();
224: 
225:         buffer.read_exact(bytes.as_mut())?;
226: 
227:         Option::from(midnight_curves::G1Affine::from_bytes(&bytes))
228:             .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("Invalid BLS12-381 point encoding in proof"))
229:             .map(|p: midnight_curves::G1Affine| p.into())
230:     }
231: }

236:     }

240:     }

245:

247: 
248:         <Fq as CircuitField>::from_bytes_le(&bytes)
249:             .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("Invalid BLS12-381 scalar encoding in proof"))
250:     }
251: }

311: #[cfg(test)]
312: mod tests {
313:     use rand::SeedableRng;
314:     use rand_chacha::ChaCha12Rng;
315: 
316:     use super::*;
317:     use crate::hash::poseidon::permutation_cpu;
318: 
319:     // A version of Poseidon's permutation, without round skips. Has been tested
320:     // against the previous version of Poseidon (replaced since Merge request #521).
321:     fn permutation_cpu_raw<F: PoseidonField>(state: &mut [F]) {
322:         for (x, k0) in state.iter_mut().zip(F::ROUND_CONSTANTS[0]) {
323:             *x += k0;
324:         }
325:         for round_index in 0..NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2 {
326:             full_round_cpu(round_index, state);
327:         }
328:         for round_index in (NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2..).take(NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS) {
329:             partial_round_cpu_raw(round_index, state);
330:         }
331:         for round_index in (NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2 + NB_PARTIAL_ROUNDS..).take(NB_FULL_ROUNDS / 2) {
332:             full_round_cpu(round_index, state);
333:         }
334:     }
335:     // Tests the performances of the cpu version of Poseidon. In debug mode, also
336:     // tests the consistency between the version with and without round skips.
337:     fn consistency_cpu<F: PoseidonField + ff::FromUniformBytes<64>>(nb_samples: usize) {
338:         println!(
339:             ">> Testing the consistency between the two cpu implementations of the permutation ({NB_SKIPS_CPU} round skips VS no round skips)."
340:         );
341: 
342:         let pre_computed = PreComputedRoundCPU::init();
343:         let mut rng = ChaCha12Rng::seed_from_u64(0xf007ba11);
344:         (0..nb_samples)
345:             .for_each(|_| {
346:                 let input: [F; WIDTH] =
347:                     core::array::from_fn(|_| F::random(&mut rng));
348:                 let mut res1 = input;
349:                 let mut res2 = input;
350:                 permutation_cpu_raw(&mut res1);
351:                 permutation_cpu(&pre_computed, &mut res2);
352:                 if res1 != res2 {
353:                     panic!("=> Inconsistencies between the cpu implementations of the permutations.\n\nOn input x = {:?},\n\npermutation_cpu_no_skip(x) = {:?}\n\npermutation_cpu_with_skips(x) = {:?}\n", input, res1, res2)
354:                 }
355:             });
356:         println!("=> No internal inconsistency found.")
357:     }
358: 
359:     #[test]
360:     fn cpu_test() {
361:         // Testing cpu performances. In debug mode, also tests the consistency between
362:         // the optimised and non-optimised cpu implementations of the permutation.
363:         consistency_cpu::<midnight_curves::Fq>(1);
364:     }
365: }

14: //! Module for in-circuit accumulators (and their off-circuit counterpart).
15: //!
16: //! An accumulator is a pair of points (lhs, rhs), represented with
17: //! respective MSMs that is supposed to satisfy:
18: //!
19: //!   e(lhs, \[τ\]₂) = e(rhs, \[1\]₂)
20: //!
21: //! where τ is the corresponding SRS toxic waste.
22: //!
23: //! This property is preserved by the `accumulate` function, which combines two
24: //! accumulators into one; the resulting accumulator satisfies the property iff
25: //! both inputs do. We thus call this property the accumulator "invariant".
26: //!
27: //! Note that implication <= holds unconditionally, whereas implication => holds
28: //! "computationally".
29: 
30: use std::collections::{BTreeMap, HashSet};
31: 
32: use ff::Field;
33: use group::Group;
34: use midnight_proofs::{
35:     circuit::{Layouter, Value},
36:     plonk::Error,
37:     poly::{
38:         PolynomialLabel,
39:         kzg::{
40:             msm::{DualMSM, MSMKZG},
41:             params::ParamsVerifierKZG,
42:         },
43:     },
44: };
45: use num_bigint::BigUint;
46: use num_traits::One;
47: 
48: #[cfg(not(feature = "truncated-challenges"))]
49: use crate::verifier::utils::powers;
50: #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")]
51: use crate::verifier::utils::{truncate_off_circuit, truncated_powers};
52: use crate::{
53:     instructions::{HashInstructions, PublicInputInstructions, hash::HashCPU},
54:     types::{AssignedBit, InnerValue, Instantiable},
55:     verifier::{
56:         SelfEmulation,
57:         msm::{AssignedMsm, Msm, Point},
58:         utils::AssignedBoundedScalar,
59:     },
60: };
61: 
62: /// Type for off-circuit accumulators.
63: ///
64: /// Note that the points are represented with MSMs which may have
65: /// a fixed-base scalars part. In order to evaluate the accumulator, one may
66: /// thus need to provide the corresponding fixed bases.
67: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
68: pub struct Accumulator<S: SelfEmulation> {
69:     lhs: Msm<S>,
70:     rhs: Msm<S>,
71: }
72: 
73: /// Type for in-circuit accumulators (in-circuit analog of `Accumulator`).
74: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
75: pub struct AssignedAccumulator<C: SelfEmulation> {
76:     pub(crate) lhs: AssignedMsm<C>,
77:     pub(crate) rhs: AssignedMsm<C>,
78: }
79: 
80: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Accumulator<S> {
81:     /// Converts an off-circuit [`DualMSM`] produced by the KZG verifier into an
82:     /// [`Accumulator`].
83:     ///
84:     /// For each term in the dual MSM, the label is looked up in `fixed_bases`:
85:     /// * if found, the term is recorded as `Point::Fixed` and the expected base
86:     ///   value is asserted to match (sanity check);
87:     /// * otherwise, the term is recorded as `Point::Variable` with the base
88:     ///   stored inline.
89:     pub fn from_dual_msm(
90:         dual_msm: DualMSM<S::Engine>,
91:         fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<PolynomialLabel, S::C>,
92:     ) -> Self {
93:         let (lhs, rhs) = dual_msm.split();
94:         Accumulator {
95:             lhs: Msm::from_terms(&lhs, fixed_bases),
96:             rhs: Msm::from_terms(&rhs, fixed_bases),
97:         }
98:     }
99: 
100:     /// Checks whether the accumulator, when evaluated with the provided
101:     /// fixed-bases, satisfies the pairing invariant w.r.t. the SRS verifier
102:     /// parameters.
103:     pub fn check(
104:         &self,
105:         params: &ParamsVerifierKZG<S::Engine>,
106:         fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<PolynomialLabel, S::C>,
107:     ) -> bool {
108:         let lhs = MSMKZG::<S::Engine>::from_base(&self.lhs.eval(fixed_bases));
109:         let rhs = MSMKZG::<S::Engine>::from_base(&self.rhs.eval(fixed_bases));
110:         DualMSM::new(lhs, rhs).check(params)
111:     }
112: 
113:     /// Returns a trivial accumulator that satisfies the pairing invariant.
114:     ///
115:     /// Both sides evaluate to the identity point for any `fixed_bases` map:
116:     /// * LHS: a single `Variable(identity)` with scalar `1`, label `NoLabel`.
117:     /// * RHS: one `Fixed` entry per label in `fixed_base_labels` with scalar
118:     ///   `0` (contributing nothing), plus one `Variable(identity)` with scalar
119:     ///   `1` and label `NoLabel`.
120:     pub fn trivial(fixed_base_labels: &[PolynomialLabel]) -> Self {
121:         let n = fixed_base_labels.len();
122:         let id = S::C::identity();
123: 
124:         Accumulator {
125:             lhs: Msm::new(
126:                 &[Point::Variable(id)],
127:                 &[S::F::ONE],
128:                 &[PolynomialLabel::NoLabel],
129:             ),
130:             rhs: Msm::new(
131:                 &[vec![Point::Fixed; n], vec![Point::Variable(id)]].concat(),
132:                 &[vec![S::F::ZERO; n], vec![S::F::ONE]].concat(),
133:                 &[fixed_base_labels, &[PolynomialLabel::NoLabel]].concat(),
134:             ),
135:         }
136:     }
137: 
138:     /// An accumulator a given lhs and rhs terms respectively.
139:     pub fn new(lhs: Msm<S>, rhs: Msm<S>) -> Self {
140:         Accumulator { lhs, rhs }
141:     }
142: 
143:     /// The left-hand side of this accumulator.
144:     pub fn lhs(&self) -> Msm<S> {
145:         self.lhs.clone()
146:     }
147: 
148:     /// The right-hand side of this accumulator.
149:     pub fn rhs(&self) -> Msm<S> {
150:         self.rhs.clone()
151:     }
152: 
153:     /// Given the actual fixed bases, resolves the fixed-base part of the
154:     /// internal MSMs by pairing each named scalar with its base and moving
155:     /// them to regular variable-base entries.
156:     ///
157:     /// After this call, `fixed_base_scalars` of each internal MSM becomes
158:     /// empty.
159:     ///
160:     /// # Panics
161:     ///
162:     /// If some of the keys in `fixed_base_scalars` from the internal MSMs do
163:     /// not appear in the provided `fixed_bases` map.
164:     pub fn resolve_fixed_bases(&mut self, fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<PolynomialLabel, S::C>) {
165:         self.lhs.resolve_fixed_bases(fixed_bases);
166:         self.rhs.resolve_fixed_bases(fixed_bases);
167:     }
168: 
169:     /// Evaluates the variable part of the Accumulator collapsing each
170:     /// side to a single point (and a scalar of 1), leaving the fixed-base part
171:     /// of both sides intact.
172:     ///
173:     /// This function mutates self.
174:     pub fn collapse(&mut self) {
175:         self.lhs.collapse(PolynomialLabel::NoLabel);
176:         self.rhs.collapse(PolynomialLabel::NoLabel);
177:     }
178: 
179:     /// Accumulates several accumulators together. The resulting acc will
180:     /// satisfy the invariant iff all the accumulators individually do.
181:     pub fn accumulate(accs: &[Self]) -> Self {
182:         let hash_input =
183:             accs.iter().flat_map(AssignedAccumulator::as_public_input).collect::<Vec<_>>();
184: 
185:         let r = <S::SpongeChip as HashCPU<S::F, S::F>>::hash(&hash_input);
186:         let rs = (0..accs.len()).map(|i| r.pow([i as u64]));
187:         #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")]
188:         let rs = rs.map(truncate_off_circuit).collect::<Vec<_>>();
189: 
190:         let mut acc = accs[0].clone();
191:         for (other, ri) in accs.iter().zip(rs).skip(1) {
192:             acc.lhs = acc.lhs.accumulate_with_r(&other.lhs, ri);
193:             acc.rhs = acc.rhs.accumulate_with_r(&other.rhs, ri);
194:         }
195: 
196:         acc
197:     }
198: }
199: 
200: impl<S: SelfEmulation> InnerValue for AssignedAccumulator<S> {
201:     type Element = Accumulator<S>;
202: 
203:     fn value(&self) -> Value<Accumulator<S>> {
204:         (self.lhs.value())
205:             .zip(self.rhs.value())
206:             .map(|(lhs, rhs)| Accumulator { lhs, rhs })
207:     }
208: }
209: 
210: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Instantiable<S::F> for AssignedAccumulator<S> {
211:     fn as_public_input(acc: &Accumulator<S>) -> Vec<S::F> {
212:         [
213:             AssignedMsm::as_public_input(&acc.lhs),
214:             AssignedMsm::as_public_input(&acc.rhs),
215:         ]
216:         .into_iter()
217:         .flatten()
218:         .collect()
219:     }
220: 
221:     #[cfg(any(test, feature = "testing"))]
222:     fn from_public_input(_fields: &[S::F]) -> Option<Accumulator<S>> {
223:         unimplemented!("Size of inner MSMs cannot be known from public input format.")
224:     }
225: }
226: 
227: impl<S: SelfEmulation> AssignedAccumulator<S> {
228:     /// Converts the off-circuit accumulator into two vectors of scalars. The
229:     /// first will be used as a normal instance, whereas the second will be
230:     /// plugged-in in as a committed instance.
231:     ///
232:     /// The committed instance part corresponds to the MSM (fixed and non-fixed)
233:     /// scalars of the accumulator RHS.
234:     pub fn as_public_input_with_committed_scalars(acc: &Accumulator<S>) -> (Vec<S::F>, Vec<S::F>) {
235:         let (rhs_normal_pi, rhs_committed_pi) =
236:             AssignedMsm::as_public_input_with_committed_scalars(&acc.rhs);
237: 
238:         let normal_instance = [AssignedMsm::as_public_input(&acc.lhs), rhs_normal_pi]
239:             .into_iter()
240:             .flatten()
241:             .collect();
242: 
243:         (normal_instance, rhs_committed_pi)
244:     }
245: }
246: 
247: impl<S: SelfEmulation> AssignedAccumulator<S> {
248:     /// Witnesses both sides of a KZG accumulator in the circuit.
249:     ///
250:     /// Each side is shaped by its `labels` slice (one entry per scalar/base
251:     /// pair) and its `fixed_base_labels` set (entries whose label appears
252:     /// there become `AssignedPoint::Fixed`; the rest are assigned as
253:     /// variable-base points).
254:     ///
255:     /// # Errors
256:     ///
257:     /// If the known accumulator value's label list does not match the supplied
258:     /// `lhs_labels` / `rhs_labels`.
259:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
260:     pub fn assign(
261:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
262:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
263:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
264:         lhs_labels: &[PolynomialLabel],
265:         rhs_labels: &[PolynomialLabel],
266:         lhs_fixed_base_labels: &HashSet<PolynomialLabel>,
267:         rhs_fixed_base_labels: &HashSet<PolynomialLabel>,
268:         acc_val: Value<Accumulator<S>>,
269:     ) -> Result<Self, Error> {
270:         let (acc_lhs_val, acc_rhs_val) = acc_val.map(|acc| (acc.lhs, acc.rhs)).unzip();
271:         Ok(AssignedAccumulator::new(
272:             AssignedMsm::<S>::assign(
273:                 layouter,
274:                 curve_chip,
275:                 scalar_chip,
276:                 lhs_labels,
277:                 lhs_fixed_base_labels,
278:                 acc_lhs_val,
279:             )?,
280:             AssignedMsm::<S>::assign(
281:                 layouter,
282:                 curve_chip,
283:                 scalar_chip,
284:                 rhs_labels,
285:                 rhs_fixed_base_labels,
286:                 acc_rhs_val,
287:             )?,
288:         ))
289:     }
290: 
291:     /// An `AssignedAccumulator` a given lhs and rhs terms respectively.
292:     pub fn new(lhs: AssignedMsm<S>, rhs: AssignedMsm<S>) -> Self {
293:         Self { lhs, rhs }
294:     }
295: 
296:     /// Scales the given acc by the given assigned bit.
297:     ///
298:     /// This function mutates self.
299:     pub fn scale_by_bit(
300:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
301:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
302:         cond: &AssignedBit<S::F>,
303:         acc: &mut Self,
304:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
305:         let cond_as_bounded = AssignedBoundedScalar {
306:             scalar: cond.clone().into(),
307:             bound: BigUint::one(),
308:         };
309:         acc.lhs.scale(layouter, scalar_chip, &cond_as_bounded)?;
310:         acc.rhs.scale(layouter, scalar_chip, &cond_as_bounded)
311:     }
312: 
313:     /// Evaluates the variable part of the AssignedAccumulator collapsing each
314:     /// side to a single point (and a scalar of 1), leaving the fixed-base part
315:     /// of both sides intact.
316:     ///
317:     /// Calls to this function will probably be the bottleneck of any recursive
318:     /// circuit, but it allows one to condense a carrying computation into a
319:     /// single point, enabling powerful predicates such as
320:     /// incrementally-verifiable computation (IVC).
321:     ///
322:     /// Alternatively, one may choose not to collapse an accumulator, fully
323:     /// restrict it with public inputs and evaluate it off-circuit.
324:     ///
325:     /// This function mutates self.
326:     pub fn collapse(
327:         &mut self,
328:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
329:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
330:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
331:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
332:         self.lhs.collapse(layouter, curve_chip, scalar_chip, PolynomialLabel::NoLabel)?;
333:         self.rhs.collapse(layouter, curve_chip, scalar_chip, PolynomialLabel::NoLabel)
334:     }
335: 
336:     /// Resolves all `Fixed` bases in both internal MSMs by substituting their
337:     /// assigned circuit points from `fixed_bases`.
338:     ///
339:     /// After this call, `lhs` and `rhs` contain no `Fixed` entries; every base
340:     /// has been materialized into an `AssignedPoint::Variable` circuit cell.
341:     ///
342:     /// # Panics
343:     ///
344:     /// If any `Fixed` label in either MSM is absent from `fixed_bases`.
345:     pub fn resolve_fixed_bases(
346:         &mut self,
347:         fixed_bases: &BTreeMap<PolynomialLabel, S::AssignedPoint>,
348:     ) {
349:         self.lhs.resolve_fixed_bases(fixed_bases);
350:         self.rhs.resolve_fixed_bases(fixed_bases);
351:     }
352: 
353:     /// Accumulates several accumulators together. The resulting acc will
354:     /// satisfy the invariant iff all the accumulators individually do.
355:     pub fn accumulate(
356:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
357:         acc_pi_chip: &impl PublicInputInstructions<S::F, AssignedAccumulator<S>>,
358:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
359:         sponge_chip: &S::SpongeChip,
360:         accs: &[Self],
361:     ) -> Result<Self, Error> {
362:         let hash_input = accs
363:             .iter()
364:             .map(|acc| acc_pi_chip.as_public_input(layouter, acc))
365:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?
366:             .into_iter()
367:             .flatten()
368:             .collect::<Vec<_>>();
369: 
370:         let r = sponge_chip.hash(layouter, &hash_input)?;
371:         #[cfg(feature = "truncated-challenges")]
372:         let rs = truncated_powers::<S::F>(layouter, scalar_chip, &r, accs.len())?;
373:         #[cfg(not(feature = "truncated-challenges"))]
374:         let rs = powers::<S::F>(layouter, scalar_chip, &r, accs.len())?
375:             .iter()
376:             .map(|ri| AssignedBoundedScalar::new(ri, None))
377:             .collect::<Vec<_>>();
378: 
379:         let mut acc = accs[0].clone();
380:         for (other, ri) in accs.iter().zip(rs).skip(1) {
381:             acc.lhs = acc.lhs.accumulate_with_r(layouter, scalar_chip, &other.lhs, &ri)?;
382:             acc.rhs = acc.rhs.accumulate_with_r(layouter, scalar_chip, &other.rhs, &ri)?;
383:         }
384: 
385:         Ok(acc)
386:     }
387: }

14: //! A module for in-circuit generic arguments. It is the in-circuit analog of
15: //! file proofs/src/plonk/argument.rs and its `verifier` submodule.
16: //!
17: //! A group of polynomials committed together, identified by their labels, is
18: //! read from the transcript as a single commitment and opened at the points
19: //! that [eval_points] assigns to each label. The arguments that own those
20: //! polynomials recover their evaluations from [Evaluated::evals_map], keyed by
21: //! label; the corresponding identities live in our `expressions/` directory.
22: 
23: use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
24: 
25: use midnight_proofs::{circuit::Layouter, plonk::Error, poly::PolynomialLabel};
26: 
27: use crate::{
28:     field::AssignedNative,
29:     verifier::{
30:         SelfEmulation,
31:         pcs::{InCircuitPCS, VerifierQuery},
32:         transcript_gadget::TranscriptGadget,
33:     },
34: };
35: 
36: /// The evaluation of a polynomial at a point, both assigned in-circuit.
37: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
38: pub(crate) struct Evaluation<S: SelfEmulation> {
39:     point: AssignedNative<S::F>,
40:     eval: AssignedNative<S::F>,
41: }
42: 
43: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Evaluation<S> {
44:     /// The claimed evaluation.
45:     pub(crate) fn eval(&self) -> &AssignedNative<S::F> {
46:         &self.eval
47:     }
48: }
49: 
50: /// The evaluation points at which the polynomial of the given label needs to be
51: /// evaluated.
52: ///
53: /// The opening points are argument-specific, but they are all listed here so
54: /// that a single implementation serves the whole group, with no trait to
55: /// dispatch on: `PolynomialLabel` is defined outside the arguments and already
56: /// names their specifics, so the label alone decides.
57: ///
58: /// It must agree with its off-circuit counterpart in
59: /// proofs/src/plonk/argument.rs.
60: fn eval_points<S: SelfEmulation>(
61:     label: &PolynomialLabel,
62:     x: &AssignedNative<S::F>,
63: ) -> Vec<AssignedNative<S::F>> {
64:     match label {
65:         PolynomialLabel::Trash(_) => vec![x.clone()],
66:         _ => unreachable!(),
67:     }
68: }
69: 
70: /// A group of polynomials read from the transcript as a single commitment.
71: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
72: pub(crate) struct Committed<S: SelfEmulation, PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>> {
73:     commitment: PCS::AssignedCommitment,
74:     polynomial_labels: BTreeSet<PolynomialLabel>,
75: }
76: 
77: /// Reads the commitment to the polynomials of the given labels.
78: pub(crate) fn read_committed<S: SelfEmulation, PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>>(
79:     labels: &[PolynomialLabel],
80:     layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
81:     transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
82: ) -> Result<Committed<S, PCS>, Error> {
83:     Ok(Committed {
84:         commitment: PCS::read_commitment(transcript_gadget, layouter, labels)?,
85:         polynomial_labels: BTreeSet::from_iter(labels.iter().cloned()),
86:     })
87: }
88: 
89: /// A [Committed] group whose polynomials have been opened at their evaluation
90: /// points.
91: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
92: pub(crate) struct Evaluated<S: SelfEmulation, PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>> {
93:     committed: Committed<S, PCS>,
94:     pub(crate) evals_map: BTreeMap<PolynomialLabel, Vec<Evaluation<S>>>,
95: }
96: 
97: impl<S: SelfEmulation, PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>> Committed<S, PCS> {
98:     /// Reads the evaluation of every polynomial of the group at each of its
99:     /// evaluation points.
100:     pub(crate) fn evaluate(
101:         self,
102:         x: &AssignedNative<S::F>,
103:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
104:         transcript_gadget: &mut TranscriptGadget<S>,
105:     ) -> Result<Evaluated<S, PCS>, Error> {
106:         let mut evals_map: BTreeMap<PolynomialLabel, Vec<Evaluation<S>>> = BTreeMap::new();
107: 
108:         for label in &self.polynomial_labels {
109:             let eval_points = eval_points::<S>(label, x);
110:             let mut evals = Vec::with_capacity(eval_points.len());
111:             for point in eval_points {
112:                 evals.push(Evaluation {
113:                     point,
114:                     eval: transcript_gadget.read_scalar(layouter)?,
115:                 });
116:             }
117: 
118:             if evals_map.insert(label.clone(), evals).is_some() {
119:                 return Err(Error::DuplicatedLabel);
120:             }
121:         }
122: 
123:         Ok(Evaluated {
124:             committed: self,
125:             evals_map,
126:         })
127:     }
128: }
129: 
130: // "expressions" are implemented in our `expressions/` directory.
131: 
132: impl<'a, S: SelfEmulation, PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>> Evaluated<S, PCS> {
133:     /// The queries that the multi-open argument checks for this group.
134:     pub(crate) fn queries(&'a self) -> Vec<VerifierQuery<'a, S, PCS>> {
135:         self.evals_map
136:             .iter()
137:             .flat_map(|(label, evaluations)| {
138:                 evaluations.iter().map(|evaluation| {
139:                     VerifierQuery::new(
140:                         &evaluation.point,
141:                         &self.committed.commitment,
142:                         label.clone(),
143:                         &evaluation.eval,
144:                     )
145:                 })
146:             })
147:             .collect()
148:     }
149: }
