203: impl<S: SelfEmulation> FromScratch<S::F> for TranscriptGadget<S>
204: where
205:     S::ScalarChip: FromScratch<S::F>,
206:     S::CurveChip: FromScratch<S::F>,
207:     S::SpongeChip: FromScratch<S::F>,
208: {
209:     type Config = (
210:         <S::ScalarChip as FromScratch<S::F>>::Config,
211:         <S::CurveChip as FromScratch<S::F>>::Config,
212:         <S::SpongeChip as FromScratch<S::F>>::Config,
213:     );
214: 
215:     fn new_from_scratch(config: &Self::Config) -> Self {
216:         let scalar_chip = S::ScalarChip::new_from_scratch(&config.0);
217:         let curve_chip = S::CurveChip::new_from_scratch(&config.1);
218:         let sponge_chip = S::SpongeChip::new_from_scratch(&config.2);
219:         TranscriptGadget::new(&scalar_chip, &curve_chip, &sponge_chip)
220:     }
221: 
222:     fn load_from_scratch(&self, layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>) -> Result<(), Error> {
223:         self.scalar_chip.load_from_scratch(layouter)?;
224:         self.curve_chip.load_from_scratch(layouter)?;
225:         self.sponge_chip.load_from_scratch(layouter)
226:     }
227: 
228:     fn configure_from_scratch(
229:         meta: &mut ConstraintSystem<S::F>,
230:         advice_columns: &mut Vec<Column<Advice>>,
231:         fixed_columns: &mut Vec<Column<Fixed>>,
232:         instance_columns: &[Column<Instance>; 2],
233:     ) -> Self::Config {
234:         (
235:             S::ScalarChip::configure_from_scratch(
236:                 meta,
237:                 advice_columns,
238:                 fixed_columns,
239:                 instance_columns,
240:             ),
241:             S::CurveChip::configure_from_scratch(
242:                 meta,
243:                 advice_columns,
244:                 fixed_columns,
245:                 instance_columns,
246:             ),
247:             S::SpongeChip::configure_from_scratch(
248:                 meta,
249:                 advice_columns,
250:                 fixed_columns,
251:                 instance_columns,
252:             ),
253:         )
254:     }

257: #[cfg(test)]
258: mod tests {
259:     use ff::Field;
260:     use group::Group;
261:     use midnight_proofs::{
262:         circuit::{Layouter, SimpleFloorPlanner, Value},
263:         dev::MockProver,
264:         plonk::{Circuit, ConstraintSystem, Error},
265:         transcript::{CircuitTranscript, Transcript},
266:     };
267:     use rand::rngs::OsRng;
268: 
269:     use super::*;
270:     use crate::{instructions::PublicInputInstructions, verifier::types::BlstrsEmulation};
271: 
272:     const SIZE: usize = 12;
273: 
274:     type S = BlstrsEmulation;
275: 
276:     type F = <S as SelfEmulation>::F;
277:     type C = <S as SelfEmulation>::C;
278: 
279:     #[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
280:     struct TestCircuit {
281:         points: Value<[C; SIZE]>,
282:         scalars: Value<[F; SIZE]>,
283:     }
284: 
285:     fn configure(
286:         meta: &mut ConstraintSystem<F>,
287:     ) -> <TranscriptGadget<S> as FromScratch<F>>::Config {
288:         let committed_instance_column = meta.instance_column();
289:         let instance_column = meta.instance_column();
290:         TranscriptGadget::<S>::configure_from_scratch(
291:             meta,
292:             &mut vec![],
293:             &mut vec![],
294:             &[committed_instance_column, instance_column],
295:         )
296:     }
297: 
298:     impl Circuit<F> for TestCircuit {
299:         type Config = <TranscriptGadget<S> as FromScratch<F>>::Config;
300:         type FloorPlanner = SimpleFloorPlanner;
301:         type Params = ();
302: 
303:         fn without_witnesses(&self) -> Self {
304:             TestCircuit::default()
305:         }
306: 
307:         fn configure(meta: &mut ConstraintSystem<F>) -> Self::Config {
308:             configure(meta)
309:         }
310: 
311:         fn synthesize(
312:             &self,
313:             config: Self::Config,
314:             mut layouter: impl Layouter<F>,
315:         ) -> Result<(), Error> {
316:             let mut transcript_gadget = TranscriptGadget::<S>::new_from_scratch(&config);
317:             transcript_gadget.init_with_proof(&mut layouter, Value::unknown())?;
318: 
319:             let assigned_scalars = transcript_gadget
320:                 .scalar_chip
321:                 .assign_many(&mut layouter, &self.scalars.transpose_array())?;
322: 
323:             let assigned_commitments = self
324:                 .points
325:                 .transpose_array()
326:                 .iter()
327:                 .map(|p| {
328:                     let assigned_p = S::assign_without_subgroup_check(
329:                         &mut layouter,
330:                         &transcript_gadget.curve_chip,
331:                         *p,
332:                     )?;
333:                     Ok(AssignedKZGMultiCommitment(vec![
334:                         AssignedKZGCommitment::simple(assigned_p, PolynomialLabel::NoLabel),
335:                     ]))
336:                 })
337:                 .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
338: 
339:             for i in 0..(SIZE / 2) {
340:                 transcript_gadget.common_scalar(&mut layouter, &assigned_scalars[i])?;
341:                 transcript_gadget.common_commitment(&mut layouter, &assigned_commitments[i])?;
342:             }
343: 
344:             let challenge_1 = transcript_gadget.squeeze_challenge(&mut layouter)?;
345:             transcript_gadget
346:                 .scalar_chip
347:                 .constrain_as_public_input(&mut layouter, &challenge_1)?;
348: 
349:             for i in (SIZE / 2)..SIZE {
350:                 transcript_gadget.common_scalar(&mut layouter, &assigned_scalars[i])?;
351:                 transcript_gadget.common_commitment(&mut layouter, &assigned_commitments[i])?;
352:             }
353: 
354:             let challenge_2 = transcript_gadget.squeeze_challenge(&mut layouter)?;
355:             transcript_gadget
356:                 .scalar_chip
357:                 .constrain_as_public_input(&mut layouter, &challenge_2)?;
358: 
359:             transcript_gadget.load_from_scratch(&mut layouter)
360:         }
361:     }
362: 
363:     #[test]
364:     fn test_transcript_gadget() {
365:         let scalars: [F; SIZE] = core::array::from_fn(|_| F::random(OsRng));
366:         let points: [C; SIZE] = core::array::from_fn(|_| C::random(OsRng));
367: 
368:         let circuit = TestCircuit {
369:             points: Value::known(points),
370:             scalars: Value::known(scalars),
371:         };
372: 
373:         let mut off_circuit_transcript = CircuitTranscript::<<S as SelfEmulation>::Hash>::init();
374: 
375:         for i in 0..(SIZE / 2) {
376:             off_circuit_transcript.common(&scalars[i]).unwrap();
377:             off_circuit_transcript.common::<C>(&points[i]).unwrap();
378:         }
379: 
380:         let challenge_1: F = off_circuit_transcript.squeeze_challenge();
381: 
382:         for i in (SIZE / 2)..SIZE {
383:             off_circuit_transcript.common(&scalars[i]).unwrap();
384:             off_circuit_transcript.common::<C>(&points[i]).unwrap();
385:         }
386: 
387:         let challenge_2 = off_circuit_transcript.squeeze_challenge();
388: 
389:         let public_inputs = vec![vec![], vec![challenge_1, challenge_2]];
390:         let prover = MockProver::run(&circuit, public_inputs).unwrap();
391:         prover.assert_satisfied();
392:     }

51: /// A gadget for KZG-based in-circuit proof verification.
52: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
53: #[doc(hidden)] // A bug in rustc prevents us from documenting the verifier gadget.
54: pub struct VerifierGadget<S: SelfEmulation> {
55:     curve_chip: S::CurveChip,
56:     scalar_chip: S::ScalarChip,
57:     sponge_chip: S::SpongeChip,
58: }

60: impl<S: SelfEmulation> Chip<S::F> for VerifierGadget<S> {
61:     type Config = ();
62:     type Loaded = ();
63: 
64:     fn config(&self) -> &Self::Config {
65:         &()
66:     }
67:

69:         &()
70:     }

73: impl<S: SelfEmulation> VerifierGadget<S> {
74:     /// Creates a new verifier gadget from its underlying components.
75:     pub fn new(
76:         curve_chip: &S::CurveChip,
77:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
78:         sponge_chip: &S::SpongeChip,
79:     ) -> Self {
80:         Self {
81:             curve_chip: curve_chip.clone(),
82:             scalar_chip: scalar_chip.clone(),
83:             sponge_chip: sponge_chip.clone(),
84:         }
85:     }
86: }

88: impl<S: SelfEmulation, PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>> PublicInputInstructions<S::F, AssignedVk<S, PCS>>
89:     for VerifierGadget<S>
90: {
91:     fn as_public_input(
92:         &self,
93:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
94:         assigned_vk: &AssignedVk<S, PCS>,
95:     ) -> Result<Vec<AssignedNative<S::F>>, Error> {
96:         self.scalar_chip.as_public_input(layouter, &assigned_vk.transcript_repr)
97:     }
98: 
99:     fn constrain_as_public_input(
100:         &self,
101:         _layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
102:         _assigned_vk: &AssignedVk<S, PCS>,
103:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
104:         unimplemented!(
105:             "We intend [assign_vk_as_public_input] to be the only entry point
106:              for assigned verifying keys."
107:         )
108:     }
109: 
110:     fn assign_as_public_input(
111:         &self,
112:         _layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
113:         _value: Value<VerifyingKey<S>>,
114:     ) -> Result<AssignedVk<S, PCS>, Error> {
115:         unimplemented!(
116:             "We intend [assign_vk_as_public_input] to be the only entry point
117:             for assigned verifying keys. (Note that its signature is more complex
118:             that this function's signature.)"
119:         )
120:     }
121: }

123: impl<S: SelfEmulation> PublicInputInstructions<S::F, AssignedAccumulator<S>> for VerifierGadget<S> {
124:     fn as_public_input(
125:         &self,
126:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
127:         assigned: &AssignedAccumulator<S>,
128:     ) -> Result<Vec<AssignedNative<S::F>>, Error> {
129:         Ok([
130:             assigned.lhs.in_circuit_as_public_input(layouter, &self.curve_chip)?,
131:             assigned.rhs.in_circuit_as_public_input(layouter, &self.curve_chip)?,
132:         ]
133:         .concat())
134:     }
135: 
136:     fn constrain_as_public_input(
137:         &self,
138:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
139:         assigned: &AssignedAccumulator<S>,
140:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
141:         (assigned.lhs).constrain_as_public_input(layouter, &self.curve_chip, &self.scalar_chip)?;
142:         (assigned.rhs).constrain_as_public_input(layouter, &self.curve_chip, &self.scalar_chip)
143:     }
144: 
145:     fn assign_as_public_input(
146:         &self,
147:         _layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
148:         _value: Value<Accumulator<S>>,
149:     ) -> Result<AssignedAccumulator<S>, Error> {
150:         unimplemented!(
151:             "This is intentionally unimplemented, use [constrain_as_public_input] instead"
152:         )
153:     }
154: }
155: 
156: impl<S: SelfEmulation> VerifierGadget<S> {
157:     /// Constrains the given accumulator as a public input. The (fixed and
158:     /// non-fixed) scalars of its RHS are constrained in committed form (as a
159:     /// committed instance), whereas the rest of the accumulator is constrained
160:     /// as a normal instance.
161:     ///
162:     /// See [AssignedAccumulator::as_public_input_with_committed_scalars] for
163:     /// the off-circuit analog of this function.
164:     pub fn constrain_acc_as_public_input_with_committed_scalars(
165:         &self,
166:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
167:         acc: &AssignedAccumulator<S>,
168:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
169:         (acc.lhs).constrain_as_public_input(layouter, &self.curve_chip, &self.scalar_chip)?;
170:         (acc.rhs).constrain_as_public_input_with_committed_scalars(
171:             layouter,
172:             &self.curve_chip,
173:             &self.scalar_chip,
174:         )
175:     }
176: 
177:     /// Witnesses the "collapsed" form of a KZG accumulator.
178:     ///
179:     /// The expected shape is:
180:     /// * LHS: exactly one `Variable` entry labeled `NoLabel`.
181:     /// * RHS: one `Fixed` entry per label in `fixed_base_labels` plus one
182:     ///   `Variable` entry labeled `NoLabel`.
183:     ///
184:     /// This shape matches the invariant maintained by the KZG multiopen
185:     /// accumulation after `collapse()` has been called off-circuit.
186:     ///
187:     /// # Errors
188:     ///
189:     /// If the expected shape is not satisfied.
190:     pub fn assign_collapsed_accumulator(
191:         &self,
192:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
193:         fixed_base_labels: &[PolynomialLabel],
194:         value: Value<Accumulator<S>>,
195:     ) -> Result<AssignedAccumulator<S>, Error> {
196:         let acc = AssignedAccumulator::assign(
197:             layouter,
198:             &self.curve_chip,
199:             &self.scalar_chip,
200:             &[PolynomialLabel::NoLabel],
201:             &[fixed_base_labels, &[PolynomialLabel::NoLabel]].concat(),
202:             &HashSet::new(),
203:             &fixed_base_labels.iter().cloned().collect(),
204:             value,
205:         )?;
206: 
207:         Ok(acc)
208:     }
209: 
210:     /// Accumulates several accumulators together. The resulting acc will
211:     /// satisfy the invariant iff all the accumulators individually do.
212:     pub fn accumulate(
213:         &self,
214:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
215:         accs: &[AssignedAccumulator<S>],
216:     ) -> Result<AssignedAccumulator<S>, Error> {
217:         AssignedAccumulator::<S>::accumulate(
218:             layouter,
219:             self,
220:             &self.scalar_chip,
221:             &self.sponge_chip,
222:             accs,
223:         )
224:     }
225: }
226: 
227: impl<S: SelfEmulation> VerifierGadget<S> {
228:     /// Assigns a verifying key as a public input. All the necessary information
229:     /// is required off-circuit, except for the `transcript_repr` value.
230:     pub fn assign_vk_as_public_input<PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>>(
231:         &self,
232:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
233:         domain: &EvaluationDomain<S::F>,
234:         cs: &ConstraintSystem<S::F>,
235:         transcript_repr_value: Value<S::F>,
236:     ) -> Result<AssignedVk<S, PCS>, Error> {
237:         let transcript_repr: AssignedNative<S::F> =
238:             self.scalar_chip.assign_as_public_input(layouter, transcript_repr_value)?;
239: 
240:         // We expect a finalized cs with no selectors, i.e. whose selectors have been
241:         // converted into fixed columns. In the context of IVC, the constraint system
242:         // might still contain selectors.
243:         let cs = if cs.num_selectors() > 0 {
244:             let selectors = vec![vec![false]; cs.num_selectors()];
245:             cs.clone().directly_convert_selectors_to_fixed(selectors).0
246:         } else {
247:             cs.clone()
248:         };
249: 
250:         let fixed_commitments = (0..cs.num_fixed_columns() + cs.num_selectors())
251:             .map(|i| PCS::fixed_commitment(PolynomialLabel::Fixed(i)))
252:             .collect();
253: 
254:         let perm_commitments = (0..cs.permutation().columns.len())
255:             .map(|i| PCS::fixed_commitment(PolynomialLabel::PermutationFixed(i)))
256:             .collect();
257: 
258:         let assigned_vk = AssignedVk {
259:             domain: domain.clone(),
260:             fixed_commitments,
261:             perm_commitments,
262:             cs: cs.clone(),
263:             cs_degree: cs.degree(),
264:             transcript_repr,
265:         };
266: 
267:         Ok(assigned_vk)
268:     }
269: 
270:     /// Assigns a verifying key as a constant. All the necessary information is
271:     /// available off-circuit, except for the `transcript_repr` which is
272:     /// "assigned fixed".
273:     pub fn assign_fixed_vk<PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>>(
274:         &self,
275:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
276:         domain: &EvaluationDomain<S::F>,
277:         cs: &ConstraintSystem<S::F>,
278:         transcript_repr_constant: S::F,
279:     ) -> Result<AssignedVk<S, PCS>, Error> {
280:         let transcript_repr = self.scalar_chip.assign_fixed(layouter, transcript_repr_constant)?;
281: 
282:         let fixed_commitments = (0..cs.num_fixed_columns() + cs.num_selectors())
283:             .map(|i| PCS::fixed_commitment(PolynomialLabel::Fixed(i)))
284:             .collect();
285: 
286:         let perm_commitments = (0..cs.permutation().columns.len())
287:             .map(|i| PCS::fixed_commitment(PolynomialLabel::PermutationFixed(i)))
288:             .collect();
289: 
290:         let assigned_vk = AssignedVk {
291:             domain: domain.clone(),
292:             fixed_commitments,
293:             perm_commitments,
294:             cs: cs.clone(),
295:             cs_degree: cs.degree(),
296:             transcript_repr,
297:         };
298: 
299:         Ok(assigned_vk)
300:     }
301: }
302: 
303: impl<S: SelfEmulation> VerifierGadget<S> {
304:     /// Given a plonk proof, this function parses it to extract the verifying
305:     /// trace.
306:     /// This function computes all Fiat-Shamir challenges, with the exception of
307:     /// `x`, which is computed in [Self::verify_algebraic_constraints]. It
308:     /// is the in-circuit analog of "parse_trace" from midnight-proofs at
309:     /// src/plonk/verifier.rs.
310:     ///
311:     /// The trace is considered to be valid if it satisfies the
312:     /// [algebraic
313:     /// constraints](crate::verifier::VerifierGadget::verify_algebraic_constraints),
314:     /// and the resulting accumulator satisfies the
315:     /// [invariant](crate::verifier::Accumulator::check).
316:     pub fn parse_trace<PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>>(
317:         &self,
318:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
319:         assigned_vk: &AssignedVk<S, PCS>,
320:         assigned_committed_instances: &[PCS::AssignedCommitment],
321:         assigned_instances: &[&[AssignedNative<S::F>]],
322:         proof: Value<Vec<u8>>,
323:     ) -> Result<(VerifierTrace<S, PCS>, TranscriptGadget<S>), Error> {
324:         let cs = &assigned_vk.cs;
325: 
326:         // Check that instances matches the expected number of instance columns
327:         assert_eq!(
328:             cs.num_instance_columns(),
329:             assigned_committed_instances.len() + assigned_instances.len()
330:         );
331: 
332:         let mut transcript =
333:             TranscriptGadget::new(&self.scalar_chip, &self.curve_chip, &self.sponge_chip);
334: 
335:         transcript.init_with_proof(layouter, proof)?;
336: 
337:         // Hash verification key into transcript
338:         transcript.common_scalar(layouter, &assigned_vk.transcript_repr)?;
339: 
340:         assigned_committed_instances
341:             .iter()
342:             .try_for_each(|com| PCS::common_commitment(&mut transcript, layouter, com))?;
343: 
344:         for instance in assigned_instances {
345:             let n = self.scalar_chip.assign_fixed(layouter, (instance.len() as u64).into())?;
346:             transcript.common_scalar(layouter, &n)?;
347:             instance.iter().try_for_each(|pi| transcript.common_scalar(layouter, pi))?;
348:         }
349: 
350:         // Hash the prover's advice commitments into the transcript and squeeze
351:         // challenges
352:         let advice_commitments = (0..cs.num_advice_columns())
353:             .map(|i| PCS::read_commitment(&mut transcript, layouter, &[PolynomialLabel::Advice(i)]))
354:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
355: 
356:         // Sample theta challenge for keeping lookup columns linearly independent
357:         let theta = transcript.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
358: 
359:         let multiplicities_committed = cs
360:             .lookups()
361:             .iter()
362:             .enumerate()
363:             .map(|(i, _l)| lookup::read_multiplicities(i, layouter, &mut transcript))
364:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
365: 
366:         let beta = transcript.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
367:         let gamma = transcript.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
368: 
369:         let trash_challenge = transcript.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
370: 
371:         let permutation_committed =
372:             // Hash each permutation product commitment
373:             permutation::read_product_commitments(layouter, &mut transcript, cs)?;
374: 
375:         let lookups_committed = multiplicities_committed
376:             .into_iter()
377:             .zip(cs.lookups().iter())
378:             .enumerate()
379:             .map(|(i, (m, batch))| {
380:                 let nb_flat = batch.num_chunks(assigned_vk.cs_degree);
381:                 // Hash each lookup product commitment
382:                 m.read_commitment(i, nb_flat, layouter, &mut transcript)
383:             })
384:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
385: 
386:         let phase2_labels =
387:             (0..cs.trashcans().len()).map(PolynomialLabel::Trash).collect::<Vec<_>>();
388:         let phase2_committed = argument::read_committed(&phase2_labels, layouter, &mut transcript)?;
389: 
390:         // Sample y challenge, which keeps the gates linearly independent
391:         let y = transcript.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
392: 
393:         Ok((
394:             VerifierTrace {
395:                 advice_commitments,
396:                 lookups: lookups_committed,
397:                 phase2_committed,
398:                 permutations: permutation_committed,
399:                 beta,
400:                 gamma,
401:                 theta,
402:                 trash_challenge,
403:                 y,
404:             },
405:             transcript,
406:         ))
407:     }
408: 
409:     /// Construct, in-circuit, the commitment to the linearization polynomial
410:     /// and its expected evaluation at `x`.
411:     ///
412:     /// The commitment is:
413:     ///
414:     ///  `S_0 * id_0(x) + y * S_1 * id_1(x) + ... + y^m * S_m * id_m(x)
415:     ///        - (h_0 + x^{n-1} * h_1 + ... + x^{l*(n-1)} * h_l) * (x^n-1),`
416:     ///
417:     /// where:
418:     /// * `y` is the batching challenge,
419:     /// * `x` is the evaluation challenge,
420:     /// * `id_j(x)` is a (partially or fully) evaluated identity at `x`,
421:     /// * `S_j` is either the commitment to a simple selector column or the
422:     ///   commitment to `P(X) = 1` (for fully evaluated identities),
423:     /// * `h_k` are commitments to the limbs of the quotient polynomial.
424:     ///
425:     /// # Returns
426:     ///
427:     /// `(commitment, expected_eval)` where the commitment to the linearization
428:     /// polynomial is expected to open to `expected_eval` at `x`.
429:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
430:     #[allow(clippy::type_complexity)]
431:     fn compute_linearization_commitment<'com, PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>>(
432:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
433:         scalar_chip: &S::ScalarChip,
434:         expressions: Vec<(Option<usize>, AssignedNative<S::F>)>,
435:         vk: &'com AssignedVk<S, PCS>,
436:         y: AssignedNative<S::F>,
437:         xn: AssignedNative<S::F>,
438:         splitting_factor: AssignedNative<S::F>,
439:         quotient_limb_commitments: &'com [PCS::AssignedCommitment],
440:     ) -> Result<(PCS::AssignedCommitment, AssignedNative<S::F>), Error> {
441:         let zero: AssignedNative<S::F> = scalar_chip.assign_fixed(layouter, S::F::ZERO)?;
442:         let one: AssignedNative<S::F> = scalar_chip.assign_fixed(layouter, S::F::ONE)?;
443: 
444:         let mut expected_eval = zero.clone();
445: 
446:         // Group multiples of the same fixed column to reduce the number of scalar
447:         // multiplications
448:         let mut grouped_points: BTreeMap<Option<usize>, AssignedNative<S::F>> = BTreeMap::new();
449:         let mut y_pow = one;
450:         for (col_idx, eval) in expressions.iter().rev() {
451:             let entry = grouped_points.entry(*col_idx).or_insert(zero.clone());
452:             *entry = scalar_chip.add_and_mul(
453:                 layouter,
454:                 (S::F::ZERO, &y_pow),
455:                 (S::F::ZERO, eval),
456:                 (S::F::ONE, entry),
457:                 S::F::ZERO,
458:                 S::F::ONE,
459:             )?; // entry += y_pow * eval
460: 
461:             y_pow = scalar_chip.mul(layouter, &y_pow, &y, None)?;
462:         }
463: 
464:         let mut splitting_pow =
465:             scalar_chip.linear_combination(layouter, &[(-S::F::ONE, xn)], S::F::ONE)?;
466:         let (first_com, rest_coms) = quotient_limb_commitments
467:             .split_first()
468:             .expect("at least one quotient limb commitment");
469: 
470:         let init = {
471:             let term = first_com.clone().mul(layouter, scalar_chip, &splitting_pow)?;
472:             splitting_pow = scalar_chip.mul(layouter, &splitting_pow, &splitting_factor, None)?;
473:             term
474:         };
475: 
476:         let commitment = rest_coms.iter().try_fold(init, |acc, com| {
477:             let term = com.clone().mul(layouter, scalar_chip, &splitting_pow)?;
478:             splitting_pow = scalar_chip.mul(layouter, &splitting_pow, &splitting_factor, None)?;
479:             acc.add(layouter, scalar_chip, term)
480:         })?;
481: 
482:         let commitment = grouped_points.into_iter().try_fold(
483:             commitment,
484:             |acc, (col_idx, eval)| match col_idx {
485:                 Some(idx) => {
486:                     let t = vk.fixed_commitments[idx].clone().mul(layouter, scalar_chip, &eval)?;
487:                     acc.add(layouter, scalar_chip, t)
488:                 }
489:                 None => {
490:                     expected_eval = scalar_chip.sub(layouter, &expected_eval, &eval)?;
491:                     Ok(acc)
492:                 }
493:             },
494:         )?;
495: 
496:         Ok((commitment, expected_eval))
497:     }
498: 
499:     /// Given a [VerifierTrace], this function computes the opening challenge,
500:     /// x, and proceeds to verify the algebraic constraints with the claimed
501:     /// evaluations. This function does not verify the PCS proof.
502:     ///
503:     /// The proof is considered to be valid if the resulting accumulator
504:     /// satisfies the [invariant](crate::verifier::Accumulator::check)
505:     /// with respect to the relevant `tau_in_g2`.
506:     pub fn verify_algebraic_constraints<PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>>(
507:         &self,
508:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
509:         assigned_vk: &AssignedVk<S, PCS>,
510:         trace: VerifierTrace<S, PCS>,
511:         assigned_committed_instances: &[PCS::AssignedCommitment],
512:         assigned_instances: &[&[AssignedNative<S::F>]],
513:         mut transcript: TranscriptGadget<S>,
514:     ) -> Result<AssignedAccumulator<S>, Error> {
515:         let cs = &assigned_vk.cs;
516:         let k = assigned_vk.domain.k();
517:         let nb_committed_instances = assigned_committed_instances.len();
518: 
519:         let VerifierTrace {
520:             advice_commitments,
521:             lookups,
522:             phase2_committed,
523:             permutations,
524:             beta,
525:             gamma,
526:             theta,
527:             trash_challenge,
528:             y,
529:         } = trace;
530: 
531:         // Read commitment(s) to the quotient polynomial h(X) = nu(X)/(X^n-1) from
532:         // the transcript. When the `single-h-commitment` feature is enabled the prover
533:         // commits to h(X) as a single polynomial (one commitment); otherwise it
534:         // splits h(X) into `quotient_poly_degree` limbs (one commitment each).
535:         #[cfg(not(feature = "single-h-commitment"))]
536:         let nb_quotient_coms = assigned_vk.domain.get_quotient_poly_degree();
537:         #[cfg(feature = "single-h-commitment")]
538:         let nb_quotient_coms = 1;
539:         let limb_commitments = {
540:             #[cfg(not(feature = "single-h-commitment"))]
541:             let labeled = (0..nb_quotient_coms)
542:                 .map(|i| {
543:                     PCS::read_commitment(
544:                         &mut transcript,
545:                         layouter,
546:                         &[PolynomialLabel::QuotientPiece(i)],
547:                     )
548:                 })
549:                 .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
550:             #[cfg(feature = "single-h-commitment")]
551:             let labeled = (0..nb_quotient_coms)
552:                 .map(|_| {
553:                     PCS::read_commitment(&mut transcript, layouter, &[PolynomialLabel::Quotient])
554:                 })
555:                 .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
556:             labeled
557:         };
558: 
559:         // Sample x challenge, which is used to ensure the circuit is satisfied with
560:         // high probability
561:         let x = transcript.squeeze_challenge(layouter)?;
562: 
563:         let instance_evals = {
564:             let instance_queries = cs.instance_queries();
565:             let min_rotation = instance_queries.iter().map(|(_, rot)| rot.0).min().unwrap();
566:             let max_rotation = instance_queries.iter().map(|(_, rot)| rot.0).max().unwrap();
567: 
568:             let max_instance_len =
569:                 assigned_instances.iter().map(|instance| instance.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
570: 
571:             let l_i_s = evaluate_lagrange_polynomials(
572:                 layouter,
573:                 &self.scalar_chip,
574:                 1 << k,
575:                 assigned_vk.domain.get_omega(),
576:                 (-max_rotation)..(max_instance_len as i32 + min_rotation.abs()),
577:                 &x,
578:             )?;
579: 
580:             instance_queries
581:                 .iter()
582:                 .map(|(column, rotation)| {
583:                     if column.index() < nb_committed_instances {
584:                         transcript.read_scalar(layouter)
585:                     } else {
586:                         let instances = assigned_instances[column.index() - nb_committed_instances];
587:                         let offset = (max_rotation - rotation.0) as usize;
588:                         inner_product(
589:                             layouter,
590:                             &self.scalar_chip,
591:                             instances,
592:                             &l_i_s[offset..offset + instances.len()],
593:                         )
594:                     }
595:                 })
596:                 .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?
597:         };
598: 
599:         let advice_evals = (0..cs.advice_queries().len())
600:             .map(|_| transcript.read_scalar(layouter))
601:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
602: 
603:         // The transcript doesn't contain evals of fixed cols corresponding to simple
604:         // selectors. Fill up the "missing" places with 1, to align with the
605:         // fixed queries
606:         let one: AssignedCell<<S as SelfEmulation>::F, <S as SelfEmulation>::F> =
607:             self.scalar_chip.assign_fixed(layouter, S::F::ONE)?;
608: 
609:         let num_evaluated_fix_queries = cs.num_fixed_columns() - cs.num_simple_selectors();
610:         let mut fixed_evals = (0..num_evaluated_fix_queries)
611:             .map(|_| transcript.read_scalar(layouter))
612:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
613:         for (idx, (col, _)) in assigned_vk.cs.fixed_queries().iter().enumerate() {
614:             if assigned_vk.cs.has_simple_selector_col(col.index()) {
615:                 fixed_evals.insert(idx, one.clone())
616:             }
617:         }
618: 
619:         let permutations_common =
620:             evaluate_permutation_common(layouter, &mut transcript, cs.permutation().columns.len())?;
621: 
622:         let permutations_evaluated = permutations.evaluate(layouter, &mut transcript)?;
623: 
624:         let lookups_evaluated = lookups
625:             .into_iter()
626:             .map(|lookup| lookup.evaluate(layouter, &mut transcript))
627:             .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
628: 
629:         let phase2_evaluated = phase2_committed.evaluate(&x, layouter, &mut transcript)?;
630: 
631:         // Partially evaluate batched identities
632:         // (without fixed columns corresponding to simple selectors)
633:         let nr_blinding_factors = cs.blinding_factors();
634:         let l_evals = evaluate_lagrange_polynomials(
635:             layouter,
636:             &self.scalar_chip,
637:             1 << k,
638:             assigned_vk.domain.get_omega(),
639:             (-((nr_blinding_factors + 1) as i32))..1,
640:             &x,
641:         )?;
642:         assert_eq!(l_evals.len(), 2 + nr_blinding_factors);
643:         let l_last = l_evals[0].clone();
644:         let l_blind = sum::<S::F>(
645:             layouter,
646:             &self.scalar_chip,
647:             &l_evals[1..=nr_blinding_factors],
648:         )?;
649:         let l_0 = l_evals[1 + nr_blinding_factors].clone();
650: 
651:         let mut expressions = Vec::new();
652:         // (Partially) evaluate polys from (custom) gates
653:         for gate in cs.gates().iter() {
654:             for poly in gate.polynomials().iter() {
655:                 let eval = eval_expression::<S>(
656:                     layouter,
657:                     &self.scalar_chip,
658:                     &advice_evals,
659:                     &fixed_evals,
660:                     &instance_evals,
661:                     poly,
662:                 )?;
663:                 expressions.push((
664:                     gate.queried_selectors()
665:                         .iter()
666:                         .filter(|s| s.is_simple())
667:                         .map(|s| s.index())
668:                         .next(),
669:                     eval,
670:                 ));
671:             }
672:         }
673: 
674:         // Evaluate polys from permutation argument
675:         permutation_expressions(
676:             layouter,
677:             &self.scalar_chip,
678:             cs,
679:             &permutations_evaluated,
680:             &permutations_common,
681:             &advice_evals,
682:             &fixed_evals,
683:             &instance_evals,
684:             &l_0,
685:             &l_last,
686:             &l_blind,
687:             &beta,
688:             &gamma,
689:             &x,
690:         )?
691:         .into_iter()
692:         .for_each(|perm_id| expressions.push((None, perm_id)));
693: 
694:         // Evaluate polys from lookup argument
695:         lookups_evaluated
696:             .iter()
697:             .zip(cs.lookups().iter())
698:             .map(|(p, argument)| {
699:                 let argument = argument.chunk_by_degree(assigned_vk.cs_degree);
700:                 let per_flat_inputs: Vec<&[Vec<_>]> =
701:                     argument.input_expression_chunks().iter().map(|c| c.as_slice()).collect();
702:                 lookup_expressions(
703:                     layouter,
704:                     &self.scalar_chip,
705:                     &p.evaluated,
706:                     argument.selector_expression(),
707:                     &per_flat_inputs,
708:                     argument.table_expressions(),
709:                     &advice_evals,
710:                     &fixed_evals,
711:                     &instance_evals,
712:                     &l_0,
713:                     &l_last,
714:                     &l_blind,
715:                     &theta,
716:                     &beta,
717:                 )
718:             })
719:             .collect::<Result<Vec<Vec<_>>, Error>>()?
720:             .concat()
721:             .into_iter()
722:             .for_each(|lookup_id| expressions.push((None, lookup_id)));
723: 
724:         // Evaluate polys from trashcan argument
725:         cs.trashcans()
726:             .iter()
727:             .enumerate()
728:             .map(|(index, argument)| {
729:                 trash_expressions(
730:                     layouter,
731:                     &self.scalar_chip,
732:                     index,
733:                     &phase2_evaluated.evals_map,
734:                     argument.selector(),
735:                     argument.constraint_expressions(),
736:                     &advice_evals,
737:                     &fixed_evals,
738:                     &instance_evals,
739:                     &trash_challenge,
740:                 )
741:             })
742:             .collect::<Result<Vec<Vec<_>>, Error>>()?
743:             .concat()
744:             .into_iter()
745:             .for_each(|trash_id| expressions.push((None, trash_id)));
746: 
747:         let splitting_factor =
748:             ArithInstructions::pow(&self.scalar_chip, layouter, &x, (1 << k) - 1)?;
749:         let xn = self.scalar_chip.mul(layouter, &x, &splitting_factor, None)?;
750: 
751:         let (lin_commitment, lin_eval) = Self::compute_linearization_commitment(
752:             layouter,
753:             &self.scalar_chip,
754:             expressions,
755:             assigned_vk,
756:             y,
757:             xn,
758:             splitting_factor,
759:             &limb_commitments,
760:         )?;
761: 
762:         let omega = assigned_vk.domain.get_omega();
763:         let omega_inv = omega.invert().unwrap();
764:         let omega_last = omega_inv.pow([cs.blinding_factors() as u64 + 1]);
765:         let x_next = self.scalar_chip.mul_by_constant(layouter, &x, omega)?;
766:         let x_prev = self.scalar_chip.mul_by_constant(layouter, &x, omega_inv)?;
767:         let x_last = self.scalar_chip.mul_by_constant(layouter, &x, omega_last)?;
768: 
769:         // Gets the evaluation point for a query at the given rotation.
770:         let get_point = |rotation: &Rotation| -> &AssignedNative<S::F> {
771:             match rotation.0 {
772:                 -1 => &x_prev,
773:                 0 => &x,
774:                 1 => &x_next,
775:                 _ => panic!("We do not support other rotations"),
776:             }
777:         };
778: 
779:         // Collect queries that are checked in the multi-open argument
780:         //
781:         // NB: Queries corresponding to simple, multiplicative selectors need not be
782:         // checked
783:         let queries = iter::empty()
784:             .chain(
785:                 cs.advice_queries().iter().enumerate().map(|(query_index, &(column, rot))| {
786:                     VerifierQuery::<S, PCS>::new(
787:                         get_point(&rot),
788:                         &advice_commitments[column.index()],
789:                         PolynomialLabel::Advice(column.index()),
790:                         &advice_evals[query_index],
791:                     )
792:                 }),
793:             )
794:             .chain(cs.instance_queries().iter().enumerate().filter_map(
795:                 |(query_index, &(column, rot))| {
796:                     if column.index() < nb_committed_instances {
797:                         Some(VerifierQuery::<S, PCS>::new(
798:                             get_point(&rot),
799:                             &assigned_committed_instances[column.index()],
800:                             PolynomialLabel::CommittedInstance(column.index()),
801:                             &instance_evals[query_index],
802:                         ))
803:                     } else {
804:                         None
805:                     }
806:                 },
807:             ))
808:             .chain(permutations_evaluated.queries(&x, &x_next, &x_last))
809:             .chain(lookups_evaluated.iter().flat_map(|lookup| lookup.queries(&x, &x_next)))
810:             .chain(phase2_evaluated.queries())
811:             .chain(
812:                 cs.fixed_queries()
813:                     .iter()
814:                     .enumerate()
815:                     // Filter out queries for simple, multiplicative selectors
816:                     .filter(|(_, (col, _))| !cs.has_simple_selector_col(col.index()))
817:                     .map(|(query_index, &(column, rot))| {
818:                         VerifierQuery::new(
819:                             get_point(&rot),
820:                             &assigned_vk.fixed_commitments[column.index()],
821:                             PolynomialLabel::Fixed(column.index()),
822:                             &fixed_evals[query_index],
823:                         )
824:                     }),
825:             )
826:             .chain(
827:                 permutations_common
828:                     .queries(&assigned_vk.perm_commitments.iter().collect::<Vec<_>>(), &x),
829:             )
830:             .chain(iter::once(VerifierQuery::new(
831:                 &x,
832:                 &lin_commitment,
833:                 PolynomialLabel::Linearization,
834:                 &lin_eval,
835:             )))
836:             .collect::<Vec<_>>();
837: 
838:         // We are now convinced the circuit is satisfied so long as the
839:         // polynomial commitments open to the correct values, which is true as long
840:         // as the following accumulator passes the invariant.
841:         PCS::multi_prepare(
842:             layouter,
843:             &self.curve_chip,
844:             &self.scalar_chip,
845:             &mut transcript,
846:             &queries,
847:         )
848:     }
849: 
850:     /// Prepares a plonk proof into a PCS instance that can be finalized or
851:     /// batched. It is responsibility of the verifier to check the validity of
852:     /// the instance columns. It is the in-circuit analog of "prepare" from
853:     /// midnight-proofs at src/plonk/verifier.rs.
854:     ///
855:     /// The proof is considered to be valid if the resulting accumulator
856:     /// satisfies the [invariant](crate::verifier::Accumulator::check)
857:     /// with respect to the relevant `tau_in_g2`.
858:     pub fn prepare<PCS: InCircuitPCS<S>>(
859:         &self,
860:         layouter: &mut impl Layouter<S::F>,
861:         assigned_vk: &AssignedVk<S, PCS>,
862:         assigned_committed_instances: &[PCS::AssignedCommitment],
863:         assigned_instances: &[&[AssignedNative<S::F>]],
864:         proof: Value<Vec<u8>>,
865:     ) -> Result<AssignedAccumulator<S>, Error> {
866:         let (trace, transcript) = self.parse_trace(
867:             layouter,
868:             assigned_vk,
869:             assigned_committed_instances,
870:             assigned_instances,
871:             proof,
872:         )?;
873: 
874:         self.verify_algebraic_constraints(
875:             layouter,
876:             assigned_vk,
877:             trace,
878:             assigned_committed_instances,
879:             assigned_instances,
880:             transcript,
881:         )
882:     }
883: }

1023:         fn configure(meta: &mut ConstraintSystem<F>) -> Self::Config {
1024:             const NB_ARITH_COLS: usize = 5;
1025:             const NB_ARITH_FIXED_COLS: usize = NB_ARITH_COLS + NB_EXTRA_ARITH_FIXED_COLS;
1026:

1028:             let nb_fixed_cols = NB_ARITH_FIXED_COLS;
1029:

1031:                 (0..nb_advice_cols).map(|_| meta.advice_column()).collect();

1071:                     fixed_columns[..NB_POSEIDON_FIXED_COLS].try_into().unwrap(),

1089:             let core_decomp_chip = P2RDecompositionChip::new(&config.1, &16);
1090:             let native_gadget = NativeGadget::new(core_decomp_chip.clone(), native_chip.clone());
1091:             let curve_chip =
1092:                 ForeignWeierstrassEccChip::new(&config.2, &native_gadget, &native_gadget);
1093:             let poseidon_chip = PoseidonChip::new(&config.3, &native_chip);
1094: 
1095:             let verifier_chip =
1096:                 VerifierGadget::<S>::new(&curve_chip, &native_gadget, &poseidon_chip);
1097: 
1098:             let assigned_inner_vk: AssignedVk<S, InCircuitKZG<S>> = verifier_chip
1099:                 .assign_vk_as_public_input(
1100:                     &mut layouter,
1101:                     &self.inner_vk.0,
1102:                     &self.inner_vk.1,
1103:                     self.inner_vk.2,
1104:                 )?;
1105: 
1106:             let assigned_committed_instance =
1107:                 AssignedKZGMultiCommitment(vec![AssignedKZGCommitment::assign(
1108:                     &mut layouter,
1109:                     &curve_chip,
1110:                     self.inner_committed_instance,
1111:                     PolynomialLabel::CommittedInstance(0),
1112:                 )?]);
1113: 
1114:             let assigned_inner_pi = native_gadget
1115:                 .assign_many(&mut layouter, &self.inner_instances.transpose_array())?;
1116: 
1117:             let mut inner_proof_acc = verifier_chip.prepare(
1118:                 &mut layouter,
1119:                 &assigned_inner_vk,
1120:                 &[assigned_committed_instance],
1121:                 &[&assigned_inner_pi],
1122:                 self.inner_proof.clone(),
1123:             )?;
1124: 
1125:             inner_proof_acc.collapse(&mut layouter, &curve_chip, &native_gadget)?;
1126: 
1127:             verifier_chip.constrain_as_public_input(&mut layouter, &inner_proof_acc)?;
1128: 
1129:             core_decomp_chip.load(&mut layouter)
1130:         }
1131:     }

1133:     #[test]
1134:     fn test_verify_proof() {
1135:         let mut rng = ChaCha8Rng::from_seed([0u8; 32]);
1136: 
1137:         let inner_k = 10;
1138:         #[cfg(not(feature = "single-h-commitment"))]
1139:         let inner_params = ParamsKZG::unsafe_setup(inner_k, &mut rng);
1140: 
1141:         #[cfg(feature = "single-h-commitment")]
1142:         let inner_params: ParamsKZG<_> = {
1143:             let inner_cs_degree = 5;
1144:             let extended_k = inner_k + ((inner_cs_degree - 1) as f64).log2().ceil() as u32;
1145:             let mut extended = ParamsKZG::unsafe_setup(extended_k, &mut rng);
1146:             extended.downsize_lagrange(inner_k);
1147:             extended
1148:         };
1149: 
1150:         let inner_vk = keygen_vk_with_k(&inner_params, &InnerCircuit::default(), inner_k).unwrap();
1151:         let inner_pk = keygen_pk(inner_vk.clone(), &InnerCircuit::default()).unwrap();
1152: 
1153:         let preimage = [F::random(&mut rng), F::random(&mut rng)];
1154:         let output = <PoseidonChip<F> as HashCPU<F, F>>::hash(&preimage);
1155:         let inner_public_inputs = vec![output];
1156: 
1157:         let inner_proof = {
1158:             let mut transcript = CircuitTranscript::<PoseidonState<F>>::init();
1159:             create_proof::<
1160:                 F,
1161:                 KZGCommitmentScheme<E>,
1162:                 CircuitTranscript<PoseidonState<F>>,
1163:                 InnerCircuit,
1164:             >(
1165:                 &inner_params,
1166:                 &inner_pk,
1167:                 &InnerCircuit::from_witness(preimage),
1168:                 1,
1169:                 &[&[], &inner_public_inputs],
1170:                 &mut transcript,
1171:                 &mut rng,
1172:             )
1173:             .unwrap_or_else(|_| panic!("Problem creating the inner proof"));
1174:             transcript.finalize()
1175:         };
1176: 
1177:         let inner_dual_msm = {
1178:             let mut transcript =
1179:                 CircuitTranscript::<PoseidonState<F>>::init_from_bytes(&inner_proof);
1180:             prepare::<F, KZGCommitmentScheme<E>, CircuitTranscript<PoseidonState<F>>>(
1181:                 &inner_vk,
1182:                 &[KZGMultiCommitment::commitment_to_zero(
1183:                     PolynomialLabel::CommittedInstance(0),
1184:                 )],
1185:                 &[&inner_public_inputs],
1186:                 &mut transcript,
1187:             )
1188:             .expect("Problem preparing the inner proof")
1189:         };
1190: 
1191:         let fixed_bases = crate::verifier::fixed_bases::<S>(&inner_vk);
1192: 
1193:         let mut inner_acc = Accumulator::<S>::from_dual_msm(inner_dual_msm.clone(), &fixed_bases);
1194: 
1195:         let inner_verifier_params = inner_params.verifier_params();
1196:         assert!(inner_dual_msm.check(&inner_verifier_params));
1197:         assert!(inner_acc.check(&inner_verifier_params, &fixed_bases));
1198: 
1199:         inner_acc.collapse();
1200: 
1201:         // The inner proof is ready.
1202:         // Now, let us make a proof that we know an inner proof.
1203: 
1204:         let mut public_inputs = AssignedVk::<S, InCircuitKZG<S>>::as_public_input(&inner_vk);
1205:         public_inputs.extend(AssignedAccumulator::as_public_input(&inner_acc));
1206: 
1207:         let circuit = TestCircuit {
1208:             inner_vk: (
1209:                 inner_vk.get_domain().clone(),
1210:                 inner_vk.cs().clone(),
1211:                 Value::known(inner_vk.transcript_repr()),
1212:             ),
1213:             inner_committed_instance: Value::known(C::identity()),
1214:             inner_instances: Value::known([output]),
1215:             inner_proof: Value::known(inner_proof),
1216:         };
1217: 
1218:         let prover =
1219:             MockProver::run(&circuit, vec![vec![], public_inputs]).expect("MockProver failed");
1220:         prover.assert_satisfied();
1221:     }
1222: }

14: //! # LogUp Lookup Argument with Selector
15: //!
16: //! This module implements a selector-extended variant of the
17: //! [LogUp (Logarithmic Derivative) lookup argument](https://eprint.iacr.org/2022/1530),
18: //! adapted for univariate polynomials in the PLONK arithmetization. LogUp
19: //! provides an efficient way to prove that a set of values is contained
20: //! within a predefined table.
21: //!
22: //! The original LogUp protocol operates over multilinear polynomials and uses
23: //! the sum-check protocol. Our implementation adapts this to the univariate
24: //! setting used in PLONK, replacing sum-check with a running sum accumulator
25: //! approach.
26: //!
27: //! ## The Core Idea
28: //!
29: //! Given lookup values `f₁, ..., fₖ` and a table `T = {t₁, ..., tₙ}`, the
30: //! logarithmic derivative relation Σⱼ 1/(fⱼ + β) = Σᵢ mᵢ/(tᵢ + β)
31: //! characterizes table membership as follows:
32: //!
33: //! Completeness:
34: //! If fⱼ ∈ T for every j, then there exists `{mᵢ}ᵢ` such that
35: //! Σⱼ 1/(fⱼ + β) = Σᵢ mᵢ/(tᵢ + β), for all β.
36: //! Here, `mᵢ` is the multiplicity of `tᵢ` (how many times it appears among the
37: //! `fⱼ`s).
38: //!
39: //! Soundness:
40: //! If fⱼ∉T for some j, then for every `{mᵢ}ᵢ` it holds
41: //! Σⱼ 1/(fⱼ + β) ≠ Σᵢ mᵢ/(tᵢ + β) w.o.p over a uniformly random choice of β.
42: //!
43: //! This result follows from partial fraction decomposition.
44: //!
45: //! Note: When duplicate values exist in the table, multiplicities are
46: //! normalized: if value `v` is looked up `k` times and appears `t` times in the
47: //! table, multiplicities are normalized as `k/t`.
48: //! (The table may contain duplicates because it is padded to the domain size.)
49: //!
50: //! ## Selector Extension
51: //!
52: //! Each lookup argument carries an optional **selector** `s(X)` that restricts
53: //! which rows participate in the lookup check. When `s(X) = 0` at a row, that
54: //! row's input values are ignored; when `s(X) = 1`, the row is active and its
55: //! inputs must be in the table.
56: //!
57: //! The selector modifies the balance equation to:
58: //! ```text
59: //! Σᵢ s(Xᵢ)·h(Xᵢ) = Σᵢ m(Xᵢ)/(t(Xᵢ) + β)
60: //! ```
61: //!
62: //! Critically, **the selector is only applied to the input side** (`h`).
63: //! Multiplicities `m` are always summed over all table rows, because they count
64: //! how many *selected* input rows reference each table entry, so
65: //! `Σᵢ mᵢ/(tᵢ + β)` evaluates to `Σᵢ sᵢ·hᵢ` unconditionally.
66: //! Applying the selector to `m` too would silently drop table-row contributions
67: //! and break the balance.
68: //!
69: //! ## Running Sum Formulation
70: //!
71: //! Rather than checking the sum equality directly (which would require
72: //! sum-check in the multilinear setting), we encode the constraint as a running
73: //! sum over the evaluation domain. We introduce:
74: //!
75: //! - **Helper polynomial** `h(X)`: Encodes `Σⱼ 1/(fⱼ(X) + β)` at each row
76: //! - **Multiplicities** `m(X)`: Counts how many times each table entry is used
77: //!   by selected input rows
78: //! - **Accumulator** `Z(X)`: Running sum that accumulates the log-derivative
79: //!   differences, for every i: Zᵢ = Σ_{j<i} sⱼ·hⱼ - mⱼ/(tⱼ + β)
80: //!
81: //! The accumulator satisfies:
82: //! ```text
83: //! Z(ω·X) - Z(X) = s(X)·h(X) - m(X)/(t(X) + β)
84: //! ```
85: //!
86: //! With boundary condition `Z(1) = 0`. If the lookup is valid, the accumulator
87: //! returns to zero after a full cycle, which we verify by checking `Z(ωⁿ) = 0`,
88: //! where `n` is the index of the last relevant row.
89: //!
90: //! The running sum is enforced in the constraint system via the following
91: //! identity:
92: //! ```text
93: //! (Z(ω·X) - Z(X) - s(X)·h(X))·(t(X) + β) + m(X) = 0
94: //! ```
95: //!
96: //! ## Lookup Width vs Parallel Lookups
97: //!
98: //! The LogUp argument handles two orthogonal dimensions:
99: //!
100: //! - **Lookup width**: The width of the lookup table we are looking up. For
101: //!   example, checking `(a, b, c) ∈ (t_1, t_2, t_3)` has width 3. These columns
102: //!   are compressed via θ-batching: `compressed = a + θ·b + θ²·c`, reducing a
103: //!   width-w lookup to a width-1 lookup.
104: //!
105: //! - **Parallel lookups**: The number of independent lookups per row. For
106: //!   instance, if each row performs 8 range checks against the same table,
107: //!   that's 8 parallel lookups. Each contributes a term `1/(fⱼ(X) + β)` to the
108: //!   helper polynomial (per row).
109: //!
110: //! The helper polynomial aggregates all parallel lookups at each row:
111: //! ```text
112: //! h(X) = Σⱼ 1/(fⱼ(X) + β)
113: //! ```
114: //!
115: //! The constraint that enforces correctness of `h(X)` is:
116: //! ```text
117: //! h(X) · ∏ⱼ(fⱼ(X) + β) = Σⱼ ∏_{k≠j}(fₖ(X) + β)
118: //! ```
119: //!
120: //! This has degree `1 + input_degree × num_parallel_lookups`, which limits how
121: //! many parallel lookups can be batched into a single argument before exceeding
122: //! the constraint system's degree bound.
123: //!
124: //! ## Shared multiplicity and accumulator across chunks
125: //!
126: //! When the number of parallel lookups exceeds the per-chunk limit, the input
127: //! expressions are partitioned into chunks, each with its own helper polynomial
128: //! `hᵢ(X)`. However, the selector `s(X)`, table `t(X)`, multiplicity `m(X)`,
129: //! and accumulator `Z(X)` are **shared across all chunks**: they are committed
130: //! to only once per [`BatchedArgument`]. The accumulator constraint becomes:
131: //! ```text
132: //! (Z(ωX) - Z(X) - s(X)·Σᵢhᵢ(X))·(t(X) + β) + m(X) = 0.
133: //! ```
134: 
135: use std::fmt::{self, Debug};
136: 
137: use ff::{Field, PrimeField};
138: 
139: use super::circuit::Expression;
140: use crate::plonk::Selector;
141: 
142: pub(crate) mod prover;
143: pub(crate) mod verifier;
144: 
145: /// A `BatchedArgument` collects all lookups that query the same table. For
146: /// multi-column lookups (e.g., checking `(a, b) ∈ (t_1, t_2)`), columns are
147: /// compressed using a random challenge `θ` into a single value. An optional
148: /// selector controls which rows participate in the lookup; contributions
149: /// from rows where the selector is disabled are not added to the accumulator.
150: ///
151: /// # Layout
152: ///
153: /// After construction, `input_expressions` is organized as
154: /// `[parallel_lookups][lookup_width]`:
155: /// - The outer dimension indexes each parallel lookup
156: /// - The inner dimension encodes the lookup width (subject to θ-compression)
157: ///
158: /// # Degree bound and chunking
159: ///
160: /// The helper polynomial constraint has degree
161: /// `1 + input_degree × num_parallel_lookups`. When this exceeds the constraint
162: /// system's degree bound, the parallel lookups are split across multiple helper
163: /// polynomials. Call [`Self::chunk_by_degree`] to produce a [`ChunkedArgument`]
164: /// with pre-computed degree-bounded chunks; each chunk gets its own committed
165: /// helper polynomial `hᵢ(X)`.
166: ///
167: /// The selector `s(X)`, table `t(X)`, multiplicity `m(X)`, and accumulator
168: /// `Z(X)` are **shared across all chunks**; only the helper polynomial is
169: /// chunk-specific.
170: #[derive(Clone)]
171: pub struct BatchedArgument<F: Field> {
172:     pub(crate) name: String,
173:     pub(crate) selector: Expression<F>,
174:     pub(crate) input_expressions: Vec<Vec<Expression<F>>>,
175:     pub(crate) table_expressions: Vec<Expression<F>>,
176: }
177: 
178: impl<F: Field> Debug for BatchedArgument<F> {
179:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
180:         f.debug_struct("BatchedArgument")
181:             .field("name", &self.name)
182:             .field("selector", &self.selector)
183:             .field("input_expressions", &self.input_expressions)
184:             .field("table_expressions", &self.table_expressions)
185:             .finish()
186:     }
187: }
188: 
189: /// A [`BatchedArgument`] whose input expressions have been split into
190: /// chunks (for respecting the CS degree bound), each requiring its own
191: /// committed helper polynomial.
192: ///
193: /// Produced by [`BatchedArgument::chunk_by_degree`]. The selector `s(X)`, table
194: /// `t(X)`, multiplicity `m(X)`, and accumulator `Z(X)` are shared across all
195: /// chunks.
196: #[derive(Clone)]
197: pub struct ChunkedArgument<F: Field> {
198:     pub(crate) name: String,
199:     pub(crate) selector: Expression<F>,
200:     /// Pre-split chunks: `[chunk][parallel_lookup][lookup_width]`
201:     pub(crate) input_expression_chunks: Vec<Vec<Vec<Expression<F>>>>,
202:     pub(crate) table_expressions: Vec<Expression<F>>,
203: }
204: 
205: impl<F: Field> Debug for ChunkedArgument<F> {
206:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
207:         f.debug_struct("ChunkedArgument")
208:             .field("name", &self.name)
209:             .field("selector", &self.selector)
210:             .field("input_expression_chunks", &self.input_expression_chunks)
211:             .field("table_expressions", &self.table_expressions)
212:             .finish()
213:     }
214: }
215: 
216: impl<F: Field> BatchedArgument<F> {
217:     /// Computes how many parallel lookups fit within the constraint system
218:     /// degree.
219:     ///
220:     /// The helper constraint `h(X) · ∏ⱼ(fⱼ(X) + β) = Σⱼ ∏_{k≠j}(fₖ(X) + β)` has
221:     /// degree `1 + lookup_degree × num_parallel_lookups`. This method returns
222:     /// the maximum number of parallel lookups before exceeding `cs_degree`.
223:     pub(crate) fn nb_parallel_lookups_per_chunk(&self, cs_degree: usize) -> usize {
224:         let max_degree = (cs_degree - 1).next_power_of_two() + 1;
225: 
226:         // Find the maximum degree across all input expressions
227:         let lookup_degree = self
228:             .input_expressions
229:             .iter()
230:             .flat_map(|exprs| exprs.iter().map(|expr| expr.degree()))
231:             .max()
232:             .unwrap_or(1);
233: 
234:         // The dominating factor of the lookup argument is:
235:         // h(X) * ∏_j(f_j(X) + β) = Σ_j ∏_{k≠j}(f_k(X) + β)
236:         // which has degree: 1 + lookup_degree * nb_parallel_lookups
237:         (max_degree - 1) / lookup_degree
238:     }
239: 
240:     /// Returns the degree of the helper polynomial constraint after batching.
241:     pub(crate) fn degree_batched_argument(&self, cs_degree: usize) -> usize {
242:         // Find the maximum degree across all input expressions
243:         let lookup_degree = self
244:             .input_expressions
245:             .iter()
246:             .flat_map(|exprs| exprs.iter().map(|expr| expr.degree()))
247:             .max()
248:             .unwrap_or(1);
249: 
250:         let helper_constraint_degree =
251:             self.nb_parallel_lookups_per_chunk(cs_degree) * lookup_degree + 1;
252: 
253:         // The accumulator constraint includes the term:
254:         //   l_active_row (degree 1) * selector * helper (degree 1) * table_value
255:         // with degree: 2 + selector.degree() + table_degree.
256:         // When a selector is present (degree 1), this yields degree 4 for a
257:         // fixed-column table, which can exceed the helper constraint degree of
258:         // 3.
259:         //
260:         // Additionally, the system requires cs.degree() - 1 to be a power of 2 so that
261:         // helper degrees after chunking equal cs.degree().
262:         // We therefore round the minimum required degree up to the next value where
263:         // (x - 1) is a power of 2 using: (max_raw_degree - 1).next_power_of_two() + 1.
264:         let table_degree = self.table_expressions.iter().map(|e| e.degree()).max().unwrap_or(1);
265:         let accumulator_constraint_degree = 2 + self.selector.degree() + table_degree;
266: 
267:         (std::cmp::max(helper_constraint_degree, accumulator_constraint_degree) - 1)
268:             .next_power_of_two()
269:             + 1
270:     }
271: 
272:     /// Constructs a new lookup argument.
273:     ///
274:     /// `table_map` is a sequence of `(input, table)` tuples. `selector`, if
275:     /// provided, restricts the lookup to rows where it is active; passing
276:     /// `None` activates all rows.
277:     pub fn new<S: AsRef<str>>(
278:         name: S,
279:         selector: Option<Selector>,
280:         table_map: Vec<(Vec<Expression<F>>, Expression<F>)>,
281:     ) -> Self {
282:         let (input_expressions, table_expressions): (Vec<Vec<Expression<F>>>, Vec<Expression<F>>) =
283:             table_map.into_iter().unzip();
284: 
285:         // The input expressions are a 2D array, where the first dimension represents
286:         // the width of the lookup, while the second represents the size of the
287:         // parallel lookup (how many columns are we looking up in a single
288:         // table). The β batching happens over the first dimension.
289:         // Therefore, we transpose the array so that it is easier to handle later.
290:         let lookup_width = input_expressions.len();
291:         let nb_parallel_lookups = input_expressions[0].len();
292:         let mut transposed_input_expressions =
293:             vec![vec![Expression::Constant(F::ZERO); lookup_width]; nb_parallel_lookups];
294: 
295:         input_expressions.into_iter().enumerate().for_each(|(i, width)| {
296:             assert_eq!(width.len(), nb_parallel_lookups);
297:             width
298:                 .into_iter()
299:                 .enumerate()
300:                 .for_each(|(j, parallel)| transposed_input_expressions[j][i] = parallel)
301:         });
302: 
303:         let selector = selector.map(Expression::Selector).unwrap_or(Expression::Constant(F::ONE));
304: 
305:         BatchedArgument {
306:             name: name.as_ref().to_string(),
307:             selector,
308:             input_expressions: transposed_input_expressions,
309:             table_expressions,
310:         }
311:     }
312: 
313:     /// Returns the number of degree-bounded chunks of parallel lookups.
314:     ///
315:     /// Each chunk gets its own committed helper polynomial.
316:     pub fn num_chunks(&self, cs_degree: usize) -> usize {
317:         let nb = self.nb_parallel_lookups_per_chunk(cs_degree);
318:         self.input_expressions.chunks(nb).count()
319:     }
320: 
321:     /// Splits `input_expressions` into degree-bounded chunks and returns a
322:     /// [`ChunkedArgument`] with those chunks pre-computed.
323:     ///
324:     /// Each chunk contains at most [`Self::nb_parallel_lookups_per_chunk`]
325:     /// entries and corresponds to one committed helper polynomial `hᵢ(X)`.
326:     pub fn chunk_by_degree(&self, cs_degree: usize) -> ChunkedArgument<F> {
327:         let nb = self.nb_parallel_lookups_per_chunk(cs_degree);
328:         let input_expression_chunks =
329:             self.input_expressions.chunks(nb).map(|chunk| chunk.to_vec()).collect();
330: 
331:         ChunkedArgument {
332:             name: self.name.clone(),
333:             selector: self.selector.clone(),
334:             input_expression_chunks,
335:             table_expressions: self.table_expressions.clone(),
336:         }
337:     }
338: 
339:     /// The name of this argument.
340:     pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
341:         &self.name
342:     }
343: 
344:     /// Returns the selector expression for this argument.
345:     pub fn selector_expression(&self) -> &Expression<F> {
346:         &self.selector
347:     }
348: 
349:     /// Returns the table expressions for this argument.
350:     pub fn table_expressions(&self) -> &[Expression<F>] {
351:         &self.table_expressions
352:     }
353: }
354: 
355: impl<F: Field> ChunkedArgument<F> {
356:     /// Returns the number of chunks (one helper polynomial per chunk).
357:     pub fn num_chunks(&self) -> usize {
358:         self.input_expression_chunks.len()
359:     }
360: 
361:     /// Returns the pre-split input expression chunks.
362:     ///
363:     /// Each element is one chunk: a `[parallel_lookup][lookup_width]` slice.
364:     pub fn input_expression_chunks(&self) -> &[Vec<Vec<Expression<F>>>] {
365:         &self.input_expression_chunks
366:     }
367: 
368:     /// Returns the selector expression for this argument.
369:     pub fn selector_expression(&self) -> &Expression<F> {
370:         &self.selector
371:     }
372: 
373:     /// Returns the table expressions for this argument.
374:     pub fn table_expressions(&self) -> &[Expression<F>] {
375:         &self.table_expressions
376:     }
377: }
378: 
379: #[derive(Debug)]
380: pub(crate) struct Evaluated<F: PrimeField> {
381:     multiplicities_eval: F,
382:     helper_evals: Vec<F>,
383:     accumulator_eval: F,
384:     accumulator_next_eval: F,
385: }
386: 
387: impl<F: PrimeField> Evaluated<F> {
388:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
389:     /// Computes the constraint expressions.
390:     ///
391:     /// When a lookup involves multiple columns, `theta` is used as a random
392:     /// challenge to compress them into a single value via a random linear
393:     /// combination. That is, given expressions `(e₁, ..., eₗ)`, the compressed
394:     /// value is `e₁·θˡ⁻¹ + e₂·θˡ⁻² + ... + eₗ`. Both the input values `fⱼ`
395:     /// and the table value `t` are compressed this way before being
396:     /// substituted into the LogUp identities.
397:     ///
398:     /// Checks two identities (where `fⱼ` and `t` denote the compressed values):
399:     /// **Helper constraint**: `h(x) · ∏ⱼ(fⱼ(x) + β) = Σⱼ ∏_{k≠j}(fₖ(x) + β)`
400:     /// **Accumulator constraint**:
401:     ///   `(Z(ωx) - Z(x) - selector·h(x))·(t(x) + β) + m(x) = 0` where the
402:     ///   selector gates only the input side (`h`); multiplicities are
403:     ///   always subtracted so the table-side balance is maintained.
404:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
405:     pub(in crate::plonk) fn expressions<'a>(
406:         &'a self,
407:         l_0: F,
408:         l_last: F,
409:         l_blind: F,
410:         argument: &'a ChunkedArgument<F>,
411:         theta: F,
412:         beta: F,
413:         advice_evals: &[F],
414:         fixed_evals: &[F],
415:         instance_evals: &[F],
416:     ) -> impl Iterator<Item = F> + 'a {
417:         use crate::plonk::circuit::Expression;
418: 
419:         let active_rows = F::ONE - (l_last + l_blind);
420:         let evaluate_expressions = |expressions: &[Expression<F>]| {
421:             expressions
422:                 .iter()
423:                 .map(|expression| {
424:                     expression.evaluate(
425:                         &|scalar| scalar,
426:                         &|_| panic!("virtual selectors are removed during optimization"),
427:                         &|query| fixed_evals[query.index.unwrap()],
428:                         &|query| advice_evals[query.index.unwrap()],
429:                         &|query| instance_evals[query.index.unwrap()],
430:                         &|a| -a,
431:                         &|a, b| a + b,
432:                         &|a, b| a * b,
433:                         &|a, scalar| a * scalar,
434:                     )
435:                 })
436:                 .collect::<Vec<_>>()
437:         };
438:         let compress_expressions = |expressions: &[Expression<F>]| {
439:             evaluate_expressions(expressions)
440:                 .iter()
441:                 .fold(F::ZERO, |acc, eval| acc * theta + eval)
442:         };
443: 
444:         let compressed_table = compress_expressions(&argument.table_expressions);
445:         let selector =
446:             evaluate_expressions(std::slice::from_ref(&argument.selector)).swap_remove(0);
447: 
448:         let boundary = (l_0 + l_last) * self.accumulator_eval;
449: 
450:         let mut sum_helpers = F::ZERO;
451:         let helper_constraints: Vec<F> = argument
452:             .input_expression_chunks()
453:             .iter()
454:             .zip(self.helper_evals.iter())
455:             .map(|(chunk, &helper_eval)| {
456:                 let compressed_inputs_with_beta: Vec<F> =
457:                     chunk.iter().map(|input| compress_expressions(input) + beta).collect();
458: 
459:                 // Helper constraint: h(x) · ∏ⱼ(fⱼ(x) + β) = Σⱼ ∏_{k≠j}(fₖ(x) + β)
460:                 let product: F = compressed_inputs_with_beta.iter().product();
461:                 let partial_products: Vec<F> = compressed_inputs_with_beta
462:                     .iter()
463:                     .map(|f| product * f.invert().unwrap())
464:                     .collect();
465:                 let sum: F = partial_products.iter().sum();
466: 
467:                 sum_helpers += helper_eval;
468:                 helper_eval * product - sum
469:             })
470:             .collect();
471: 
472:         // LogUp accumulator constraint with shared m and Z:
473:         // (Z(ωx) - Z(x) - s·Σᵢhᵢ)·(t(x) + β) + m(x) = 0, on active rows
474:         let accumulator_constraint = {
475:             let diff =
476:                 (self.accumulator_next_eval - self.accumulator_eval - selector * sum_helpers)
477:                     * (compressed_table + beta)
478:                     + self.multiplicities_eval;
479:             diff * active_rows
480:         };
481: 
482:         std::iter::once(boundary)
483:             .chain(helper_constraints)
484:             .chain(std::iter::once(accumulator_constraint))
485:     }
486: }

26: /// A multi-scalar multiplication in the polynomial commitment scheme.
27: /// For every i, term (bases_i, scalars_i) may be have an optional
28: /// label_i for debugging or other purposes.
29: #[derive(Clone, Default, Debug)]
30: pub struct MSMKZG<E: Engine> {
31:     pub(crate) scalars: Vec<E::Fr>,
32:     pub(crate) bases: Vec<E::G1>,
33:     pub(crate) labels: Vec<PolynomialLabel>,
34: }
35: 
36: impl<E: Engine> MSMKZG<E> {
37:     /// Create an empty MSM instance
38:     pub fn init() -> Self {
39:         MSMKZG {
40:             scalars: vec![],
41:             bases: vec![],
42:             labels: vec![],
43:         }
44:     }
45: 
46:     /// Creates an MSM instance from parallel slices of scalars, bases, and
47:     /// labels.
48:     pub fn new(scalars: &[E::Fr], bases: &[E::G1], labels: &[PolynomialLabel]) -> Self {
49:         Self {
50:             scalars: scalars.to_vec(),
51:             bases: bases.to_vec(),
52:             labels: labels.to_vec(),
53:         }
54:     }
55: 
56:     /// Create an MSM from various MSMs
57:     pub fn from_many(msms: Vec<Self>) -> Self {
58:         let len = msms.iter().map(|m| m.scalars.len()).sum();
59: 
60:         let mut scalars = Vec::with_capacity(len);
61:         let mut bases = Vec::with_capacity(len);
62:         let mut labels = Vec::with_capacity(len);
63: 
64:         for mut msm in msms {
65:             scalars.append(&mut msm.scalars);
66:             bases.append(&mut msm.bases);
67:             labels.append(&mut msm.labels);
68:         }
69: 
70:         Self {
71:             scalars,
72:             bases,
73:             labels,
74:         }
75:     }
76: 
77:     /// Create a new MSM from a given base (with scalar of 1).
78:     pub fn from_base(base: &E::G1) -> Self {
79:         MSMKZG {
80:             scalars: vec![E::Fr::ONE],
81:             bases: vec![*base],
82:             labels: vec![PolynomialLabel::NoLabel],
83:         }
84:     }
85: }

87: impl<E: Engine + Debug> MSMKZG<E>
88: where
89:     E::G1Affine: CurveAffine<ScalarExt = E::Fr, CurveExt = E::G1>,
90: {
91:     /// Evaluates the MSM to a single point and replaces all terms with that
92:     /// single point (scalar = 1), labeling the result with `label`.
93:     ///
94:     /// This mirrors `AssignedMsm::collapse` in the circuits crate.
95:     ///
96:     /// # Panics (in debug mode)
97:     ///
98:     /// If a term carries a `Fixed` or `PermutationFixed` label.
99:     //  This is because these "fixed" labels carry information that we do not want
100:     //  to lose when collapsing, since it is relevant for the `verifier_gadget`.
101:     pub fn collapse(&mut self, label: PolynomialLabel) {
102:         debug_assert!(
103:             !self.labels.iter().any(|l| matches!(
104:                 l,
105:                 PolynomialLabel::Fixed(_) | PolynomialLabel::PermutationFixed(_)
106:             )),
107:             "collapse: no label may be Fixed or PermutationFixed, found: {:?}",
108:             self.labels,
109:         );
110:         let point = self.eval();
111:         self.scalars = vec![E::Fr::ONE];
112:         self.bases = vec![point];
113:         self.labels = vec![label];
114:     }
115: }
116: 
117: impl<E: Engine + Debug> MSM<E::G1Affine> for MSMKZG<E>
118: where
119:     E::G1Affine: CurveAffine<ScalarExt = E::Fr, CurveExt = E::G1>,
120: {
121:     fn append_term(&mut self, scalar: E::Fr, point: E::G1, label: PolynomialLabel) {
122:         self.scalars.push(scalar);
123:         self.bases.push(point);
124:         self.labels.push(label);
125:     }
126: 
127:     fn add_msm(&mut self, other: &Self) {
128:         self.scalars.reserve(other.scalars().len());
129:         self.scalars.extend_from_slice(&other.scalars());
130: 
131:         self.bases.reserve(other.bases().len());
132:         self.bases.extend_from_slice(&other.bases());
133: 
134:         self.labels.reserve(other.labels().len());
135:         self.labels.extend_from_slice(&other.labels());
136:     }
137: 
138:     fn scale(&mut self, factor: E::Fr) {
139:         self.scalars.par_iter_mut().for_each(|s| {
140:             *s *= &factor;
141:         })
142:     }
143: 
144:     fn check(&self) -> bool {
145:         bool::from(self.eval().is_identity())
146:     }
147: 
148:     fn eval(&self) -> E::G1 {
149:         if self.scalars == vec![E::Fr::ONE] {
150:             self.bases[0]
151:         } else {
152:             let mut affine = vec![E::G1Affine::identity(); self.bases.len()];
153:             E::G1::batch_normalize(&self.bases, &mut affine);
154:             msm_specific::<E::G1Affine>(&self.scalars, &affine)
155:         }
156:     }
157: 
158:     fn bases(&self) -> Vec<E::G1> {
159:         self.bases.clone()
160:     }
161: 
162:     fn scalars(&self) -> Vec<E::Fr> {
163:         self.scalars.clone()
164:     }
165: 
166:     fn labels(&self) -> Vec<PolynomialLabel> {
167:         self.labels.clone()
168:     }
169: }
170: 
171: #[allow(unsafe_code)]
172: /// Wrapper over the MSM function to use the blstrs underlying function.
173: /// Bases are passed as affine points.
174: pub fn msm_specific<C: CurveAffine>(coeffs: &[C::Scalar], bases: &[C]) -> C::Curve {
175:     // We remove zeros (keep only non-zero coefficients).
176:     let (coeffs, bases): (Vec<C::Scalar>, Vec<C>) = coeffs
177:         .iter()
178:         .zip(bases)
179:         .filter(|(s, _)| !s.is_zero_vartime())
180:         .map(|(s, b)| (*s, *b))
181:         .unzip();
182: 
183:     if coeffs.is_empty() {
184:         return C::Curve::identity();
185:     }
186: 
187:     // NOTE: Empirically checked that for MSMs larger than 2**18, the blstrs
188:     // implementation regresses.
189:     // TODO: Review this threshold after optimizations.
190:     if coeffs.len() <= (2 << 18) && TypeId::of::<C>() == TypeId::of::<G1Affine>() {
191:         // Safe: we just checked the type.
192:         let coeffs = unsafe { &*(coeffs.as_slice() as *const _ as *const [Fq]) };
193:         let bases = unsafe { &*(bases.as_slice() as *const _ as *const [G1Affine]) };
194:         // TODO: 255 is fine because type is checked. Another option is propagating
195:         // nbits as an input of msm_specific.
196:         let res = G1Affine::multi_exp_affine(bases, coeffs);
197:         unsafe { std::mem::transmute_copy(&res) }
198:     } else {
199:         msm_best(&coeffs, &bases)
200:     }
201: }

203: /// Two channel MSM accumulator
204: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
205: pub struct DualMSM<E: Engine> {
206:     pub(crate) left: MSMKZG<E>,
207:     pub(crate) right: MSMKZG<E>,
208: }
209: 
210: /// A [DualMSM] split into left and right vectors of `(Scalar, Point)` tuples
211: pub type SplitDualMSM<'a, E> = (
212:     Vec<(
213:         &'a PolynomialLabel,
214:         &'a <E as Engine>::Fr,
215:         &'a <E as Engine>::G1,
216:     )>,
217:     Vec<(
218:         &'a PolynomialLabel,
219:         &'a <E as Engine>::Fr,
220:         &'a <E as Engine>::G1,
221:     )>,
222: );
223: 
224: impl<E: MultiMillerLoop + Debug> Default for DualMSM<E>
225: where
226:     E::G1Affine: CurveAffine<ScalarExt = E::Fr, CurveExt = E::G1>,
227: {
228:     fn default() -> Self {
229:         Self::init()
230:     }
231: }
232: 
233: impl<E: MultiMillerLoop> Guard<E::Fr, KZGCommitmentScheme<E>> for DualMSM<E>
234: where
235:     E::G1: Default + CurveExt<ScalarExt = E::Fr> + ProcessedSerdeObject,
236:     E::G1Affine: Default + CurveAffine<ScalarExt = E::Fr, CurveExt = E::G1>,
237: {
238:     fn verify(
239:         self,
240:         params: &<KZGCommitmentScheme<E> as PolynomialCommitmentScheme<E::Fr>>::VerifierParameters,
241:     ) -> Result<(), Error> {
242:         self.check(params).then_some(()).ok_or(Error::OpeningError)
243:     }
244: }
245: 
246: impl<E: MultiMillerLoop + Debug> DualMSM<E>
247: where
248:     E::G1Affine: CurveAffine<ScalarExt = E::Fr, CurveExt = E::G1>,
249: {
250:     /// Create an empty two channel MSM accumulator instance
251:     pub fn init() -> Self {
252:         Self {
253:             left: MSMKZG::init(),
254:             right: MSMKZG::init(),
255:         }
256:     }
257: 
258:     /// Create a new two channel MSM accumulator instance
259:     pub fn new(left: MSMKZG<E>, right: MSMKZG<E>) -> Self {
260:         Self { left, right }
261:     }
262: 
263:     /// Split the [DualMSM] into `left` and `right`
264:     pub fn split(&self) -> SplitDualMSM<'_, E> {
265:         let left = izip!(
266:             self.left.labels.iter(),
267:             self.left.scalars.iter(),
268:             self.left.bases.iter()
269:         )
270:         .collect();
271:         let right = izip!(
272:             self.right.labels.iter(),
273:             self.right.scalars.iter(),
274:             self.right.bases.iter(),
275:         )
276:         .collect();
277:         (left, right)
278:     }
279: 
280:     /// Scale all scalars in the MSM by some scaling factor
281:     pub fn scale(&mut self, e: E::Fr) {
282:         self.left.scale(e);
283:         self.right.scale(e);
284:     }
285: 
286:     /// Add another multiexp into this one
287:     pub fn add_msm(&mut self, other: Self) {
288:         self.left.add_msm(&other.left);
289:         self.right.add_msm(&other.right);
290:     }
291: 
292:     /// Performs final pairing check with given verifier params and two channel
293:     /// linear combination
294:     pub fn check(self, params: &ParamsVerifierKZG<E>) -> bool {
295:         let left = if self.left.scalars.len() == 1 && self.left.scalars[0] == E::Fr::ONE {
296:             self.left.bases[0]
297:         } else {
298:             self.left.eval()
299:         };
300: 
301:         let right = self.right.eval();
302: 
303:         let (term_1, term_2) = (
304:             (&left.into(), &params.s_g2_prepared),
305:             (&right.into(), &params.n_g2_prepared),
306:         );
307:         let terms = &[term_1, term_2];
308: 
309:         bool::from(E::multi_miller_loop(&terms[..]).final_exponentiation().is_identity())
310:     }
311: }
